1. Fruit Tartlets

Category: Pastry / Tartlets | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: N/A | Yield: 500 g / 2 x 7 cm dia. tartlets. (2 individual tarts)

Ingredients

  • 2 pcs Tart cases (fully baked)
  • 200 g Crème Pâtissière
  • A/R Fresh fruit & berries
  • 40 g Clear or apricot jam glaze

Method

  1. Pipe Crème Pâtissière into tart cases, decorate with plenty of sliced fresh fruit, glaze, and serve chilled.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: N/A (assembled with pre-baked tart cases; served chilled)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Decorated with plenty of sliced fresh fruit, glazed, and served chilled.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: For optimum quality of the fresh fruit, the tart must be assembled on the same day as service. Served chilled.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. For optimum quality of the fresh fruit, the tart must be assembled on the same day as service.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Measuring jugs, Mixing bowls, Paring knife, Peeler, Green chopping board, Piping bag, Nozzle, plain 10 mm, Disposable gloves.
    • Key Learning: Rub in method, Sheeting, Docking, Blind Baking, Boiling, Whisking.

2. Crème Pâtissière

Category: Custards & Creams | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: N/A | Yield: 215 g

Ingredients

  • 75 ml Milk
  • 75 ml Cream
  • 35 g Sugar
  • 10 g Corn starch
  • ½ tsp / 2 g Vanilla
  • 1 pc / 18 g Egg yolk
  • 5 g Butter

Method

  1. Mix the milk and cream together and bring ¾ of the mixture to a boil in a saucepan.
  2. To a mixing bowl add sugar, cornstarch, vanilla, egg yolk, and ¼ of cold milk mixture and mix until combined.
  3. Pour the hot mixture on the egg yolk- sugar mixture, whisking continuously.
  4. Pour it back in the saucepan and bring it to boil, while continuously mixing.
  5. Cook until it bubbles right in the center and pour into a clean mixing bowl.
  6. Keep whisking until it is lukewarm and then mix in butter.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Bring to a boil (100°C / 212°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Cooked until it bubbles right in the center; whisked until smooth before use.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Cover the Crème Pâtissière with a cartouche or cling film touching the surface and refrigerate until used.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Cover the Crème Pâtissière with a cartouche or cling film touching the surface and refrigerate until used.
      3. Whisk until smooth before use.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Measuring jugs, Mixing bowls, Sauce pan, Rubber spatula, Whisk, Cling film.
    • Key Learning: Boiling, Whisking.

3. Savoury Short Pastry / Pâte Brisée

Category: Pastry / Savoury | Prep: 50 mins (chilling) | Cook/Bake: 12–15 mins | Yield: 6 x 7 cm dia. Tartlet shells (3-4 tartlet shells)

Ingredients

  • 100 g Flour
  • 2 g Salt
  • 50 g Butter, cubed, cold
  • 30 g Eggs

Method

  1. Mix flour and salt; add cubes of cold butter and rub in to get a breadcrumb texture.
  2. Add egg gradually and bind to form a soft dough. Do not knead or handle the dough more than required, as the pastry will become tough after baking.
  3. Chill for half an hour.
  4. Sheet the dough to the required thickness or about 3-4 mm thickness and line the molds.
  5. Dock and chill again for 20 minutes.
  6. Blind bake at 160° C in a preheated oven for 12 - 15 minutes.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 160°C (320°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Breadcrumb texture when rubbing in butter; soft dough; blind baked until set without over-handling so pastry does not become tough.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Baked tart shells can be stored in airtight containers at ambient (room) temperature and refrigerated or frozen for longer storage.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. For commercial production, the temperatures and proving times mentioned in the recipe must be followed.
      3. For home baking, the temperatures mentioned have to be increased by 20° C, since home ovens are slightly weaker than industrial grade.
      4. The proportion of flour to butter for short crust pastry is 2:1.
      5. Savoury Short Pastry (Pâte Brisée) refers to pastry made with salt and no added sugar.
      6. Pate á Foncer refers to the inclusion of egg yolk in the pastry.
      7. Cold liquids such as milk or water can also be used to bind the pastry.
      8. Sheeting the pastry between two sheets of cling film or baking paper, make it easier to handle.
      9. Baked tart shells can be stored in airtight containers at ambient (room) temperature and refrigerated or frozen for longer storage.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Whisk, Fork, Rolling pin, Pastry docker, Aluminium foil, Ceramic baking beads/Weight for blind baking, Cling film, Tart molds, Baking trays, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Rub in method, Sheeting, Docking, Blind Baking, Baking.
↑ Back to top

4. Apple Pie

Category: Pastry / Pies & Tarts | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 12–15 mins | Yield: 400 g / 2 x 7 cm dia. tartlets. (for 2 tartlets)

Ingredients

Base

  • 2 pcs Tart cases, half baked (7 cm dia)

Apple Filling

  • 2 pcs / 165 g Green apple
  • 15 g Butter
  • 15 g Brown sugar
  • ¼ tsp / 0.75 g Cinnamon powder

To Finish

  • 150 g Sweet short pastry
  • 25 g Egg (whole)
  • 10 g Icing sugar

Method

  1. Preheat the oven to 160° C.
  2. To make the apple filling, peel, core and cut apple to small cubes (10 mm x 10 mm).
  3. Heat a sauté pan and add the apple, stir until it caramelizes slightly.
  4. Add sugar, butter, and cinnamon powder, and toss to combine.
  5. Cool the filling and divide it between the two tart cases.
  6. To make the weave to cover the top, roll out sweet pastry in between two sheets of cling film in to a thin (1-2 mm) rectangular shape.
  7. Using a parallel wheel pastry cutter, cut into 8 mm strips.
  8. Use the strips to make a woven mat slightly larger than the size of the tart.
  9. Turn it over the top, press it along the edge and cut off the excess, and brush the surface with egg wash.
  10. Bake in the preheated oven for 12-15 minutes or until golden brown.
  11. Cool on a cooling rack and served dusted with icing sugar and sauce Anglaise or ice cream.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 160°C (320°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Apples caramelized slightly; baked until golden brown.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Cool on a cooling rack and serve dusted with icing sugar and sauce Anglaise or ice cream.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. When the filling in a tart is covered with another layer, it is referred to as a ‘Pie’.
      3. If a sheet of pastry is used to cover the top, it is important to dock it or make a cut in the centre to allow the steam to escape.
      4. If a dessert is served with ice cream, it is referred to as ‘a la mode’.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Peeler, Corer, Paring knife, Green chopping board, Sauté pan, Rubber spatula, Rolling pin, Cling film, Baking tray, Fork, Parallel wheel pastry cutter, Pastry brush, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Peeling, Coring, Dicing, Caramelizing, Tossing, Sheeting, Weaving, Brushing, Baking, Dusting.
↑ Back to top

5. Chocolate Tart

Category: Pastry / Pies & Tarts | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 10–12 mins | Yield: 300 g / 2 x 7 cm dia. tartlets. (for 2 tartlets)

Ingredients

Base

  • 2 pcs Tart cases, half baked (7 cm dia)

Chocolate Custard

  • 10 g Dark chocolate 55%
  • 65 ml Cream
  • 10 g Sugar
  • 7 g Cocoa powder
  • 1 pc / 18 g Egg yolk
  • 1/8 tsp / 0.5 g Vanilla

Method

  1. Preheat the oven to 140° C.
  2. To make the filling, heat chocolate chips, cream, and sugar together. Add cocoa powder and vanilla and mix well.
  3. Add the egg yolk in, whisking continuously.
  4. Pour the mixture into the tart cases and bake in the preheated oven for 10-12 minutes or until just set.
  5. Store in the refrigerator until served.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 140°C (284°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Baked until just set.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Store in the refrigerator until served.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. When the filling in a tart is covered with another layer, it is referred to as a ‘Pie’.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Baking tray, Sauce pan, Rubber spatula, Whisk, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Melting, Whisking.

6. Quiche Lorraine

Category: Savory / Pastry | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 10–12 mins | Yield: 500 g / 2 x 7 cm dia. tartlets. (2 tartlets)

Ingredients

Base

  • 2 pcs Savoury tart cases (7 cm dia)

Savoury Custard

  • 150 ml Cream
  • 50 g Egg
  • A/R Salt
  • A pinch Nutmeg

Filling

  • 100 g Bacon
  • 40 g Cheddar cheese

Method

  1. Preheat the oven to 150° C.
  2. To make the custard mixture, heat the cream (to warm temperature) and mix it with salt and the beaten eggs.
  3. Sauté chopped bacon.
  4. Divide the bacon between the two tart cases and spread evenly.
  5. Cover the filling with custard mixture almost to the brim.
  6. Sprinkle the top with grated cheese.
  7. Bake in the preheated oven for 10-12 minutes or until set and golden brown.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 150°C (302°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Baked until set and golden brown.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Different fillings and cheeses can be used for variation.
      3. The salt in the custard mixture must be adjusted according to the saltiness and quantity of cheese used.
      4. For a richer custard, egg yolk instead of whole egg can be used to the ratio of 100 ml cream to 30 g cream [egg yolk].
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Red chopping board, Chef’s knife, Sauté pan, Rubber spatula, Cheese grater, Baking tray, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Chopping, Beating, Baking.

7. Puff Pastry

Category: Pastry / Viennoiserie | Prep: ~75 mins (resting/chilling between folds) | Cook/Bake: N/A | Yield: 355 g

Ingredients

Basic Dough

  • 200 g All purpose flour
  • 1 g Salt
  • 5 g Sugar
  • 100 g Water
  • 3 ml Vinegar
  • 10 g Butter
  • 100 g Croissant butter

Method

  1. Make elastic semi-soft dough using flour, salt, sugar, melted butter, and water. Rest for 15 minutes.
  2. Roll the croissant butter to a flat square slab.
  3. Roll the dough to form a rough square, ensuring that the sides are thinner than the centre and it is big enough to envelope the margarine slab.
  4. Place the block of margarine diagonally in the center and fold the corners of the dough over the margarine like an envelope, tucking the edges to completely enclose the butter.
  5. Roll the dough to about ¼ inches thick and approximately three times as long as wide. Fold the bottom 1/3rd and the top 1/3rd to the centre. This is a single/three fold.
  6. Rest the dough covered in the chiller for about 20 minutes.
  7. Roll the dough to about ¼ inches thick and approximately three times as long as wide. Fold the bottom 1/4th and the top 1/4th to the centre and then fold from the centre. This is a double/ book/ four fold.
  8. Rest the dough in the chiller for 20 minutes wrapped in cling film (to ensure that the dough doesn’t dry out and preventing cracks from appearing on the surface).
  9. Repeat the step 6, 7, 8 and 9 one more time, turning the dough by 90° before each folding.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: N/A (unbaked laminated dough; chill between folds)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Elastic semi-soft dough; laminated evenly without surface cracks.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Rest wrapped in cling film in the chiller for 20 minutes between folds; puff paste stores very well frozen and it helps to set the layers (tends to develop “black spot” oxidation in the fridge).
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. The dough is given 2 single and 2 double fold in this recipe.
      3. Folding methods may differ.
      4. The most often used alternates are 5 or 6 single folds or 3 doubles instead of the above.
    • Puff Paste Reference Theory (Pages 10–13 / Book Pages 156–159):
      • Layer Calculations: Single Fold: 3x3 = 9-2 = 7, 3x7 = 21-2 = 19, 3x19 = 57-2 = 55, 3x55 = 165-2 = 163, 3x163 = 489-2 = 487, 3x487 = 1461-2 = 1459. Double Fold: 4x4 = 16-3 = 13, 4x13 = 52-3 = 49, 4x49 = 196-3 = 193, 4x193 = 772-3 = 769.
      • Production Methods: French method (Prepare the dough, form the Maltese cross and envelop the fat, then give the required turns); English method (Prepare the dough, roll into a rectangle and place the butter sheet on top and envelop); Scotch method (Use all the ingredients together, the fat should be in larger pieces; achieves less rise, ideal if in a hurry or for products requiring less lift).
      • Types of Puff Paste Ratios: Full Puff (1000g Flour, 1000g Fat, ~600ml Water, 10g Salt); 3/4 Puff (1000g Flour, 750g Fat, ~600ml Water, 10g Salt); 1/2 Puff (1000g Flour, 500g Fat, ~600ml Water, 10g Salt). Fresh puff paste after its final turn is referred to as virgin paste.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Sauce pan, Rolling pin, Cling film, Dough Sheeter, Pastry Brush.
    • Key Learning: Folding, Lamination.
↑ Back to top

8. Cheese and Vegetable Turnovers

Category: Savory / Pastry | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 12–15 mins | Yield: 300 g / 6 pcs

Ingredients

Pastry & Egg Wash

  • 150 g Puff pastry
  • 25 g Egg (whole)

Cheese and vegetable filling

  • 25 g Eggplants (diced)
  • 25 g Red bell pepper
  • 25 g Green bell pepper
  • 25 g Marrow
  • 25 g Cheese (Grated)
  • 10 g Onion
  • 10 g Oil
  • A/R Salt
  • A/R Black pepper

Method

  1. Heat oil in a pan and sauté eggplant until golden brown, add the onion, bell peppers, and zucchini, and cook till soft.
  2. Mix the cheese black pepper with the cooked vegetables and season as required.
  3. Roll the puff pastry to 4 mm thickness. Cut into 15 cm circle or 10 cm x 10 cm or 6 cm x 6 cm squares.
  4. Place filling in the center, moisten the edges with egg wash, fold over to close and press gently, without disturbing the edges. Adjust the amount of filling to allow the pastry sheet to be fold over and seal.
  5. Using a paring knife make a small slit on the top for the steam to escape. Place the turnovers on a greased or lined baking tray, chill until the pastry is hard.
  6. Brush with egg wash and bake for 12-15 min at 180° C in a preheated oven.
  7. Heat oil in a pan and sauté eggplant until golden brown, add the onion, bell peppers, and zucchini, and cook till soft. (Note: Step 7 is printed as a duplicate of Step 1 in the original text).

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 180°C (356°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Vegetables cooked till soft; pastry chilled until hard before baking; baked for 12–15 minutes at 180°C.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Different fillings and cheeses can be used for variation.
      3. The ingredients of the filling can be varied as desired.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon, Mixing bowls, Green chopping board, Green chef knife, Grater, Sautee pan, Rubber spatula, Rolling pin, Paring knife, Pastry brush, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Rolling, Dicing, Grating, Sautéing, Folding, Brushing, Baking.

9. Jalousie

Category: Pastry / Viennoiserie | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 15–20 mins | Yield: 375 g / 1 pc or 5 small portions

Ingredients

  • 200 g Puff pastry sheet
  • 150 g Apple
  • 5 g Butter
  • 15 g Brown sugar
  • 2 g Cinnamon powder
  • 50 g Egg (whole)
  • A/R Icing sugar or apricot glaze

Method

  1. To make the filling, chop apple into small dices, sauté in butter until cooked, add brown sugar and cinnamon powder. Continue to cook until excess liquid has evaporated. Cool before using in pastry.
  2. On a lightly floured surface, using a floured rolling pin, roll out pastry to a rectangular sheet of 3-4 mm thickness.
  3. Cut lengthwise into two strips, one with a slightly narrower width than the other.
  4. Transfer the smaller strip to greased or lined baking tray. Arrange the filling in the centre leaving 1 cm around each edge. Brush the edges with egg wash.
  5. Cut the other strip with a lattice cutter lengthwise, making sure the pastry is cut all the way through; leave a little joint in the end. Place on top of the filling and gently stretch until the edges of the upper and lower sheets meet. Gently press to join the two sheets, without disturbing the cut edge of the pastry.
  6. Chill until the pastry is hard, brush with egg wash and bake in a preheated oven at 165° C for 15-20 minutes or until pastry is puffed and golden.
  7. Cool on the tray for 5 minutes before moving to a wire rack to cool completely.
  8. Before serving, dust with icing sugar or brush with apricot glaze.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 165°C (329°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Pastry is puffed and golden.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Cool on the tray for 5 minutes before moving to a wire rack to cool completely.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Jalousie gets its name from the pattern of the slits on the upper sheet of pastry.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon, Mixing bowls, White chopping board, White chef knife, Grater, Sautee pan, Rubber spatula, Rolling pin, Paring knife, Pastry brush, Baking tray, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Rolling, Dicing, Sautéing, Jalousie/Lattice cutting, Brushing, Baking, Dusting, Glazing, Rolling.
↑ Back to top

10. Cheese Straws

Category: Savory / Pastry | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 10–15 mins | Yield: 200 g / 10-12 pcs

Ingredients

  • 150 g Puff pastry
  • 50 g Egg
  • 50 g Parmesan cheese
  • A/R Salt
  • A/R Dried oregano or mixed herbs

Method

  1. Roll out the puff pastry into a rectangular sheet of 4 mm thickness.
  2. Brush the surface with beaten egg, and sprinkle evenly with cheese and herbs. Sprinkle salt only if the pastry has no salt.
  3. Cut into long strips of about 1 cm x 8 – 10 cm and twist. Lay the strips about an inch apart on a greased or lined baking tray.
  4. Chill until pastry is hard and bake in a preheated oven at 165° C for 10 -15 minutes or until puffed and lightly browned.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 165°C (329°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Baked until puffed and lightly browned.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Grater, Rolling pin, Brush, Knife or pastry wheel cutter, Baking paper, Baking tray, Pastry brush, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Rolling, Cutting, Brushing, Grating, Baking.

11. Palmiers

Category: Pastry / Viennoiserie | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 12 mins (6 mins per side) | Yield: 130 g / 8-10 pcs

Ingredients

  • 100 g Puff pastry
  • 30 g Sugar
  • 4 g Cinnamon

Method

  1. Combine sugar and cinnamon together. Sprinkle a working surface with 1/3rd the mixture and place puff pastry sheet on it.
  2. Roll to a 4 mm thick rectangle and sprinkle the remaining sugar evenly on top.
  3. Starting from the short side, fold both the edge inwards to the center. Then fold the 2 parts on top of each other. Cover the roll with cling film and freeze until hard.
  4. Slice the roll into 0.5 - 1 cm thickness slices and place 1 inch apart on a greased or lined baking tray.
  5. Bake in a preheated oven at 180° C for 6 minutes on one side and flip them over and bake for another 6 minutes or until golden brown.
  6. Remove from baking tray to wire racks when hot and allow to cool completely.
  7. Store in an airtight container until served.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 180°C (356°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Baked 6 minutes on one side, flipped over and baked another 6 minutes or until golden brown.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Store in an airtight container until served.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowl, Rolling pin, Cling film, Baking tray, Baking paper, Palette knife, White cutting board, Chef knife, Wire rack, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Rolling, Folding, Baking.
↑ Back to top

12. Pineapple Tart Tatin

Category: Pastry / Pies & Tarts | Prep: 10–12 mins (caramelizing) | Cook/Bake: 15–20 mins | Yield: 130 g / 8-10 pcs (20 cm pan)

Ingredients

  • 6 – 8 pcs Pineapple ring
  • 15 g Brown sugar
  • 10 g Butter
  • a pinch Cinnamon powder
  • 1 pc Puff pastry disc (3 mm thick x 23cm dia)

Method

  1. Place the sugar, butter, and cinnamon in a frying pan and stir until the sugar caramelizes to a toffee colour.
  2. Toss the pineapple slice in the sauce for 10-12 minutes until completely caramelized.
  3. Place the caramelized pineapple in a 20cm non stick pan along with the sauce.
  4. Roll the pastry out to a thickness of 3 mm. Cut out the pastry to a diameter of 23 cm and place it on the top of the pineapple. Tuck in the edges around the pineapple and chill until the pastry is hard.
  5. Bake in a preheated oven at 160° C for 15-20 minutes or until done.
  6. Turn over onto the serving plate and serve with crème fraiche or ice cream.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 160°C (320°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Sugar caramelized to a toffee colour; pineapple completely caramelized; pastry chilled until hard and baked for 15–20 minutes or until done.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Turn over onto the serving plate and serve with crème fraiche or ice cream.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Tarte Tatin is a tart that is baked upside down and gets its name from the hotel in Lomette-Beuvron [Lamotte-Beuvron], where it was first made with caramelized apples.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Mixing bowls, Chef knife, White chopping board, Corer, Sautee pan, Rubber spatula, Rolling pin, Baking tray, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Melting, Caramelizing, Tossing, Rolling, Baking.

13. Pear Strudel

Category: Pastry | Prep: 30 mins (resting dough) | Cook/Bake: 30 mins | Yield: 300 g, (1-2 portions)

Ingredients

Strudel Dough

  • 145 g Bread Flour
  • 1/8 tsp / 1 g Salt
  • 80 g Water, lukewarm
  • 15 g Vegetable Oil
  • 1/2 tsp / 2.5 g Vinegar

Pear Filling

  • 4 pcs Pear (peeled and sliced)
  • 20 g Raisins, Black
  • A/R Cinnamon powder
  • 80 g Demerara sugar
  • A/R Cookie crumbs
  • 25 g Walnuts chopped

Brushing

  • 20 g Butter (melted)

Dusting

  • 5 g Icing sugar

Method

  1. Make a smooth dough, by combining all the ingredients and rest the dough at room temperature for 30 minutes.
  2. Spread a cloth on the work surface and start stretching the dough till paper thin.
  3. Brush it with melted butter and [reheat the oven to 160° C.
  4. Combine the raisins, Thinly sliced pear, cinnamon powder, demerara sugar, cookie crumbs, walnuts and butter in a large bowl.
  5. Place the pear mixture on the nearest third of the dough, being sure to leave a 2 inch border.
  6. Gently lift the bottom edge of dough to cover the filling and fold the side edges over.
  7. Continue to roll and stack away from you until the filling is completely sealed in and the seam is on the bottom.
  8. Transfer the rolled up strudel on to the prepared baking sheet and brush the top with butter and sprinkle with granulated sugar.
  9. Bake for 30 minutes in a 160° C in a preheated oven, until golden brown.
  10. Sprinkle with cinnamon and confectioner’s sugar.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 160°C (320°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Dough stretched till paper thin; baked until golden brown with a crisp outer crust.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Strudel is an Austrian pastry dish, commonly made in other central European countries as well.
      3. Apart from apples, other fillings such as quark (soft cheese), sour cherry, nuts, apricots, plums, poppy seeds, raisins and savoury vegetable fillings are also used for variety.
      4. It is important to have at least 8 to 10 layers of pastry around the filling to get a crisp outer crust that is why the filling is placed at one end of the sheet and rolled rather than in the middle.
    • Equipment: Measuring spoon set, Measuring scale, Mixing bowls, Peeler, Corer, White chopping board, Chef knife, Baking paper, Baking tray, Pastry brush, Strainer, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Brushing, Shaping/Rolling, Baking, Dusting.

14. Cream Anglaise

Category: Custards & Creams | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: Cook to 86°C | Yield: 150 g

Ingredients

  • 100 g Cream
  • 25 g Caster sugar
  • 20 g Egg yolk
  • 0.5 g Vanilla

Method

  1. Place cream in a saucepan and bring to a boil on medium heat.
  2. Whisk egg yolk, vanilla, and sugar with a hand whisk in a mixing bowl and pour the hot cream in, whisking continuously.
  3. Pour the mixture back into the pan and cook to 86° C, whisking continuously to prevent it from catching at the base.
  4. Take of the heat and continue whisking for a few minutes. Strain into a clean bowl or serving container to prevent the mixture from over cooking in the saucepan.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Cook to 86°C (187°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Light, pouring custard cooked to 86°C and strained.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Strain into a clean bowl or serving container.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Crème Anglaise is a light, pouring custard popularly used as a dessert sauce with a number of traditional desserts such as pies, cobblers, cakes, fresh or baked or stewed fruit, among others.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Mixing bowls, Sauce pan, Rubber Spatula, Whisk, Strainer.
    • Key Learning: Boiling, Whisking, Straining.

15. Filo Cups with Caramel Nut Filling

Category: Pastry / Petit Fours | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 8–10 mins | Yield: 24 pcs

Ingredients

Filo Cups

  • 2 pcs Filo pastry sheets
  • 30 ml Butter (melted)

Caramel Nut Filling

  • 150 g Granulated sugar
  • 35 g Honey
  • 35 g Glucose syrup
  • 50 g Water
  • 100 g Cream (hot)
  • 35 g Butter
  • 65 g Pistachio
  • 65 g Almonds
  • 65 g Pecan nuts
  • 65 g Hazelnuts
  • 65 g Pine nuts

Method

  1. Pre heat the oven to 160° C.
  2. Place one sheet of pastry flat on a surface, brush the sheet with melted butter and place the second sheet on top and brush with melted butter.
  3. Cut the sheet into 6 cm square shaped pieces and place them in to the mini muffin tins. Gently press down to take the shape of the mould.
  4. Bake them for about 8-10 minutes or until golden brown.
  5. For the filling, spread the nuts separately in a single layer on baking trays and toast in the oven until done. Each kind of nut will take a different amount of time.
  6. In a saucepan cook together water, sugar, glucose, and honey until caramelized.
  7. Take off the heat and deglaze with butter, stirring continuously.
  8. Put it back on low heat and pour the hot cream gradually from the side of the sauce pan while stirring continuously.
  9. When it is well combined to form a sauce, take off the heat and add the toasted nuts and mix together.
  10. Using a spoon, fill up the cups with the filling and serve.
  11. Baked cases can be stored in an airtight container and left over filling in the refrigerator until required.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 160°C (320°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Filo cups baked until golden brown; nuts toasted until done; sugar mixture cooked until caramelized and combined with butter and cream into a sauce.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Baked cases can be stored in an airtight container and leftover filling in the refrigerator until required.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Filo cups can be filled with a variety of fillings sweet or savoury and used as petit fours or canapés.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Mixing bowl, Sauce pan, Rubber spatula, Sautee pan, Pastry brush, Mini muffin tins, Spoon, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Melting, Brushing, Shaping, Baking, Toasting, Caramelizing, Deglazing, Filling.

16. Spanakopita / Filo Triangles

Category: Savory / Pastry | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 6–10 mins | Yield: 24 pcs

Ingredients

  • 3-4 sheets Filo pastry
  • 50 g Feta cheese
  • 300 g Spinach
  • 40 g Onion (finely chopped)
  • 3 g Garlic (finely chopped)
  • 15 ml Oil
  • A/R Salt
  • A/R Black pepper

Method

  1. Preheat the oven to 160° C.
  2. Blanch the spinach, squeeze the excess water and chop it fine.
  3. Gently sauté the onion and garlic in the olive oil for about 1 minute, or until transparent.
  4. Mix in the finely shredded spinach. Cook till water evaporates.
  5. Cool down the mixture completely and add the feta cheese, and then season to taste. Feta cheese is salty, so salt may not be required.
  6. Lay a sheet of filo pastry out on a work surface, brush with oil and place another sheet on top of it, brush with oil. Cut the sheet into long narrow strips with approx 10 cm width.
  7. Place a small amount of the mixture in one corner. Roll the pastry around the mixture and keep folding and rolling, until a perfectly sealed triangle is obtained.
  8. Repeat with the remaining mixture and sheets.
  9. Place on the baking sheet. Brush each parcel with olive oil again, and bake for 6-10 minutes until crisp and golden.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 160°C (320°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Onion and garlic sautéed until transparent; spinach cooked till water evaporates; triangles baked until crisp and golden.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Spanakopita is a traditional Greek pie make with a crust of filo pastry, and filled with spinach and feta cheese. The shape may vary from region to region.
      3. Other greens such as sorrel and chard and other salty fresh white cheeses are also used in some regions.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring jug, Mixing bowl, Green chef knife, Green chopping board, Baking tray, Oven, Oven mitt/gloves, Sauce pan, Rubber spatula, Pastry brush.
    • Key Learning: Chopping, Grating, Mincing, Blanching, Shredding, Sautéing, Brushing, Rolling/sealing, Baking.

17. Choux Pastry / Pâte à Choux

Category: Pastry | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 30 mins total (15 mins + 10 mins + 5 mins) | Yield: 20-25 small shells

Ingredients

  • 100 g Water
  • 40 g Butter
  • 5 g Sugar
  • 2 g Salt
  • 60 g All purpose flour
  • 80-100 g Eggs

Method

  1. In a saucepan bring (chopped) butter, water, sugar, and salt to a boil.
  2. Take off the heat and add flour & stir it until smooth. Place back to the heat and cook it mixing vigorously (so that there are no lumps) until the mixture forms dough and leaves the sides of the pan (about a minute).
  3. Transfer the mixture into a mixing bowl and mix it with a spatula for about a minute, until cools down a bit. Add the beaten eggs little by little, until get the right consistency (might not need all the eggs).
  4. Pipe the mixture on to non stick trays with a piping bag fitted with (10mm) plain or star nozzle.
  5. Bake at 170° C in a preheated oven for 15 minutes with 30 second water injection (alternatively just sprinkle water on the top of the tray before putting in the oven), fan off.
  6. Continue the cooking on the same temperature (170 C) for next 10 minute and the last 5 minute with opened vent (alternatively, open the door slightly) in order to make the crust crispy and get rid of the majority of the water.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 170°C (338°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Panade leaves the sides of the pan without lumps; baked until very crisp without becoming too brown.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Top of the raw product can be made smooth using a fork dipped in cold water.
      3. The choux pastry must be baked very crisp or it will become soggy and soft when filled. If the pastry is not crisp, return to the oven and continue to bake. The initial heat must be high to puff the product, then the heat must be reduced to help the product to crisp, without becoming too brown.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring jug, Mixing bowl, Baking tray, Oven, Oven mitt/gloves, Sauce pan, Rubber spatula, Pastry brush.
    • Key Learning: Baking, Leavening.
↑ Back to top

18. Chocolate Éclairs

Category: Pastry | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: See Choux Pastry (30 mins) | Yield: 20-25 small shells

Ingredients

Base

  • 1 recipe Choux Paste

Cream Diplomat

  • 150 g Cream patisserie
  • 75 g Cream Chantilly

Finishing

  • 100 g Dark Chocolate Ganache
  • A/R Caramel

Method

  1. Pipe choux pastry into long shapes (about 2.5-inch lengths) on to greased baking sheet or non stick tray.
  2. Bake them as described above.
  3. Cool them on a wire rack. With a paring knife tip, drill holes on either end of each shells and pipe the crème diplomat inside the center until full.
  4. Dip the upper surface in melted chocolate, dark chocolate ganache or chocolate fondant and allow them to set in the fridge.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 170°C (338°F) (baked as described in Choux Pastry)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Baked very crisp so it does not become soggy and soft when filled; glazed upper surface set in the fridge.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Allow to set in the fridge.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Top of the raw product can be made smooth using a fork dipped in cold water.
      3. The choux pastry must be baked very crisp or it will become soggy and soft when filled. If the pastry is not crisp, return to the oven and continue to bake. The initial heat must be high to puff the product, then the heat must be reduced to help the product to crisp, without becoming too brown.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring jug, Mixing bowl, Baking tray, Oven, Oven mitt/gloves, Sauce pan, Sauté pan, Rubber spatula, Pastry brush.
    • Key Learning: Baking, Leavening, Filling, Dipping.

19. Crème Pâtissière (Choux Section — Page 33 / Book Page 184)

Category: Custards & Creams | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: N/A | Yield: 215 g

Ingredients

  • 75 ml Milk
  • 75 ml Cream
  • 35 g Sugar
  • 10 g Corn starch
  • ½ tsp / 2 g Vanilla
  • 18 g Egg yolk
  • 5 g Butter

Method

  1. Mix the milk and cream together and bring ¾ of the mixture to a boil in a saucepan.
  2. To a mixing bowl add sugar, cornstarch, vanilla, egg yolk, and 1/4 of cold milk mixture and mix until combined.
  3. Pour the hot mixture on the egg yolk- sugar mixture, whisking continuously.
  4. Pour it back in the saucepan and bring it to boil, while continuously mixing.
  5. Cook until it bubbles right in the center and pour into a clean mixing bowl.
  6. Keep whisking until it is lukewarm and then mix in butter.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Bring to a boil (100°C / 212°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Cooked until it bubbles right in the center; whisked until smooth before use.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Cover the Crème Pâtissière with a cartouche or cling film touching the surface and refrigerate until used.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Cover the Crème Pâtissière with a cartouche or cling film touching the surface and refrigerate until used.
      3. Whisk until smooth before use.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Measuring jugs, Mixing bowls, Sauce pan, Rubber spatula, Whisk, Cling film.
    • Key Learning: Boiling, Whisking.

20. Cream Chantilly & Cream Diplomat

Category: Custards & Creams | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: N/A | Yield: 215 g

Ingredients

Cream Chantilly

  • 10 g Icing sugar
  • ¼ tsp Vanilla paste

Cream Diplomat

  • 2:1 ratio Mixture of cream patisserie and cream Chantilly

Method

Cream Chantilly

  1. Mix cream vanilla and sifted icing sugar together and whip it to the firm peak stage.
  2. Keep refrigerated till required.

Cream Diplomat

  1. Mixture of cream patisserie and cream Chantilly (2:1).

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: N/A (chilled preparation)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Whipped to the firm peak stage.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Keep refrigerated till required.

22. Caramel Sugar

Ingredients

  • 50 g Water
  • 250 g Caster sugar
  • 75 g Glucose syrup

Method

  1. Place all ingredients in a sauce pan and allow for the caster sugar to melt and turn a light amber colour.
  2. Once the desired colour is reached, place the saucepan in a bowl of ice to cool it down.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Caramelization stage (light amber colour)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Melted and cooked to a light amber colour (one stage before the desired final colour).

  • Additional Notes & Equipment:

      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Caramel will become a darker colour when it cools down, so cook the caramel one stage before the desired colour.

23. French Meringue

Category: Meringues & Pavlovas | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 1½ hours | Yield: 170 g

Ingredients

  • 70 g Egg white
  • 100 g Caster Sugar
  • A pinch Salt
  • 1/8 tsp / 1 g Lemon juice / Cream of tartar

Method

  1. In a clean bowl, combine salt & lemon juice to egg whites & whisk till firm peak.
  2. Drizzle sugar slowly & continue whisking.
  3. Pipe the meringue shapes & bake at 100° C in a preheated oven venting valve open for 1½ hours or until dry.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 100°C (212°F) with venting valve open
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Whisked till firm peak; baked for 1½ hours or until dry.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Temperatures may vary from oven to oven.
      3. Make sure the bowl and the whisk attachments are clean and devoid of any debris or fat, because this can and will affect the texture of the meringue.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Mixing bowls, Hand mixer, Piping bags, Piping nozzle, Silicone mat, Baking tray, Baking paper, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Making a French meringue, Piping, Baking, Whisking.

24. Swiss Meringue

Category: Meringues & Pavlovas | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: Heat over bain-marie to 60°C | Yield: 180 g

Ingredients

  • 60 g Egg white
  • 120 g Caster Sugar
  • ½ tsp / 2 g Lemon juice

Method

  1. In a clean bowl, whisk egg whites and sugar over a bain-marie until the mixture reaches 60° C.
  2. Remove from heat and continue whisking until cold.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 60°C (140°F) over a bain-marie
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Heated to 60°C over a bain-marie, then whisked until cold.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Make sure the bowl and the whisk attachments are clean and devoid of any debris or fat.
      3. French and Swiss meringue is often made with a different method. Half caster and icing sugar is used. Whip the egg whites with the caster sugar and fold in the icing sugar in the end. If the recipe contains corn starch (e.g. Pavlova shell).
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Mixing bowls, Hand mixer, Piping bags, Piping nozzle, Silicone mat, Baking tray, Baking paper, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Making a Swiss meringue, Piping, Baking, Whisking.

25. Italian Meringue

Category: Meringues & Pavlovas | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: Sugar syrup cooked to 118°C | Yield: 225 g

Ingredients

  • 70 g Egg white
  • 125 g Caster Sugar
  • 30 g Water

Method

  1. In a clean bowl, Whisk egg whites to firm peak stage.
  2. Cook sugar and water to soft ball stage or 118° C.
  3. Pour in a thin stream over the whisked egg, continuously whisking.
  4. Continue whisking until cold.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 118°C (244°F) / soft ball stage for sugar syrup
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Egg whites whisked to firm peak stage; syrup at soft ball stage (118°C); whisked until cold.

      2. Make sure the bowl and the whisk attachments are clean and devoid of any debris or fat, because this can and will affect the texture of the meringue.

29. Merveilleux

Category: Meringues & Pavlovas / Entremet | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: Bake at 100°C | Yield: 220 g

Ingredients

  • 1 recipe French Meringue
  • 250 ml Cream
  • 1 tsp Vanilla extract
  • A/R Chocolate shavings

Method

  1. Pipe meringues into discs (2 or 3 discs for each product). Bake at 100° C and allow it to cool.
  2. Whisk cream and vanilla to a firm peak.
  3. Pipe cream onto meringue discs, place another meringue disc on top to form a sandwich.
  4. With a spatula, spread a layer of cream uniformly along the side. (Note: Coat with chocolate shavings as shown in ingredients and photo).

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 100°C (212°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Meringue discs baked at 100°C and cooled; cream whisked to a firm peak.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Make sure the bowl and the whisk attachments are clean and devoid of any debris or fat, because this can and will affect the texture of the meringue.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Mixing bowls, Hand mixer, Piping bags, Piping nozzle, Silicone mat, Baking tray, Baking paper, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Making a French meringue, Piping, Baking, Whisking.

30. Eton Mess

Category: Meringues & Pavlovas / Plated Dessert | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: Bake meringue and boil berry coulis | Yield: 3 – 4 portions

Ingredients

Base & Chantilly

  • 1 recipe Swiss Meringue
  • 250 ml Whipping Cream
  • ½ tsp Vanilla extract

Berry Coulis

  • 70 g Frozen berry
  • 35 g Sugar
  • 35 g Water
  • 3.5 g Corn flour
  • A/R Mixed berries

Method

  1. Pipe meringues into tray size long pieces. Bake it and allow it to cool.
  2. Whisk cream and vanilla paste to a firm peak.
  3. Place it to piping bag fitted with star nozzle (12-14 mm).
  4. For the mixed berry coulis, boil the berry with the sugar.
  5. Mix corn flour with the water and add it to the berry. Cook it until thicken and allow it to cool down. Fill it into piping bag without nozzle.
  6. Hull strawberries and cut them half.
  7. Arrange the broken meringue sticks into glasses or onto plates with the Chantilly, berry sauce and berries.
  8. Serve immediately.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 100°C (212°F) for meringue; boil for coulis
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Meringue baked and cooled; cream whisked to firm peak; berry coulis cooked until thickened.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Serve immediately.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Make sure the bowl and the whisk attachments are clean and devoid of any debris or fat, because this can and will affect the texture of the meringue.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Mixing bowls, Hand mixer, Piping bags, Piping nozzle, Silicone mat, Baking tray, Baking paper, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Making a Swiss meringue, Piping, Baking, Whisking.

31. Macaron shells with Italian Meringue

Category: Pastry / Petit Fours & Macarons | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 12–14 mins at 140°C (per Chocolate Macaron shells) | Yield: 50 – 60 pieces

Ingredients

  • 120 g Almond Powder
  • 120 g Icing sugar
  • 42 g Egg White (I)
  • 120 g Caster sugar
  • 46 g Egg White (II)
  • 36 g Water

Method

  1. Blend icing sugar and almond powder into the food processor.
  2. Sift the above mixture into a mixing bowl and mix with egg white (I) to form a smooth dough.
  3. Cook sugar and water to 118° C.
  4. Start to whisk egg white (II) in a separate bowl when the sugar reaches the temperature of 115° C.
  5. Pour the cooked sugar syrup (118° C) onto the whisked egg white slowly while continuously whipping the egg whites with a hand beater to make the Italian meringue.
  6. Beat the egg whites to stiff and until it cools down to around 50° C.
  7. To check if the meringue is ready, turn the bowl upside-down and it should not fall off.
  8. If any colour needs to be added, it can be done at this stage.
  9. Add a third of the Italian meringue to the almond dough and mix until smooth.
  10. Fold the remaining meringue into it and fold until it forms a glossy texture.
  11. Pipe the mix onto baking sheet lined with baking paper with piping bag fitted with 10 mm plain nozzle.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Sugar syrup: 118°C (244°F) (start whisking at 115°C / 239°F; cool meringue to ~50°C / 122°F); Bake at 140°C (284°F) for 12–14 minutes.
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Meringue beaten to stiff (does not fall off when bowl is turned upside-down); batter folded until it forms a glossy texture.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: After filling store the macarons in the refrigerator overnight to get the right texture.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Paste colour can be added at any stage of preparation. Powdered colours must be added while preparing the meringue.
      3. Ganache, butter cream, cream mousseline, or jam are some common fillings used.
      4. After filling store the macarons in the refrigerator overnight to get the right texture.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Sieve, Mixing bowls, Rubber spatula, Food processor, Baking tray, Baking paper, Macaroon silicone mats, Plastic scraper, Piping bag, Piping nozzle 10 mm, plain, Sauce pan, Electric hand mixer, Digital /candy thermometer, Cling film, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Grinding, Sifting, Swiss/ Italian meringue, Folding, Piping, Baking, Filling.

32. Chocolate Macaron shells with Italian Meringue

Category: Pastry / Petit Fours & Macarons | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 12 to 14 mins | Yield: 50 – 60 pieces

Ingredients

  • 150 g Almond Powder
  • 150 g Icing sugar
  • 55 g Egg White (I)
  • 60 g Cocoa paste 100%
  • 165 g Caster sugar
  • 55 g Egg White (II)
  • 37 g Water
  • A/R Red colour

Method

  1. Blend icing sugar and almond powder into the food processor.
  2. Sift the above mixture into a mixing bowl and mix with egg white (I) to form a smooth dough.
  3. Cook sugar and water to 118° C.
  4. Start to whisk egg white (II) in a separate bowl when the sugar reaches the temperature of 115° C.
  5. Pour the cooked sugar syrup (118° C) onto the whisked egg white slowly while continuously whipping the egg whites with a hand beater to make the Italian meringue.
  6. Add the red colour at this stage.
  7. Mix a third of the Italian meringue to the almond dough and mix until smooth.
  8. Mix in the melted (still warm) cocoa paste.
  9. Fold the remaining meringue into it and fold until it forms a glossy texture.
  10. Pipe the mix onto baking sheet lined with baking paper with piping bag fitted with 10 mm plain nozzle.
  11. Tap the tray to flatten the macaron and bake at 140° C for 12 to 14 minutes. (Open vent for the last 2-3 minutes of baking.)

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Sugar syrup: 118°C (244°F); Bake at 140°C (284°F) for 12 to 14 minutes (open vent for the last 2–3 minutes of baking).
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Folded until it forms a glossy texture; tray tapped to flatten the macaron before baking.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: After filling store the macarons in the refrigerator overnight to get the right texture.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Paste colour can be added at any stage of preparation. Powdered colours must be added while preparing the meringue.
      3. Ganache, butter cream, cream mousseline, or jam are some common fillings used.
      4. After filling store the macarons in the refrigerator overnight to get the right texture.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Sieve, Mixing bowls, Rubber spatula, Food processor, Baking tray, Baking paper, Macaroon silicone mats, Plastic scraper, Piping bag, Piping nozzle 10 mm, plain, Sauce pan, Electric hand mixer, Digital /candy thermometer, Cling film, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Grinding, Sifting, Swiss/ Italian meringue, Folding, Piping, Baking, Filling.

33. Dark Chocolate Ganache Filling

Category: Custards & Creams / Fillings | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: Bring cream to 85°C | Yield: 150 g

Ingredients

  • 75 g Cream
  • 75 g Dark chocolate

Method

  1. Bring cream to 85° C in a saucepan.
  2. Pour over the top of the chopped chocolate and stir until it is fully melted.
  3. Cover the ganache (contact cling film) and allow it to set in room temperature or fridge. Use when set and still smooth consistency.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 85°C (185°F) for cream
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Fully melted, set and still smooth consistency.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Cover with contact cling film and allow to set at room temperature or in the fridge.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Flavours for dark chocolate ganache include lemon zest and chili, to name just a few.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Sauce pan, Rubber spatula, Mixing bowls, Cling film, Digital thermometer.
    • Key Learning: Making a ganache.

34. Milk Chocolate Filling

Category: Custards & Creams / Fillings | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: Melt at 40°C; pour cream at 35°C | Yield: 150 g

Ingredients

  • 65 g Cream
  • 100 g Milk chocolate
  • 10 g Unsalted butter

Method

  1. Melt chocolate over a Bain Marie at 40° C. Once melted, take the bowl off the Bain Marie and add soft butter.
  2. Boil the cream and bring the temperature down to 35° C and pour onto the chocolate.
  3. Mix to form ganache.
  4. If adding any flavour, it can be added to the cream while heating.
  5. Cover the ganache (contact cling film) and allow it to set in room temperature or fridge. Use when set and still smooth consistency.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Melt chocolate at 40°C (104°F); cool boiled cream to 35°C (95°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Set and still smooth consistency.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Cover with contact cling film and allow to set at room temperature or in the fridge.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Flavours for milk chocolate ganache include; orange zest, sea salt, coffee paste, spices, any aroma oils and essences to name a few.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Sauce pan, Rubber spatula, Mixing bowls, Cling film, Digital thermometer.
    • Key Learning: Making a ganache.

36. White Chocolate Ganache Filling for Fruit Purees

Category: Custards & Creams / Fillings | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: Melt at 40°C; heat puree to 35°C | Yield: 240 g

Ingredients

  • 60 g Fruit puree
  • 130 g Milk chocolate (Note: Printed as “Milk chocolate 130 g” in the original text under White Chocolate Ganache Filling for Fruit Purees)
  • 50 g Unsalted butter

Method

  1. Melt chocolate over a Bain Marie to 40° C. Once melted, take the bowl off the Bain Marie and add soft butter.
  2. Heat puree to 35° C and pour onto the chocolate, make sure the temperature of puree is not above 35° C. Mix to form ganache.
  3. Cover the ganache (contact cling film) and allow it to set in room temperature or fridge. Use when set and still smooth consistency.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Melt chocolate to 40°C (104°F); heat puree to 35°C (95°F) (not above 35°C)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Set and still smooth consistency.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Cover with contact cling film and allow to set at room temperature or in the fridge.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Purees like Raspberry, Strawberry, Passion fruit and Blueberry can be used well and work well.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Sauce pan, Rubber spatula, Mixing bowls, Cling film, Digital thermometer.
    • Key Learning: Making a ganache.

37. Butter Based Filling

Category: Custards & Creams / Fillings | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: Cook anglaise base up to 82°C | Yield: 180 g

Ingredients

  • 25 g Cream
  • 15 g Milk
  • 20 g Egg yolk
  • 20 g Caster sugar
  • 10 g White chocolate
  • 90 g Butter

Method

  1. In a saucepan, bring the cream and milk to a boil.
  2. Whisk the egg yolk and sugar in a mixing bowl and pour the milk over, whisking to mix well.
  3. Pour it back into the saucepan and cook till the liquid becomes thick - up to 82° C.
  4. Pour this over the white chocolate and mix until all the chocolate has melted.
  5. With a hand beater, beat in the butter little by little and cream it until fluffy.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Cook liquid up to 82°C (180°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Liquid cooked until thick (up to 82°C); butter creamed in until fluffy.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. Adding the butter to a warm mixture will melt it and make the butter cream runny.
      2. Fruit puree may be added to the butter cream for variation in flavour.
      3. Other notable flavours include for butter-based filling-nut paste, Coffee paste, spices and any aroma oils, essence.
      4. Adding the butter to a warm mixture will melt it and make the butter cream runny.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Mixing bowl, Sauce pan, Rubber spatula, Hand whisk, Digital thermometer, Electric hand beater.
    • Key Learning: Making an Anglaise, Incorporating butter.

38. Anzac Cookies

Category: Cookies | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 8–10 mins | Yield: 180 g

Ingredients

  • 45 g Oats
  • 75 g All-purpose flour
  • 115 g Sugar
  • 25 g Desiccated coconut
  • 70 g Butter
  • 21 g Honey
  • 15 ml Water
  • ¼ tsp / 1.25 g Baking soda

Method

  1. Preheat the oven to 160° C.
  2. Mix together oats, sifted flour, sugar, and desiccated coconut, and keep aside.
  3. In a sauce pan, heat together butter, honey, and water.
  4. Remove from heat, add bicarbonate of soda, mix in and pour onto the dry ingredients; mix to form a dough.
  5. Shape into 12 gram balls and place on a lightly greased or lined baking tray at least 3 cm apart, as they will spread and flatten when baked.
  6. Bake in the preheated oven for 8-10 minutes or until golden.
  7. Cool on the tray for 5 min or until firm, and loosen from base using an offset spatula.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 160°C (320°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Baked until golden; crunchy and chewy texture; cooled on tray for 5 minutes or until firm.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Anzac biscuit is a sweet biscuit, popular in Australia and New Zealand, made using rolled oats, flour, sugar, butter, and bicarbonate of soda.
      3. The high sugar content makes the cookie melt, spread and caramelize when baking, giving it a crunchy and chewy texture.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Sauce pan, Sieve, Rubber Spatula, Baking tray, Baking paper, Oven mitts/gloves, Cooling rack.
    • Key Learning: Sifting, Melting method, Shaping, Baking.

39. Langues de Chat / Cat Tongue

Category: Cookies | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 8–10 mins | Yield: 15-20 pcs./ 350 g

Ingredients

  • 62.5 g Butter
  • 62.5 g Icing sugar
  • 6 g Vanilla
  • 60 g Egg white
  • 87.5 g All-purpose flour
  • 30 g Dark chocolate 55%

Method

  1. Preheat the oven to 160° C.
  2. Sift flour and icing sugar separately.
  3. Cream the butter, sifted icing sugar, and vanilla until fluffy and add egg whites little by little and mix in.
  4. Fold in flour to form a soft paste.
  5. Fill into a piping bag fitted with a 10 mm plain nozzle.
  6. Pipe about 7 cm long shapes on to a silicone mat or lined baking tray.
  7. Bake in the preheated oven for 8-10 minutes.
  8. When cooled, dip them half way into tempered chocolate and place on baking paper until chocolate sets.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 160°C (320°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Light and crisp cookie; dipped halfway into tempered chocolate and set.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Langue de chat are small flat biscuits or chocolate bars shaped like a ‘Cat’s tongue’.
      3. The high content of egg white makes the cookie light and crisp.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Sieve, Electric hand mixer, Rubber spatula, Piping bag, Nozzle 10 mm, plain, Baking tray, Baking paper or silicone mat, Oven mitt/gloves, Sauce pan.
    • Key Learning: Sifting, Creaming method, Piping, Baking, Tempering.

40. Cantucci / Hazelnut Chocolate Biscotti

Category: Cookies | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 25 mins (first bake) + 8–10 mins (second bake) | Yield: 345g; 15 – 20 pieces

Ingredients

  • 130 g All-purpose flour
  • ¼ tsp / 1 g Baking powder
  • ¼ tsp / 1.25 g Baking soda
  • 100 g Sugar
  • 1/8 tsp / 0.75 g Salt
  • ½ tsp / 1.5 g Cinnamon powder
  • 55 g Hazelnut, chopped
  • 30 ml Espresso (liquid)
  • ½ tbsp / 7.5 ml Milk
  • 50 g Egg, whole
  • ½ tsp / 2 g Vanilla
  • 45 g Chocolate chips

Method

  1. Preheat the oven to 160° C.
  2. Sieve together flour, baking powder, bicarbonate of soda, into a large mixing bowl; then add sugar, salt, cinnamon powder, and chopped hazelnuts, and mix until combined.
  3. Make a well in the middle, and add all the liquid ingredients; mix until almost combined.
  4. Fold in the chocolate chips and continue to mix until well combined to form a soft dough.
  5. Shape the dough into two long narrow cylinders and place on a lined baking tray or tuille mould.
  6. Bake in the preheated oven for 25 minutes.
  7. Allow to cool on the tray and slice to 0.75 - 1 cm equal slices, using a bread knife.
  8. Lay the slices on lined baking trays and bake a second time at 160° C for 8-10 minutes or until dry.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 160°C (320°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Baked 25 minutes as logs, cooled, sliced to 0.75–1 cm, and baked a second time for 8–10 minutes or until dry.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Freezing the loaves after the first baking, makes them easier to slice.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring jug, Measuring spoon set, Sieve, Mixing bowls, Rubber spatula, Plastic scraper, Cling film, Baking tray, Baking paper, Oven mitts/gloves, White chopping board, Bread knife.
    • Key Learning: Chopping, Sifting, All in method, Shaping, Baking.

41. Double Chocolate Drop Cookies

Category: Cookies | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 7–10 mins | Yield: 20-25 pcs./ 350 g

Ingredients

  • 14 g Milk chocolate
  • 24 g Dark chocolate (70%)
  • 42 g Butter, softened
  • 28 g Dark brown sugar
  • 50 g Caster sugar
  • 7 g / ½ tbsp Molasses
  • 28 g Egg, whole
  • 4 g / 1 tsp Vanilla
  • 100 g All-purpose flour
  • 16 g / 2 tbsp Cocoa powder
  • 1.25 g / ¼ tsp Bicarbonate of soda
  • 0.75 g / 1/8 tsp Salt
  • 72 g Chocolate chips, semi sweet, baking

Method

  1. Preheat the oven to 160° C.
  2. Melt the dark and milk chocolate.
  3. Cream the butter, dark brown sugar, caster sugar, and molasses until pale and fluffy.
  4. Beat in egg and vanilla until light.
  5. Add the melted chocolate to the above mixture and mix until combined.
  6. Sift dry ingredients together and add to the butter mixture. Mix with a rubber spatula to make a firm paste and lastly fold in chocolate chips.
  7. Using a small ice cream scoop, scoop the paste onto a lined baking tray, placing each scoop about 3 cm apart.
  8. Rest in the refrigerator until the scoops harden.
  9. Bake in the preheated oven for 7-10 minutes.
  10. Allow to cool on the tray.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 160°C (320°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Butter, sugars, and molasses creamed until pale and fluffy; scooped paste rested in refrigerator until hardened; baked for 7–10 minutes and cooled on the tray.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring jug, Measuring spoon set, Sieve, Mixing bowls, Rubber spatula, Electric hand mixer, Saucepan, Baking paper, Baking tray, Ice cream scoop, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Sifting, Melting, Creaming method, Shaping, Baking.

42. Sesame Seed Tuille

Category: Cookies / Petit Fours | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 4–5 mins | Yield: 230g; 12 – 15 pieces

Ingredients

  • 50 g Butter
  • 50 g Light brown Sugar
  • 50 g Golden syrup
  • 2 g Lemon zest
  • 20 ml Lemon juice
  • 20 g Sesame seeds
  • 50 g All-purpose flour

Method

  1. Preheat the oven to 160° C.
  2. Melt butter, sugar, and golden syrup together.
  3. Add lemon zest, juice, sesame seeds, and flour; mix to make smooth paste.
  4. Spread the mixture onto silicone mats or non-stick trays using stencils.
  5. Bake in the preheated oven for 4-5 minutes or until golden brown.
  6. Take them off the tray and shape them while still hot.
  7. Store in an air tight container until used.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 160°C (320°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Baked for 4–5 minutes or until golden brown; shaped while still hot.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Store in an air tight container until used.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Take care not to make more than 4 tuilles at a time, or they will get too cold and hard to shape.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring jug, Lemon squeezer, Zester, Microplane, Sauce pan, Mixing bowl, Rubber spatula, Palette knife, Silicone mat/non-stick trays, Tuille mould, Oven mitts/gloves, Baking tray.
    • Key Learning: Zesting, Melting method, Using a stencil, Baking, Shaping.

43. Ginger Drop Cookies

Category: Cookies | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 10 – 12 mins | Yield: 345g; 15 – 20 pieces

Ingredients

  • 175 g All-purpose flour
  • ½ tsp / 2 g Baking powder
  • 1/8 tsp / 0.75 g Salt
  • 100 g Soft brown sugar
  • 15 g Ground ginger
  • 60 g Butter
  • 45 g Golden syrup
  • ½ tsp / 2.5 g Baking soda
  • 25 g Egg, whole, beaten
  • 75 g Preserved ginger in syrup, chopped
  • 100 g White caster sugar

Method

  1. Preheat the oven to 160° C.
  2. Into a large mixing bowl, sift flour and baking powder.
  3. Add salt, brown sugar, ground ginger, and mix to combine.
  4. On a gentle heat melt butter and golden syrup in sauce pan.
  5. Remove from heat and stir in the bicarbonate of soda.
  6. Pour the mixture over the dry ingredients then add the egg and 2/3 of the chopped ginger.
  7. Mix to form a soft dough.
  8. Divide and shape the dough into 12 gram balls and roll them in caster sugar to coat.
  9. Place them on lined baking trays, keeping 2.5 cm gap between each other.
  10. Press a few pieces of the remaining chopped preserved ginger onto the top of each cookie.
  11. Bake in the preheated oven for 10 – 12 minutes.
  12. Allow to cool on the tray.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 160°C (320°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Soft dough shaped into 12 g balls, rolled in caster sugar, topped with preserved ginger, and baked for 10–12 minutes.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Allow to cool on the tray.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Fresh ground ginger or dry ginger powder can be used.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Sieve, Sauce pan, Rubber spatula, Chef’s knife, White chopping board, Baking tray, Baking paper, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Sifting, Chopping, Melting method, Shaping, Baking.

44. Almond Cookies

Category: Cookies | Prep: 30 mins (resting in refrigerator) | Cook/Bake: 10–12 mins | Yield: 430g; 30 – 35 pieces

Ingredients

  • 100 g Butter, softened
  • 80 g Caster sugar
  • 18 g Egg yolk
  • ½ tsp / 2.5 g Almond essence
  • 120 g All-purpose flour
  • 80 g Ground almonds
  • 30 – 35 pcs. Almond, whole
  • 10 g Egg white to stick almonds

Method

  1. Preheat the oven to 160° C.
  2. Cream butter and sugar until light and fluffy.
  3. Add the egg yolk and almond essence, and beat well.
  4. Using a spatula mix in the sifted flour and ground almond in to the butter mixture gradually to form a smooth dough.
  5. Divide and roll the dough into 12 gram balls; place on a lightly greased or lined tray about 2.5 cm apart.
  6. Press each round slightly with a whole almond dipped in egg white on top of each cookie to embed.
  7. Rest in the refrigerator for 30 minutes or until hardened.
  8. Bake in the preheated oven for 10 -12 min minutes.
  9. Allow to cool on the baking tray.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 160°C (320°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Creamed butter and sugar light and fluffy; smooth dough; chilled until hardened; baked for 10–12 minutes.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Almond powder can be replaced with other nut powders such as hazelnut, or pistachio.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Sieve, Mixing bowls, Rubber spatula, Electric hand mixer, Saucepan, Baking paper, Baking tray, Ice cream scoop, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Sifting, Melting, Creaming method, Shaping, Baking.

45. Butter Cookies

Category: Cookies | Prep: N/A (chill until hard) | Cook/Bake: 10–12 mins | Yield: 150 g

Ingredients

Base

  • 87.5 g Butter, softened
  • 45 g Light brown sugar
  • 125 g All-purpose flour

Coating

  • 100 g Demerara sugar

Method

  1. Preheat the oven to 160° C.
  2. Cream together the butter and sugar until pale and fluffy.
  3. Mix in the sifted flour to form a smooth dough.
  4. Form into a uniform cylinder of 3 cm diameter and roll in Demerara sugar to coat evenly.
  5. Wrap in cling film and chill until the dough becomes hard.
  6. Slice the dough cylinder into 1 cm thick slices and place on a lightly greased or lined baking tray.
  7. Bake in the pre heated oven for 10–12 minutes.
  8. Cool on the tray before removing.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

46. Sable a la Poche / Vanilla-Chocolate Swirls

Category: Cookies | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 10–12 mins | Yield: 30-35 pcs. / 570 g

Ingredients

Vanilla base

  • 75 g Butter, softened
  • 50 g Icing sugar (sifted)
  • 2 g / ½ tsp Vanilla
  • 7 g Lemon zest
  • 50 g / 1 pc. Egg, whole
  • 125 g All-purpose flour

Chocolate base

  • 75 g Butter, softened
  • 50 g Icing sugar (sifted)
  • 1 g / ¼ tsp Vanilla
  • 110 g All-purpose flour
  • 15 g Cocoa powder
  • 50 g / 1 pc. Egg, whole

Method

  1. Preheat the oven to 160° C.
  2. Cream the butter and icing sugar together until pale and fluffy.
  3. Add vanilla, lemon zest, and egg gradually, and beat for a few more seconds.
  4. Add the dry ingredients to the butter mixture little by little and mix with a spatula, to make a firm paste.
  5. Similarly make the chocolate dough. (Sift the flour and cocoa powder together until uniformly mixed, before adding to the creamed mixture).
  6. Place both the mixtures side by side into a piping bag fitted with star nozzle and pipe into the required shapes.
  7. Bake in the preheated oven for about 10–12 minute.
  8. Allow to cool on the baking tray.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 160°C (320°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Butter and icing sugar creamed until pale and fluffy; mixed with dry ingredients to make a firm paste; baked for about 10–12 minutes.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring jug, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Sieve, Zester, Microplane, Electric hand mixer, Rubber spatula, Piping bag, Star nozzle 10 mm, Baking tray, Oven mitts/gloves, Baking paper.
    • Key Learning: Zesting, Sifting.

47. Florentine Slice

Category: Cookies / Confectionery Bars | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 8–10 mins (optional peanut roasting) | Yield: 740 g ; 12 – 15 small pieces (20 cm x 20 cm x 1.5 cm)

Ingredients

Crunchy Base

  • 110 g Roasted Peanuts
  • 130 g Corn flakes
  • 70 g Sultanas (chopped)
  • 80 g Butter
  • 110 g Peanut butter

Ganache Topping

  • 70 g Dark chocolate 55%
  • 70 g Milk chocolate
  • 100 g Cream

Method

Crunchy Base

  1. Line the base of the frame with aluminum foil and baking paper.
  2. To make the crunchy base, crush peanuts and cornflakes and mix together with the chopped sultanas.
  3. Melt butter and peanut butter together and pour on to the dry ingredients; mix until well combined.
  4. Press in the frame and set in the refrigerator.

Ganache Topping

  1. Combine both the chocolates in a mixing bowl.
  2. Heat the cream and pour on to the chocolate, mix to form a ganache.
  3. Pour it on the top of the set base and spread it evenly.
  4. Chill in the refrigerator until the chocolate layer has set.
  5. Cut into neat squares or rectangles with a bread knife.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: No-bake assembly; 160°C (320°F) for 8–10 minutes if roasting peanuts (per Note 3).
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Base pressed firmly and set in refrigerator; chocolate ganache layer chilled until set; peanuts golden brown if roasted.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Chill in refrigerator until set.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Sultanas could be replaced with any other dry fruit as black currants etc.
      3. Peanuts can be roasted in a preheated oven at 160° C for 8-10 minutes, until golden brown.
      4. Heating the blade of the knife by dipping it in hot water and wiping it dry before each cut, helps to get a neat edge.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Rolling pin, Zip lock bag for crushing, Mixing bowls, Rubber spatula, Sauce pan, 20 cm x 20 cm frame, Aluminium foil, Baking paper, Bread Knife, White chopping board.
    • Key Learning: Crushing, Roasting, Melting method, Lining frame, Cutting.

48. Sea Salted Caramel Date Bar

Category: Cookies / Confectionery Bars | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 10 mins (toasting nuts/seeds) | Yield: 880 g ; 10 – 12 small pieces (18 cm x 18 cm x 1 cm)

Ingredients

Dates, Nuts and Seeds Base

  • 40 g Walnuts
  • 30 g Pistachios
  • 30 g Almonds
  • 30 g Pumpkin seeds
  • 30 g Sunflower seeds
  • 30 g Oats
  • 200 g Dates (pitted)

Salted Caramel

  • 70 g Sugar
  • 20 g Water
  • ¼ tsp / 1.5 g Sea salt
  • 15 g Butter
  • 55 g Cream

Topping

  • 50 g Desiccated coconut
  • 150 g Salted caramel
  • 3 g Sea salt flakes

Method

Date, Nuts and Seeds Base

  1. Preheat the oven to 140° C.
  2. Chop the nuts and seeds, and mix with oats.
  3. Spread evenly on a baking tray and toast in the oven for 10 minutes or until lightly golden.
  4. Chop dates and mix with the warm nuts, seeds, and oats until combined; spread into an 18 cm x 18 cm frame and press it down firmly.

Salted Caramel

  1. Combine sugar and water in a saucepan and cook till it caramelizes.
  2. Remove from heat and stir in salt and softened butter.
  3. In another saucepan, heat the cream and add it little by little into the caramel mixture, and mix well to combine.

Topping

  1. Pour the caramel on to the date and nut base and spread evenly with a palette knife.
  2. Sprinkle with desiccated coconut and sea salt flakes.
  3. Allow to set in the refrigerator.
  4. Cut into squares with a chef’s knife.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 140°C (284°F) for toasting nuts, seeds, and oats.
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Nuts, seeds, and oats toasted until lightly golden; sugar and water cooked till caramelized; chilled in refrigerator until set.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Allow to set in the refrigerator.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Heating the blade of the knife by dipping it in hot water and wiping it dry before each cut, helps to get a neat edge.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Mixing bowls, Sauce pan, Rubber spatula, Palette knife, Chef knife, White chopping board, Baking tray, Square frame 18 cm x 18 cm, Baking paper, Aluminium foil.
    • Key Learning: Chopping, Toasting, All in method, Making a caramel, Cutting, Lining frame.
