51. Black Forest Gateaux

Category: Cakes / Gateaux | Prep: 4 hours (refrigeration before serving) | Cook/Bake: 8 mins | Yield: 1 x 20 cm dia. X 10 cm height / 1200 g (8-12 portions)

Ingredients

A. Genoise Sponge

  • 80 g All-purpose flour
  • 20 g Cocoa powder
  • 200 g Egg, whole
  • 100 g Sugar
  • ½ tsp / 2 g Vanilla
  • 40 g Butter, melted

B. Stock Syrup

  • 100 g Water
  • 20 g Sugar
  • 30 g Amarena cherry syrup

B. Cream Chantilly

  • 450 g Whipping cream
  • 50 g Icing sugar
  • ½ tsp / 2 g Vanilla

B. Filling

  • 80 g Black cherry pitted (canned)

C. Garnish

  • 4 – 8 pcs Black cherries
  • 100 g Chocolate shavings

Method

A. Genoise Sponge

  1. Preheat the oven to 180° C.
  2. Line 3 x 18 cm diameter cake tins with baking paper.
  3. Sift flour and cocoa powder together.
  4. In a large mixing bowl, place egg, vanilla, and sugar, and whisk over a bain marie.
  5. Whisk vigorously up to 40-45° C.
  6. Take off the bain-marie and continue whisking it using an electric hand mixer until light and foamy (ribbon stage).
  7. Fold in sifted flour and cocoa powder in two additions. Do not over mix, else the volume of the batter will decrease.
  8. Temper melted butter with spoonful of cake batter.
  9. Fold the tempered butter into the cake batter and immediately divide the mixture into the 3 cake tins, 125 g into each. Spread them briefly.
  10. Bake in the preheated oven at 180° C for 8 minutes or until done.
  11. Remove from tin and cool on a cooling rack.

B. Stock Syrup and Cream Chantilly

  1. To make the Stock Syrup - Add all the ingredients to a saucepan, bring it to a boil. Cool before using.
  2. To make a Cream Chantilly - Add the whipping cream, icing sugar and vanilla to a large mixing bowl and whisk together to medium peak.
  3. For the filling - Drain and coarsely chop the cherries.

C. Assembling and Finishing

  1. Place one layer of cake on the cake board and, soak with the syrup.
  2. Pipe or spread a layer of cream Chantilly and evenly spread ½ the chopped cherries on top.
  3. Place the second layer of cake on it and press down gently to get an even surface.
  4. Repeat the process and cover the top with the third layer of cake.
  5. Cover the entire cake with an even layer of cream Chantilly.
  6. Decorate the top with piped cream rosettes, cherries, and chocolate shavings.
  7. Refrigerate for at least 4 hours before serving.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 180°C (356°F) for baking sponge; 40–45°C (104–113°F) over bain-marie for egg-sugar mixture.
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Egg mixture whisked until light and foamy (ribbon stage); Cream Chantilly whisked to medium peak (do not over-whisk or it will become grainy); sponge baked for 8 minutes or until done.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Refrigerate for at least 4 hours before serving.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Components Overview: Black Forest Gateaux are made up of 3 components: a. Genoise Sponge, b. Stock Syrup and Cream Chantilly, c. Assembling and Finishing.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Fold the melted butter into the cake batter, just before baking. Fat breaks down the air bubbles, reducing the volume of the sponge.
      3. Use the syrup generously over the sponge otherwise it will be too dry.
      4. Do not over- whisk the cream or it will become grainy in texture.
      5. Refrigerating the cake before serving helps the sponge absorb the syrup and excess liquid from the Chantilly and fluff up.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Cake tins/ rings 3 x 18 cm, Baking paper, Scissors, Sieve, Electric hand mixer, Rubber spatula, Cooling rack, Sauce pan, Strainer, White chopping board, Paring knife, Hand whisk, Turntable, Cake board, Offset palette knife, Piping bag, Piping nozzle, 10 mm, plain and star, Baking tray, Bamboo skewer, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Melting, Sifting, Foaming, Whisking, Folding, Baking, Boiling, Straining, Chopping, Assembling, Piping.
↑ Back to top

52. Swiss Roll / Vanilla Roulade

Category: Cakes / Roulade | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 5–6 mins | Yield: 1 x 20 cm dia. X 10 cm height / 1200 g (8-12 portions)

Ingredients

A. Sponge Sheet

  • 40 g All-purpose flour
  • 10 g Cornstarch
  • 36 g / 2 pcs. Egg yolk
  • 2 g / ½ tsp Vanilla
  • 15 g Sugar I.
  • 60 g / 2 pcs. Egg white
  • 35 g Sugar II.

B. Filling and Finishing

  • 100 g Raspberry Jam
  • 15 ml Hot water

Garnish

  • 20 g Icing sugar

Method

A. Sponge Sheet

  1. Preheat the oven to 180° C.
  2. Sift flour and cornstarch together.
  3. Whisk egg yolk, vanilla, and sugar I. with an electric mixer, until light and fluffy (ribbon stage).
  4. Whisk egg whites with an electric mixer until medium firm peak and add sugar II. Little by little to make a French meringue.
  5. Add a little egg white to the egg yolk and fold together.
  6. Mix the rest of the egg white smooth with a spatula until creamy, but still firm and fold it in to the egg yolk mixture.
  7. Fold in the flour. Mix until just combined.
  8. Spread the mixture evenly to 5 mm thickness on baking paper lined tray to get an approx. sheet of 25 cm x 22 cm.
  9. Bake it in the preheated oven for 5-6 minutes.
  10. Evenly sprinkle caster sugar on a sheet of baking paper similar to the size of the baked sponge.
  11. Cool the sponge on the tray for 5 minutes then turn it over the caster sugar and peel off the baking paper it was baked on.

B. Filling and Finishing

  1. To prepare the filling mix the jam and add water as required to make it smooth and spreadable.
  2. Spread the filling on the sponge leaving 1 cm at the end clean.
  3. Gently with even pressure, start rolling the sponge from one side, ensuring that there are no gaps.
  4. Wrap the roll in the baking paper, fasten with cling film, place in a baguette tray and set in the refrigerator or freezer.
  5. To serve, dust with icing sugar and slice the roll into even and thick slices.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 180°C (356°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Egg yolk mixture whisked to ribbon stage; egg whites whisked to medium firm peak (French meringue); sponge spread to 5 mm thickness (approx. 25 cm x 22 cm) and baked for 5–6 minutes; rolled tightly with no gaps.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Set in the refrigerator or freezer wrapped in baking paper and cling film.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Components Overview: Swiss rolls are made up of 2 components: a. Sponge Sheet, b. Filling and Finishing.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Jam Rolly-Polly is a product using sweet pastry and jam. The dough is rolled (as roulade) with the jam row and bake with the jam.
      3. For chocolate roulade: Substitute the cornstarch with cocoa powder. Method remains the same.
      4. For variation in filling: Ganache based, butter based fillings, cream Chantilly, mousses, jam, berries, and fresh fruit, etc… can be used.
      5. Jam is the traditional filling for a Swiss roll.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Sieve, Electric hand mixer, Rubber spatula, Baking tray, Baking paper, Scissors, Oven mitts/gloves, Offset pallet knife.
    • Key Learning: Sifting, Foaming method, Folding, Baking, Whipping, Rolling.
↑ Back to top

53. Sour Cream Coffee Cake

Category: Cakes | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 20–25 mins | Yield: 18 cm x 18 cm x 3 cm / 400 g (1 small box tin/6-8 portions)

Ingredients

A. Sponge Sheet (Sour Cream Cake)

  • 90 g All-purpose flour
  • ½ tsp / 2 g Baking powder
  • 40 g Butter
  • 80 g Caster sugar
  • 50 g Egg, whole
  • ½ tsp / 2 g Vanilla
  • ½ tsp / 2.5 ml Vinegar
  • ½ tsp / 2.5 g Baking soda
  • 80 g Sour cream

B. Topping

  • 35 g Granulated sugar
  • 30 g Walnut (chopped)
  • 5 g Butter
  • 2 tsp / 6 g Cinnamon powder

Method

A. Sponge Sheet

  1. Preheat the oven to 165° C.
  2. Line the base of the frame or tin with aluminum foil and baking paper.
  3. Sift flour and baking powder.
  4. In a large mixing bowl cream butter and sugar together, until pale and fluffy.
  5. Add vanilla and egg little by little; Mix until combined.
  6. Place bicarbonate of soda in a spoon, pout the vinegar over it and mix it in to the mixture.
  7. Fold in dry ingredients and sour cream in 3 additions. Alternate with each other.
  8. Spread half the batter in the frame and sprinkle half the topping evenly on the surface.
  9. Repeat the with the remaining batter and topping.
  10. Bake in the preheated oven for 20 – 25 minutes, or until done.

B. Topping

  1. Mix all ingredients together for topping and set on the side.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 165°C (329°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Butter and sugar creamed until pale and fluffy; baked for 20–25 minutes or until done.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Components Overview: Sour cream coffee cakes are made up of 2 components: a. Sour Cream Cake, b. Topping.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Sieve, Mixing bowls, Rubber spatula, Baking frame or tin, Baking paper, Aluminium foil, Scissors, Electric hand mixer or planetary mixer with paddle attachment, Baking tray, Paring knife/bamboo skewer, Bread knife, White chopping board.
    • Key Learning: Chopping, Sifting, Creaming method, Layering, Baking.

54. Old Fashioned Cherry Cake

Category: Cakes | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 20–25 mins | Yield: 360 g; 6 - 8 portions

Ingredients

  • 100 g All-purpose flour
  • ½ tsp / 2 g Baking powder
  • 1/8 tsp / 0.75 g Salt
  • 90 g Amarena cherry (chopped)
  • 60 g Icing sugar
  • 50 g Butter
  • ½ tsp / 2 g Vanilla
  • 5 g Glucose syrup
  • 50 g Egg, whole
  • 25 ml Milk

Method

  1. Preheat the oven to 165° C.
  2. Line the tin with baking paper.
  3. Sift flour and baking powder together, and mix in salt.
  4. Toss with the chopped cherries in the sifted flour.
  5. In a large mixing bowl cream butter, sugar, glucose syrup, and vanilla, together until pale and fluffy.
  6. Add egg little by little, and mix until smooth.
  7. Fold in flour- fruit mixture and milk. Alternate with each other in 3 additions and mix until just combined.
  8. Pour cake mixture into the prepared tin.
  9. Bake in the preheated oven for 20 - 25 minutes or until done.
  10. Remove from baking tin and cool on a cooling rack.
  11. To serve, dust with icing sugar.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 165°C (329°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Butter, sugar, glucose syrup, and vanilla creamed until pale and fluffy; baked for 20–25 minutes or until done.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. To make an English fruit cake, replace the amarena cherry to 30 grams of raisin and 60g of candied orange peel or other candied fruit.
      3. Tossing the fruit in the flour prevents it from sinking to the base when baking.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring jug, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Fork, Chef knife, White chopping board, Baking tin, Baking paper, Sieve, Electric hand mixer, Rubber spatula, Bread knife, Baking tray, Paring knife/bamboo skewer.
    • Key Learning: Sifting, Creaming method, Baking.

55. Pineapple Upside Down Cake

Category: Cakes | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 12–15 mins (individual) or 25–30 mins (large ring) | Yield: 4 x 8 cm rings for individual portions or 1 x 16 cm ring/ 4 portions/ 300 g

Ingredients

Cake Batter

  • 75 g All-purpose flour
  • 1 tsp / 4 g Baking powder
  • 1/8 tsp / 0.5 g Salt
  • 50 g Butter
  • 50 g Sugar
  • ½ tsp / 2 g Vanilla
  • 50 g Egg, whole
  • 40 ml Pineapple/ Pear can water

Pineapple Base

  • A/R Soft brown sugar
  • A/R Butter
  • 1 pc. per 8 cm ring / 4-6 pcs. per large ring Pineapple rings (small, canned)
  • A/R Maraschino cherries

Method

  1. Preheat the oven to 165° C.
  2. Prepare the rings: seal them with baking paper, aluminum foil, and sprinkle a thin layer of brown sugar on the base. Crumble butter on to the brown sugar, and place the pineapple ring(s) to cover the entire surface, trimming as required to fill in the gaps. Fill the centre of each pineapple ring with a cherry.
  3. To make the cake batter, sift flour and baking powder together and mix in salt.
  4. In a large mixing bowl, cream butter and sugar until light and fluffy.
  5. Add vanilla, pour in egg little by little, and mix to combine.
  6. Fold in dry ingredients alternating with the milk in two additions and mix it until just combined.
  7. Pour the mixture equally into the prepared ring/s and spread the surface evenly.
  8. Bake in the preheated oven for 12 - 15 minutes (individual) or 25 - 30 minutes (large ring).
  9. When hot, turn out upside down on a cooling rack and cool before serving.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 165°C (329°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Butter and sugar creamed until light and fluffy; baked for 12–15 minutes (individual) or 25–30 minutes (large ring); turned out upside down while hot.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Pineapple can be replaced with different fresh or canned fruits as apple, peach, apricot, etc. for variation.
      3. An upside down pineapple cake can be served with sauce Anglaise.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring jug, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Fork, Chef knife, White chopping board, Baking tin, Baking paper, Sieve, Electric hand mixer, Rubber spatula, Bread knife, Baking tray, Paring knife/bamboo skewer.
    • Key Learning: Sifting, Creaming method, Baking.

56. Victoria Sandwich Cake

Category: Cakes | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 8–10 mins | Yield: 1 x 16 cm dia. cake/ 8 - 12 portions/ 740 g

Ingredients

A. Victoria Sponge

  • 115 g All-purpose flour
  • 1.5 tsp / 6 g Baking powder
  • 115 g Butter
  • 115 g Caster sugar
  • 2 g Vanilla
  • 100 g Egg, whole
  • 15 ml Milk

B. Jam and Chantilly Filling

  • 100 g Raspberry jam
  • 15 ml / 1 tbsp Hot water
  • 200 g Whipping cream
  • 20 g Icing sugar
  • 2 g / ½ tsp Vanilla

Method

A. Victoria Sponge

  1. Preheat the oven to 160° C.
  2. Line 3 x 16 cm diameter sandwich tins with baking paper.
  3. Sift together flour and baking powder.
  4. In a large mixing bowl, cream butter and sugar with an electric mixer until pale and fluffy.
  5. Add vanilla and beaten egg little by little and mix until combined.
  6. Fold in dry ingredients and milk alternate with each other in 2 or 3 additions and mix until just combined.
  7. Scale and divide mixture equally into three rings; spread the batter evenly.
  8. Bake in the preheated oven for 8 - 10 minutes, or until done.
  9. Cool for 5 minutes in the tin and then remove and allow to cool on a cooling rack.

B. Jam and Chantilly Filling

  1. Mix the jam with a spatula to make it smooth and spreadable. Add a little hot water if required to get the right consistency.
  2. Make a Chantilly by whipping the cream with the icing sugar and vanilla to firm peak, and fill into a piping bag with 10 mm plain nozzle.
  3. Place one layer of cake on a cake board and spread half the jam evenly, taking care not to go over the edge.
  4. Pipe half the Chantilly on the top of the jam taking care not to go over the edge.
  5. Place another layer of cake over and repeat.
  6. To finish, dust the top of the cake with icing sugar, using a stencil.
  7. Store refrigerated.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 160°C (320°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Butter and sugar creamed until pale and fluffy; Chantilly whipped to firm peak; sponge baked for 8–10 minutes or until done.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Store refrigerated. Leave at room temperature for 10–15 minutes before serving as the butter in the cake hardens when refrigerated.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Components Overview: Victoria Sandwich cakes are made up of 2 components: a. Victoria Sponge, b. Jam and Chantilly Filling.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. The above recipe could be used to make any flavoured pound cake as orange, lemon etc.
      3. The butter in the cake hardens when refrigerated; hence the cake can be assembled just before service or left at room temperature for 10 – 15 minutes before serving.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Sieve, Rubber spatula, Sandwich tin round, 3 x 16 cm, Baking paper, Scissors, Electric hand mixer, Piping bag, Nozzle 10 mm, plain, Offset palette knife, Strainer, Stencil, Oven mitts/gloves, Cooling rack, Baking tray, Turn table, Cake board, Paring knife/ bamboo skewer.
    • Key Learning: Lining rings, Sifting, Creaming method, Baking, Whipping, Piping, Dusting.
↑ Back to top

57. Carrot Cake

Category: Cakes | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 10–15 mins | Yield: 650 g (1 small cake loaf tin or cake ring/ 6-8 portions)

Ingredients

A. Carrot Cake

  • ½ cup / 65 g All-purpose flour
  • ½ tsp / 2 g Baking powder
  • ½ tsp / 2.5 g Baking soda
  • ½ tsp / 1.5 g Cinnamon powder
  • 90 g Sugar
  • 1/8 tsp / 0.75 g Salt
  • 50 g Egg, whole
  • 1/3 cup / 80 ml Vegetable oil
  • ½ tsp / 2 g Vanilla
  • ¾ cup / 35 g Carrot (finely grated)
  • 20 g Sultanas
  • 30 g Walnuts (chopped)
  • ½ cup / 65 g All-purpose flour (Note: listed twice in original ingredient column)

B. Cream Cheese Frosting

  • 75 g Butter
  • 30 g Icing sugar
  • 120 g Cream cheese

Finishing

  • 100 g Walnuts, chopped
  • 8 pcs. Marzipan carrots

Method

A. Carrot Cake

  1. Preheat the oven to 165° C.
  2. Line the base of the sandwich tins with baking paper.
  3. Into a large bowl, sieve together flour, baking powder, bicarbonate of soda, and cinnamon powder, and mix in sugar and salt.
  4. In another bowl, mix together eggs, vegetable oil, and vanilla, and mix in the dry ingredients in 2 - 3 additions, until combined.
  5. Fold in finely grated carrot, sultanas, and nuts.
  6. Pour batter equally into the 3 prepared cake tins.
  7. Bake in the preheated oven for 10-15 minutes, or until done.
  8. Allow the cakes to cool in tins for 5 minutes, and then turn out onto a cooling rack.

B. Cream Cheese Frosting

  1. Cream the butter and sieved icing sugar until fluffy.
  2. Beat cream cheese separately for a very short time to smoothen the lumps, and add to the butter mixture.
  3. Mix until just combined, using a spatula. Do not over mix, or the cream cheese will curdle due to the high fat content.
  4. Fill frosting into piping bag set with a 10 mm plain or star nozzle.

Assembling and Finishing

  1. Place one layer of cake on a cake board and pipe 1/3 rd of the frosting evenly on the surface.
  2. Place the next layer and repeat until all the layers are completed.
  3. Decorate with chopped or whole walnuts and small carrots made from colored marzipan.
  4. Refrigerate until served.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 165°C (329°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Baked for 10–15 minutes or until done; butter and icing sugar creamed until fluffy; cream cheese mixed until just combined without curdling.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Refrigerate until served.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Components Overview: Carrot cakes are made up of 2 components: a. Carrot Cake, b. Cream Cheese Frosting.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Carrot cake need not be layered; it can also be baked as a whole in round, square or loaf tins.
      3. Carrot can be substituted with grated beetroot for variation.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring jug, Measuring cup set, Measuring spoon set, Sieve, Mixing bowls, Rubber spatula, Grater, White chopping board, Chef knife, Sandwich tin round, 3 x 16 cm, Baking paper, Scissors, Electric hand mixer, Piping bag, Palette knife, Oven mitts/gloves, Cooling rack, Bread knife, Turn table, Paring knife/ bamboo skewer.
    • Key Learning: Sifting, All in method, Baking, Mixing, Piping, Assembling.

58. Banana Cake Loaf

Category: Cakes | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 25–30 mins | Yield: 10 portions/ 745 g (1 small long cake loaf tin/8-10 portion)

Ingredients

Cake Batter

  • 100 g All-purpose flour
  • ¼ tsp / 1 g Baking powder
  • ½ tsp / 2.5 g Baking soda
  • 1/8 tsp / 0.75 g Salt
  • 40 g Butter or oil
  • 2 pcs / 250 g Bananas, ripe
  • 115 g Sugar
  • 50 g Egg, whole
  • ½ tsp / 2 g Vanilla

Topping

  • 125 g Banana
  • 30 g Caster sugar

Method

  1. Preheat the oven to 165° C.
  2. Line the loaf tin with baking paper.
  3. Sift together flour, baking powder, and bicarbonate of soda, and mix in salt.
  4. Mix melted butter and mashed bananas in a large mixing bowl.
  5. Add in the sugar, egg, and vanilla and mix until combined.
  6. Finally add the flour and mix until just combined.
  7. Pour mixture into the loaf pan.
  8. Bake in the preheated oven for 25-30 minutes or until done.
  9. Remove from tin after 5 minutes and allow to cool on a cooling rack.

Finishing

  1. Can be dusted with icing sugar, sliced and served.
  2. Can be decorated with thick slices of banana dusted with icing sugar and torched for caramelization.
  3. Can also be decorated with roasted, candied or dried banana slices or nuts.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 165°C (329°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Baked for 25–30 minutes or until done; optional banana slices torched for caramelization.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Chocolate banana loaf could be made by replacing 15 grams of flour with cocoa powder for the above recipe or adding chocolate chips as required into the batter.
      3. To reduce sweetness, sugar can be reduced by 15 – 20 g.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Sieve, Loaf tin, small, Baking paper, Scissors, Hand whisk, Rubber spatula, Strainer, Chef knife, White chopping board, Bread knife, Baking tray, Bamboo skewer, Oven mitts/gloves, Blowtorch.
    • Key Learning: Melting, Sieving, All in method, Baking.

59. Red Velvet Cupcakes

Category: Cakes / Cupcakes | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 10–12 mins | Yield: 10 medium or 24 mini cup cakes / 900 g

Ingredients

A. Red Velvet Cup Cake

  • 125 g All-purpose flour
  • ½ tsp / 2 g Baking powder
  • 7.5 g Cocoa powder
  • 1/8 tsp / 0.75 g Salt
  • 80 g Butter (unsalted)
  • 150 g Caster sugar
  • 50 g Egg, whole
  • 1 tsp / 4 g Vanilla
  • A/R Liquid red food coloring
  • 5 ml White vinegar
  • ½ tsp / 2.5 g Baking soda
  • 130 g Buttermilk
  • 125 g All-purpose flour (Note: listed twice in original ingredient column)

B. Cream Cheese Frosting

  • 125 g Cream cheese
  • 175 g Whipping cream
  • ½ tsp / 2 g Vanilla
  • 50 g Icing sugar

Garnish

  • 20 g Silver Pearls

Method

A. Red Velvet Cup Cake

  1. Preheat the oven to 160° C.
  2. Line mini cupcake tins with paper cases.
  3. In a mixing bowl sift together the flour, baking powder, and cocoa powder, and add the salt.
  4. In a large mixing bowl, cream the butter and sugar until light and fluffy.
  5. Add the egg little by little, beating well after each addition.
  6. Add the vanilla and red colour, and beat until combined.
  7. Separately in a table spoon combine the vinegar and bicarbonate of soda. Allow the mixture to fizz and then quickly mix into butter and egg mixture.
  8. Fold in the sifted flour and the buttermilk to the mixture, in three additions, alternating with each other.
  9. Pour cake mixture into a piping bag without nozzle and pipe to ¾ of the cupcake cases.
  10. Bake in the preheated oven for 10-12 minutes, or until a toothpick inserted in the center of the cupcakes comes out clean.
  11. Allow them to cool down completely on a cooling rack before decorating.

B. Cream Cheese Frosting

  1. To make the frosting, beat the cream cheese to just remove the lumps. Overbeating will make it curdle.
  2. Separately whisk the cream with the sugar and vanilla to soft peak.
  3. Using a spatula, fold it to the cheese in 3 additions. As you complete the folding the texture will reach stiff peak.
  4. Pipe the frosting onto the top of the cupcakes decoratively and finish with sugar pearl.
  5. Keep refrigerated until served.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 160°C (320°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Toothpick inserted in the center of the cupcakes comes out clean (10–12 mins); cream whisked to soft peak, reaching stiff peak once folded into cream cheese in 3 additions.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Keep refrigerated until served.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Components Overview: Red velvet cupcakes are made up of 2 components: a. Red Velvet Cupcake, b. Cream Cheese Frosting.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. To make a Red Velvet Cake, bake the cake batter in a cake tin. With a serrated knife, cut the cake in three equal layers, horizontally. Place one of the cake layers on a cake board. Spread a layer of frosting on it. Repeat until all layers are stacked. Frost the top and sides of the cake. Decorate as desired.
      3. Sour cream or yoghurt can be used to substitute the buttermilk.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Sieve, Muffin tin, Baking paper, Piping Bag, Piping Nozzle, Scissors, Hand whisk, Rubber spatula, Strainer, Baking tray, Bamboo skewer, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Melting, Sieving, All in method, Baking.
↑ Back to top

60. Sticky Date Pudding

Category: Plated Dessert / Cakes | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 8–10 mins | Yield: 400 g / 4 – 5 pieces

Ingredients

Date Pudding

  • 60 g Pear (canned)
  • 60 g Dates (pitted)
  • 175 g Water
  • 90 g All-purpose flour
  • 1 tsp / 5 g Baking soda
  • 30 g Butter
  • 85 g Dark brown sugar
  • 50 g Egg, whole
  • ¼ tsp / 1 g Vanilla

Toffee Sauce

  • 125 g Caster sugar
  • 5 g Butter
  • 125 g Cream

Garnish

  • 6 – 8 pcs. Pecan nuts

Method

Date Pudding

  1. Preheat the oven to 160° C.
  2. Line muffin tins with paper cases or line the desired cake tin/ring.
  3. Place pitted dates in a bowl and sprinkle bicarbonate of soda.
  4. Bring water to a boil and pour over the dates. Allow to stand until cool. Mix in finely minced or pureed pears.
  5. Sift flour, and set aside.
  6. In a mixing bowl, cream the butter and brown sugar until light and fluffy.
  7. Add vanilla and egg and beat in.
  8. Using a spatula, mix in softened date mixture.
  9. Fold in four (flour).
  10. Fill the batter into piping a bag no nozzle and pipe to ¾ of the cupcake cases.
  11. Bake in the preheated oven for 8-10 minutes or until done.

Toffee Sauce

  1. Caramelize sugar in a heavy bottomed saucepan.
  2. Add butter and stir in.
  3. Pour warm cream onto it gently, stirring constantly and bring to a boil.

Garnish

  1. To serve, trim the cakes and place them in the presentation plate.
  2. Pour hot toffee sauce to soak the cake.
  3. Serve warm garnished with pecan.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 160°C (320°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Butter and brown sugar creamed until light and fluffy; baked for 8–10 minutes or until done; served warm soaked in hot toffee sauce.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Salt flakes can be added to the toffee sauce or sprinkled on the presented dessert before serving to get salted caramel.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Sieve, Piping Bag, Muffin tin, Baking paper, Hand whisk, Rubber spatula, Strainer, Chef knife, White chopping board, Bread knife, Baking tray, Bamboo skewer, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Melting, Sieving, Creaming Method, Baking.

61. Chocolate Cupcake

Category: Cakes / Cupcakes | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 8–10 mins | Yield: 10-12 pcs. / 750 g

Ingredients

Chocolate Cupcakes

  • 25 g Cocoa powder
  • 80 g Water
  • 85 g All-purpose flour
  • 1.5 tsp / 6 g Baking powder
  • 1/8 tsp / 0.75 g Salt
  • 55 g Butter
  • 100 g Sugar
  • 50 g Egg, whole
  • 1 tsp / 4 g Vanilla

Frosting

  • 150 g Butter
  • 30 g Cocoa powder
  • 150 g Icing sugar

Garnish

  • A/R Chocolate Garnishes

Method

Chocolate Cupcakes

  1. Preheat the oven at 160° C.
  2. Prepare the muffin tins with paper cases.
  3. In a small mixing bowl stir cocoa powder and boiling hot water until smooth and allow cooling.
  4. Sift together the flour, baking powder and salt.
  5. In a large mixing bowl, cream butter and sugar until light and fluffy. Add egg and vanilla, and beat until combined.
  6. Fold in dry ingredients and cocoa mixture in 3 additions, alternate with each other.
  7. Fill the batter into a piping bag with no nozzle and pipe to ¾ of the cupcake cases.
  8. Bake in a preheated oven for 8-10 minutes or until done.

Frosting

  1. Cream the butter, sifted cocoa powder and icing sugar until fluffy.
  2. Pipe the frosting from a piping bag with the desired nozzle, onto the cupcakes.
  3. Decorate with chocolate or sugar decoration.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 160°C (320°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Cocoa powder and boiling hot water stirred until smooth; butter and sugar creamed until light and fluffy; baked for 8–10 minutes or until done.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. For mocha flavor, add 1 tsp instant coffee powder to the boiling water mixture.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Sieve, Mixing bowls, Rubber spatula, Electric hand mixer, Muffin tins, Cupcake paper cases, Piping bag, Piping nozzle, as desired, Baking tray, Bamboo skewer, Oven gloves/mitts.
    • Key Learning: Sifting, Creaming method, Piping, Baking.

62. Apple, Oats and Cinnamon Muffins

Category: Cakes / Muffins | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 12–15 mins | Yield: 6 pieces / 400 g (12 - 14 small pieces)

Ingredients

Muffin Batter

  • 1 pc. / 70 g Apple, chopped
  • 30 ml Orange juice
  • 85 g All-purpose flour
  • 20 g Oats
  • ½ tsp / 2.5 g Baking soda
  • 95 g White sugar
  • ½ tsp / 1.5 g Cinnamon powder
  • 60 ml Vegetable oil
  • 50 g Egg, whole
  • ½ tsp / 2 g Vanilla
  • 5 g Orange zest
  • 15 g Walnuts, chopped
  • 5 g Raisin

Topping

  • 15 g Icing sugar
  • ½ tsp / 1.5 g Cinnamon powder

Method

  1. Preheat to oven to 180° C.
  2. Line ½ cup muffin tins with paper cases.
  3. Peel, core, and chop the apple and toss it with orange juice.
  4. In a large bowl, combine all the dry ingredients except raisins and nuts.
  5. Mix oil, egg, vanilla, and lemon zest (or orange zest per ingredients list) together and add the dry blend. Mix until smooth.
  6. Fold in apples, raisins, and nuts and mix until combined.
  7. Pour batter into a piping bag without nozzle and pipe to fill the muffin cases.
  8. Bake in the preheated oven, for 12-15 minutes or until done.
  9. Remove from muffin tin and cool on a cooling rack.
  10. To serve, dust with a mixture of icing sugar and Cinnamon powder.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 180°C (356°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Baked for 12–15 minutes or until done.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Apples could be replaced with pears, carrots, etc. to make desired muffins.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring jug, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Peeler, Corer, Zester/Microplane, Citrus juicer, White chopping board, Chef knife, Muffin tins, ½ cup, Muffin cases, Rubber spatula, Piping bag, Cooling rack, Baking tray, Bamboo skewer, Oven gloves/ mitts.
    • Key Learning: Peeling, Coring, Zesting, Chopping, All in method, Piping, Baking, Dusting.

Ingredients

Muffin Batter

  • ¼ tsp / 1.25 g Baking soda
  • 65 g Sugar
  • 1/8 tsp / 0.75 g Salt
  • 75 g Yoghurt
  • ½ tsp / 2 g Vanilla
  • 50 g Egg, whole
  • 40 g Vegetable oil
  • 50 g Frozen blueberry

Crumble Topping

  • 75 g All-purpose flour
  • 25 g Sugar
  • 50 g Butter

Method

  1. Preheat the oven to 180° C.
  2. To make the muffins, sift flour and bicarbonate of soda together, and add sugar and salt; Mix to combine.
  3. Mix yoghurt, vanilla, eggs, and oil together, and mix in to the dry ingredients.
  4. Fold in the frozen blueberry puree and portion the batter to tulip cases or muffin tins lined with paper cases.
  5. Make a crumble by mixing flour, sugar, and butter to a bread crumb texture.
  6. Sprinkle generously on top of the muffin mixture.
  7. Bake in the preheated oven for 12 -15 minutes or until done.
  8. Remove from muffin tin and cool on a cooling rack.

      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Blueberries could also be replaced with other frozen berry or fruit purees.
      3. For variation the butter in the crumble can be replaced with peanut butter.

    • Key Learning: Melting, Sieving, All in method, Baking.

64. Multigrain Loaves

Category: Breads | Prep: 1 day soaking + ~75–80 mins proofing | Cook/Bake: 21 mins (loaf) or ~8 mins (rolls) | Yield: 860g

Ingredients

Multigrain Dough

  • 150 g Multigrain Flour
  • 350 g Bread Flour
  • 10 g Yeast
  • 8 g Salt
  • 290 ml Water
  • 30 g Molasses/White sugar

Assorted Seeds

  • 16.5 g Flax seed
  • 6.5 g Sunflower Seed
  • 3.3 g Sesame Seed White
  • 3.3 g Sesame Seed Dark
  • 6.5 g Pumpkin Seed
  • 10 g Chia Seed
  • 33.3 g Water

Topping

  • A/R Mixed Seeds

Method

Pre-prep

  1. Soak all the grains in water 1 day before.

Mixing

  1. Sift the bread flour into a mixing bowl and add multigrain flour on top. Add the dry yeast and sugar on one side of the bowl and salt on the other side.

Kneading

  1. Mix the dry ingredients and the soaked seeds and incorporate the water in small additions till it forms into a medium to soft dough.

1st Proofing

  1. Prove the dough for about 45 minutes in the prover OR at room temperature or until the dough doubles in volume.

Knock down

  1. Knock back the dough on a clean table with a little extra flour if necessary.

Pre-shaping & Portioning

  1. For a loaf, scale 500g and shape accordingly, for loaves (rolls), scale 50g portions and shape as required.
  2. Shape it into a round (boule) or long (batard) shape.

2nd Proofing

  1. Rest for another 15 minutes.

Final Proofing

  1. Shape the dough as required once again and spray the surface of the loaf/rolls with water and then press on the seeds gently or dip the shaped dough onto a tray of the assorted soaked seeds gently and prove the loaves for a further 15 - 20 minutes for a final proof..

Scoring

  1. Score the loaves with a bakers blade.

Baking

  1. Bake the loaf at 220° C for 10 minutes, with 1 - 2 minutes steam, then reduce the temperature to 180° C for 8 minutes and finish the baking process by baking the bread with the vent open for the last 3 minutes.
  2. If baking 50 g rolls, bake at 200° C for 5 minutes with 1 minute steam, and at 160° C till the bread is done with the vent open for the last 3 minutes.

Cooling

  1. Remove from the oven and rest on a wire rack.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Loaf: 220°C (428°F) for 10 mins with 1–2 mins steam, then 180°C (356°F) for 8 mins + 3 mins with vent open. Rolls (50 g): 200°C (392°F) for 5 mins with 1 min steam, then 160°C (320°F) until done with vent open for last 3 mins. Prover: 27–35°C (80.6–95°F).
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Medium to soft dough; proofed until doubled in volume; cooled properly on a wire rack before slicing.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. For commercial production of bread, the temperatures and proving times mentioned in the recipe must be followed.
      3. The Prover should be set between 27-35° C.
      4. For home baking, the temperatures mentioned have to be increased by 20° C, since home ovens are slightly weaker than industrial grade.
      5. To prove the dough without a prover, the dough has to rest until it doubles in volume. The timing may vary depending on how cold or warm the bread making environment is.
      6. It is important to cool the bread properly after baking before slicing into it because the internal heat contained within the loaf/roll accounts for part of the baking process.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Oven with steam, Proofer, Dough kneader, Mixing bowls, Rolling pins, Loaf tins, Baking trays, Measuring spoon set, Sieve, Baking paper, Scissors, Bread knife, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Kneading, Proofing, Shaping, Scoring, Baking.
↑ Back to top

65. Wholemeal Loaf

Category: Breads | Prep: ~60 mins proofing | Cook/Bake: 21 mins (loaf) or ~8 mins (rolls) | Yield: 860g

Ingredients

Whole meal Dough

  • 150 g Whole meal flour
  • 350 g Bread Flour
  • 10 g Instant dry yeast
  • 8 g Salt
  • 320 ml Water
  • 30 g Molasses / White Sugar

Topping

  • A/R Oatmeal

Method

Mixing

  1. Sift the flour into a mixing bowl and add whole meal flour on top. Add the dry yeast and sugar on one side of the bowl and salt on the other side. If using molasses, it can be dissolved in the water and incorporated into the dough with the wet ingredients.

Kneading

  1. Mix the dry ingredients well and incorporate the water in small additions till it forms into a medium to soft dough.

1st Proofing

  1. Prove the dough for about 45 minutes in the prover or until the dough doubles in volume.

Knock down

  1. Knock back the dough on a clean table with a little extra flour if necessary.

Pre-shaping & Portioning

  1. Shape it into a round (boule) or long (batard) shape.
  2. For a loaf, scale 500g and shape accordingly, for loaves (rolls), scale 50g portions and shape as required.

2nd Proofing

  1. Prove the dough for 15 minutes in the room temperature.

Final Proofing

  1. Shape the dough as required once again and spray the surface of the loaf/rolls with water and then press on the oatmeal gently or dip the shaped dough onto a tray of oatmeal gently.

Scoring

  1. Score the loaves with a bakers blade.

Baking

  1. Bake the loaf at 220° C for 10 minutes, with 1 - 2 minutes steam, then reduce the temperature to 180° C for 8 minutes and finish the baking process by baking the bread with the vent open for the last 3 minutes.
  2. If baking 50 g rolls, bake at 200° C for 5 minutes with 1 minute steam, and at 160° C till the bread is done with the vent open for the last 3 minutes.

Cooling

  1. Remove from the tray immediately after baking and transfer it onto a wire rack to cool down.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Loaf: 220°C (428°F) for 10 mins with 1–2 mins steam, then 180°C (356°F) for 8 mins + 3 mins with vent open. Rolls (50 g): 200°C (392°F) for 5 mins with 1 min steam, then 160°C (320°F) until done with vent open for last 3 mins. Prover: 27–35°C (80.6–95°F).
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Medium to soft dough; proofed until doubled in volume; cooled on a wire rack before slicing.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. For commercial production of bread, the temperatures and proving times mentioned in the recipe must be followed.
      3. The Prover should be set between 27-35° C.
      4. For home baking, the temperatures mentioned have to be increased by 20° C, since home ovens are slightly weaker than industrial grade.
      5. To prove the dough without a prover, the dough has to rest until it doubles in volume. The timing may vary depending on how cold or warm the bread making environment is.
      6. It is important to cool the bread properly after baking before slicing into it because the internal heat contained within the loaf/roll accounts for part of the baking process.
      7. Whole meal flour can be replaced to rye or multi-grain flour to get rye or multi-grain loaf.
      8. If the dough is cut into 6-10 pcs, hard rolls or buns can be made. Bake them on trays or on silicon moulds. The baking time can be reduced in this case by 1-2 minutes in each phase.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Oven with steam, Proofer, Dough kneader, Mixing bowls, Rolling pins, Loaf tins, Baking trays, Measuring spoon set, Sieve, Baking paper, Scissors, Bread knife, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Kneading, Proofing, Shaping, Scoring, Baking.
↑ Back to top

66. White Loaf

Category: Breads | Prep: ~65–75 mins proofing | Cook/Bake: 21 mins (loaf) or ~8 mins (rolls) | Yield: 860g

Ingredients

White Dough

  • 500 g Bread Flour
  • 10 g Yeast
  • 8 g Salt
  • 320 ml Water
  • 30 g White sugar

For Dusting

  • A/R Bread Flour

Method

Mixing

  1. Sift the flour into a mixing bowl. Add the dry yeast and sugar on one side of the bowl and salt on the other side.

Kneading

  1. Mix the dry ingredients well and incorporate the water in small additions till it forms into a medium to soft dough.

1st Proofing

  1. Prove it for 45 minutes in the prover or at room temperature till the dough doubles in volume.

Knock down

  1. Knock back the dough on a clean table with a little extra flour if necessary.

Pre-shaping

  1. For a loaf, scale 500g and shape accordingly, for loaves (rolls), scale 50g portions and shape as required.
  2. Shape it into a round (boule) or long (batard) shape.

2nd Proofing

  1. Prove the dough for 10 - 15 minutes at room temperature.

Scoring

  1. Score the loaves with a bakers blade.

3rd Proofing

  1. Sift with additional flour and prove the dough for a further 10 – 15 minutes.

Baking

  1. Bake the proved loaves in 3 phases.
    • Phase 1: 220° C for 10 minutes with the first 2 minutes with steam injection.
    • Phase 2: Reduce the temperature to 180° C and bake for 8 minutes.
    • Phase 3: Continue baking at 180° C for the last 3 minutes, with the vent open.
  2. If baking 50 g rolls, bake at 200° C for 5 minutes with 1 minute steam, and at 160° C till the bread is done with the vent open for the last 3 minutes.

Cooling

  1. (Step numbered 14 in original text) Remove from the tray immediately after baking and transfer it onto a wire rack to cool down.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Loaf: Phase 1 at 220°C (428°F) for 10 mins (first 2 mins steam), Phase 2 at 180°C (356°F) for 8 mins, Phase 3 at 180°C (356°F) for 3 mins with vent open. Rolls (50 g): 200°C (392°F) for 5 mins with 1 min steam, then 160°C (320°F) until done with vent open for last 3 mins. Prover: 27–35°C (80.6–95°F).
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Medium to soft dough; proofed until doubled in volume; cooled on a wire rack before slicing.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. For commercial production of bread, the temperatures and proving times mentioned in the recipe must be followed.
      3. The Prover should be set between 27-35° C.
      4. For home baking, the temperatures mentioned have to be increased by 20° C, since home ovens are slightly weaker than industrial grade.
      5. To prove the dough without a prover, the dough has to rest until it doubles in volume. The timing may vary depending on how cold or warm the bread making environment is.
      6. It is important to cool the bread properly after baking before slicing into it because the internal heat contained within the loaf/roll accounts for part of the baking process.
      7. Whole meal flour can be replaced to rye or multi-grain flour to get rye or multi-grain loaf.
      8. If the dough is cut into 6-10 pcs, hard rolls or buns can be made. Bake them on trays or on silicon moulds. The baking time can be reduced in this case by 1-2 minutes in each phase.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Oven with steam, Proofer, Dough kneader, Mixing bowls, Rolling pins, Loaf tins, Baking trays, Measuring spoon set, Sieve, Baking paper, Scissors, Bread knife, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Kneading, Proofing, Shaping, Scoring, Baking.
↑ Back to top

67. Focaccia

Category: Breads | Prep: 85–135 mins proofing | Cook/Bake: 25 mins | Yield: 450g

Ingredients

Focaccia Dough

  • 250 g Bread Flour
  • 5 g Yeast
  • 4 g Salt
  • 8 g Sugar
  • 160 g Water
  • 30 ml Olive Oil

Toppings

  • 16.5 g Rosemary, fresh
  • 6.5 g Cherry Tomatoes
  • 3.3 g Sea salt flakes
  • 3.3 g Olive oil
  • 6.5 g Water

Oil and Water Brush

  • 100 ml Olive Oil
  • 100 ml Water

Method

Mixing

  1. Sift the flour into a mixing bowl. Add the dry yeast on one side of the bowl and salt on the other side.

Kneading

  1. Mix the dry ingredients well and incorporate the water in small additions till it forms into a medium to soft dough.
  2. Add the olive oil in and knead until smooth and the dough absorbs the oil and reaches a silky texture and passes the window test.

1st Proofing

  1. Prove it for 40 - 90 min in the prover or at room temperature till the dough doubles in volume.

Knock down

  1. Knock back the dough and place it into a lined baking tin (18 cm square frame).

Pre-shaping

  1. Flatten the dough down in to the edges of the frame and press them down until the dough is spread evenly till the edges of the frame. Use your fingers to poke deep dents (dimples) into the dough all over the surface of the dough.

Final Shaping

  1. Sprinkle the dough with the fresh toppings.

1st Fat wash

  1. Mix olive oil and water in a 1 to 1 ratio and drizzle it generously on the dough using a pastry brush.

2nd Proofing

  1. Prove the dough for the second time for about 45 minutes or until it fills the dish.

2nd Fat wash

  1. Sprinkle the oil and water wash once again and then top with sea salt flakes.

Baking

  1. Bake the Focaccia in 2 stages:
      1. First at 200° C for 5 minutes
      1. Then, at 180° C for 20 minutes

2nd Fat wash (Post-bake)

  1. Brush with olive oil after baking.

Cooling

  1. Allow the Focaccia to cool down in the dish.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Stage 1: 200°C (392°F) for 5 minutes; Stage 2: 180°C (356°F) for 20 minutes. Prover: 27–35°C (80.6–95°F).
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Kneaded until smooth, silky texture, passing the window test; 1st proof until doubled in volume; 2nd proof until it fills the dish.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. For commercial production of bread, the temperatures and proving times mentioned in the recipe must be followed.
      3. The Prover should be set between 27-35° C.
      4. For home baking, the temperatures mentioned have to be increased by 20° C, since home ovens are slightly weaker than industrial grade.
      5. To prove the dough without a prover, the dough has to rest until it doubles in volume. The timing may vary depending on how cold or warm the bread making environment is.
      6. It is important to cool the bread properly after baking before slicing into it because the internal heat contained within the loaf/roll accounts for part of the baking process.
      7. As additional toppings, chopped or whole pitted olives, chopped sundried tomato, cherry tomato whole or segments, oregano, cracked pepper can be used. These toppings have to be placed on top with the rosemary, before the second proving.
      8. Traditional Italian Focaccia is made with biga. The process is the same. Proving time might be shortened in that case.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Oven with steam, Proofer, Dough kneader, Mixing bowls, Rolling pins, Loaf tins, Baking trays, Measuring spoon set, Sieve, Baking paper, Scissors, Bread knife, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Kneading, Proofing, Shaping, Baking.
↑ Back to top

68. Grissini

Category: Breads | Prep: 45–90 mins (1st proof) + 2nd proof | Cook/Bake: 12–15 mins | Yield: 450g

Ingredients

Grissini Dough

  • 250 g Bread Flour
  • 5 g Salt
  • 22 ml Olive Oil
  • 7 g Sugar
  • 150 ml Water
  • 4 g Yeast
  • 37 g Semolina

Oil and Water Brush

  • 100 ml Olive oil
  • 100 ml Water

Toppings

  • A/R Oregano
  • A/R Cheese
  • A/R Sesame
  • A/R Sumac

Method

Mixing

  1. Sift the flour into a mixing bowl and add the semolina on top. Add the dry yeast and sugar on one side of the bowl and salt on the other side.

Kneading

  1. Mix the dry ingredients well and incorporate the water in small additions till it forms into a medium to soft dough and knead well.
  2. Knead in olive oil in and knead until smooth and the oils are absorbed and the dough reaches a silky texture and passes the window test.

1st Proofing

  1. Rest for 60 - 90 minutes at room temperature or for 45 minutes in the prover.

Knock down

  1. Knock back the dough.

Shaping

  1. Sheet the dough to ½ cm thickness and cut into ½ cm strips.

Final Shaping

  1. Sprinkle the desired toppings and cut into ½ cm thick strips.

2nd Proofing

  1. Arrange the strips slightly apart on a baking sheet and allow to prove for a second time.

Fat wash

  1. Drizzle the water and oil brush (1:1 ratio) generously on the dough using a pastry brush.

Baking

  1. Bake at 160° C in a pre-heated oven for 12-15 minutes (printed as “1215- minutes”) or till light brown and crispy.

Cooling

  1. Allow the Grissini to cool down a little before serving.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 160°C (320°F). Prover: 27–35°C (80.6–95°F).
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Kneaded until silky texture and passes the window test; baked until light brown and crispy.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. For commercial production of bread, the temperatures and proving times mentioned in the recipe must be followed.
      3. The Prover should be set between 27-35° C.
      4. For home baking, the temperatures mentioned have to be increased by 20° C, since home ovens are slightly weaker than industrial grade.
      5. To prove the dough without a prover, the dough has to rest until it doubles in volume. The timing may vary depending on how cold or warm the bread making environment is.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Oven with steam, Proofer, Dough kneader, Mixing bowls, Rolling pins, Loaf tins, Baking trays, Measuring spoon set, Sieve, Baking paper, Scissors, Bread knife, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Kneading, Proofing, Shaping, Baking.

69. Lavash

Category: Breads | Prep: 15–20 mins resting | Cook/Bake: 6–8 mins | Yield: 410g

Ingredients

Lavash Dough

  • 125 g Bread Flour
  • 125 g All Purpose Flour
  • 60 g Egg
  • 85 ml Water
  • 6 g Salt
  • 15 g Oil
  • A/R Oil (for spreading)

Toppings

  • A/R White sesame seeds
  • A/R Black sesame seeds

Method

Mixing

  1. Sift the flour into a mixing bowl and add in the salt.

Kneading

  1. Mix the dry ingredients well and incorporate the water, egg and oil in small additions till it forms into a medium to soft dough and passes the window test.

Resting

  1. Rest the dough for 15 - 20 minutes.

Pre-shaping

  1. Spread the dough out into a thin sheet of about 2mm in thickness on a well oil baking sheet.

Final Shaping

  1. Drizzle with oil and water brush and sprinkle desired toppings.
  2. Sprinkle some sesame seeds or spices if desired.

Baking

  1. Bake in a pre-heated oven at 160° C for 6- 8 minutes till completely dry.

Cooling

  1. Allow the lavash to cool down a little before taking it off the baking trays and serve.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 160°C (320°F). Prover: 27–35°C (80.6–95°F).
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Kneaded until medium to soft dough that passes the window test; spread to 2 mm thin sheet; baked for 6–8 minutes till completely dry.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. For commercial production of bread, the temperatures and proving times mentioned in the recipe must be followed.
      3. The Prover should be set between 27-35° C.
      4. For home baking, the temperatures mentioned have to be increased by 20° C, since home ovens are slightly weaker than industrial grade.
      5. To prove the dough without a prover, the dough has to rest until it doubles in volume. The timing may vary depending on how cold or warm the bread making environment is.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Oven with steam, Proofer, Dough kneader, Mixing bowls, Rolling pins, Loaf tins, Baking trays, Measuring spoon set, Sieve, Baking paper, Scissors, Bread knife, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Kneading, Proofing, Shaping, Baking.

70. Pizza

Category: Savory / Breads | Prep: 45 mins in prover (or 3 days in chiller) | Cook/Bake: 4–5 mins (7–8 mins for Calzone) | Yield: 2 – 4 portions

Ingredients

Pizza Dough

  • 250 g Bread Flour
  • 5 g Yeast
  • 4 g Salt
  • 150 ml Water
  • 15 ml Olive Oil
  • A/R Semolina (for dusting)

Pomodoro Sauce

  • 500 g Tomato
  • 6 ea Garlic (chopped)
  • 25 g Onion (chopped)
  • 25 ml Olive oil
  • 5 ea Basil leaves
  • A/R Salt

Method

Pre-prep

  1. Preheat the deck oven to 300° C or the convection oven to 270° C with the heavy trays inside.

Mixing

  1. Sift the bread flour into a mixing bowl. Add the dry yeast and sugar on one side of the bowl and salt on the other side.

Kneading

  1. Mix the dry ingredients well and incorporate the water, followed by the olive oil, in small additions, till it forms into a soft dough and passes the window test.

1st Proofing

  1. Prove the dough for 45 minutes at 30° C in the prover OR prove for 3 days in the chiller for extra crisp texture and flavour.

Pomodoro Sauce

  1. Blanch the tomatoes (place tomatoes in boiling water, let the water come to a boil – remove the tomatoes & put in cold water and remove the skin).
  2. Blend the peeled tomatoes.
  3. Heat oil in a saucepan, add onion, garlic, sweat, add tomato puree & simmer to get the right consistency, then add in the basil leaves.
  4. Adjust seasoning as required.

Shaping

  1. Roll out the pizza dough to ½ cm thickness and spread a thin layer of Pomodoro sauce and top with desired toppings.

Baking

  1. Bake at 250° C - 280° C in a well preheated oven for 4 - 5 minutes. Brush the edges with olive oil and sprinkle some oregano, when done.
  2. The base of the pizza must be crisp; hence the pizza must be baked on the base of the oven or on a preheated griddle.

Serving

  1. Cut with a pizza cutter and serve while hot.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Preheat deck oven to 300°C (572°F) or convection oven to 270°C (518°F) with heavy trays inside; bake pizza at 250°C–280°C (482°F–536°F) for 4–5 minutes; Calzone at 250°C (482°F) in convection oven for 7–8 minutes. Prover: 30°C (86°F) (general prover range 27–35°C / 80.6–95°F).
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Soft dough that passes the window test; base of the pizza must be crisp.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Dough can be proved for 3 days in the chiller for extra crisp texture and flavour.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Pizza Variations Table:
      • Margherita: tomato sauce + mozzarella + oregano
      • Seafood: tomato sauce + mozzarella + seafood (prawn, squid, fish etc)+vegetables optional (pepper, mushroom etc)
      • Mexican: tomato sauce + mozzarella + pepper + jalapeño + olives + tomato slice
      • Pepperoni: tomato sauce + mozzarella + lamb pepperoni
      • Supreme: tomato sauce + mozzarella + chicken mortadela, sausage, Chicken fillet
      • four seasons: 4 different toppings on the same pizza.
      • Calzone: Folded pizza with tomato sauce + mozzarella + any filling.
      • Stromboli: Stuffed and rolled pizza with various fillings that include cheese, tomato sauce and cold cuts or vegetables
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. For commercial production of bread, the temperatures and proving times mentioned in the recipe must be followed.
      3. The Prover should be set between 27-35° C.
      4. For home baking, the temperatures mentioned have to be increased by 20° C, since home ovens are slightly weaker than industrial grade.
      5. To prove the dough without a prover, the dough has to rest until it doubles in volume. The timing may vary depending on how cold or warm the bread making environment is.
      6. Pizza can be made from the indirect method of the focaccia dough, as another option.
      7. The working out of the dough is the same.
      8. The third option to make pizza dough in the long fermented version.
      9. For calzone, do not prebake the dough. When all the topping on, turn it half to a half round shape and press the sides together. Bake it for 7-8 minutes on 250°C in the convection oven on heavy trays.
      10. For ratatouille topping, chop all vegetables to uniform size and cook it with olive oil for 7-8 minutes on high temperature, so it won’t make the pizza dough soggy.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Oven, Proofer, Dough kneader, Weighing scales, Chiller, Mixing bowls, Baking trays, Measuring spoon set, Sieve, Baking paper, Scissors, Pizza cutter, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Kneading, Resting, Baking.
↑ Back to top

71. Doughnuts

Category: Viennoiserie / Sweet Yeasted Dough | Prep: 45–50 mins (1st proof) + 30–45 mins (2nd proof) | Cook/Bake: ~1–2 mins per side (deep frying) | Yield: 460 g; 12 – 15 pieces

Ingredients

Doughnut Dough

  • 250 g Bread Flour
  • 6 g Yeast
  • 3 g Salt
  • 37 g Sugar
  • 100 ml Milk
  • 25 g Butter
  • 20 g Egg Yolk
  • 25 g Whole egg
  • 2 ml Vanilla
  • 1.5 L Oil (for frying)

Finishing

  • A/R Dark Chocolate, Compound
  • A/R White Chocolate, Compound

Method

Mixing

  1. Sift the flour into a mixing bowl and add the dry yeast and sugar on one side of the bowl and salt on the other side.

Kneading

  1. Mix the dry ingredients well and incorporate the vanilla, eggs and milk in small additions till it forms into a soft silky dough and passes the window test.

1st Proofing

  1. Prove the dough in the prover for 45 - 50 minutes or at room temperature till the dough doubles in volume.

1st Proofing (Knock down)

  1. Knock back the dough.

Pre-shaping

  1. Roll to 1 cm thickness and cut round discs or rings.

2nd Proofing

  1. Place them on gently floured surface, cover them and allow them to prove for another 30 - 45 minutes in the prover or at room temperature until the dough doubles in volume.

Deep Frying

  1. Heat oil up to 140 - 145° C in a large saucepan.
  2. Place 3 - 4 doughnuts in the oil 1 by 1 next to each other and cover them.
  3. Cook them on low heat for 1-2 minutes (printed as “12- minutes”), until grow to about double size and the bottom side is golden brown.
  4. Flip them over and continue frying them till they are golden brown.
  5. Take them out from the oil and transfer them to a wire rack or tissue paper.

Finishing

  1. Rings can be dipped in caster sugar while they are still hot, or dipped in melted compound chocolate.
  2. Alternatively, the doughnuts are cooled down and dusted with icing sugar or glazed with sugar icing made from icing sugar, vanilla and as much milk as need to make a thick glaze.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Deep-frying oil at 140–145°C (284–293°F). Prover: 27–35°C (80.6–95°F).
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Soft silky dough passing the window test; fried until grown to about double size and golden brown on both sides with an identical white ring in the middle.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. For commercial production of bread, the temperatures and proving times mentioned in the recipe must be followed.
      3. The Prover should be set between 27-35° C.
      4. For home baking, the temperatures mentioned have to be increased by 20° C, since home ovens are slightly weaker than industrial grade.
      5. To prove the dough without a prover, the dough has to rest until it doubles in volume. The timing may vary depending on how cold or warm the bread making environment is.
      6. Temperature of the deep-frying oil is critical. Deep frying the products in too hot oil results too dark and may be row (raw) product.
      7. Right temperature will result light/golden brown and baked through product.
      8. Well proved doughnut will have an identical white ring in the middle after baking, while under or over proved product has flat appearance and even colour.
      9. There are many different types of Doughnuts. The above-mentioned recipe is Yeast Doughnut.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Deep saucepan, Proofer, Dough kneader, Mixing bowls, Rolling pins, Spider, Baking trays, Measuring spoon set, Sieve, Baking paper, Scissors.
    • Key Learning: Kneading, Proofing, Shaping, Deep Frying.
↑ Back to top

72. Soft Rolls

Category: Breads / Enriched Dough | Prep: 45 mins (1st proof) + 15–30 mins (2nd proof) + 15–30 mins (final proof) | Cook/Bake: 8–10 mins | Yield: 450 g; 18 – 20 pieces

Ingredients

Bun Dough

  • 250 g Bread Flour
  • 5 g Yeast
  • 4 g Salt
  • 20 g Sugar
  • 100 ml Milk
  • 30 g Butter
  • 50 g Whole egg

Egg Wash

  • 50 g Whole Egg
  • 20 g Egg yolk
  • 50 g Cream
  • a pinch Salt

Toppings

  • A/R Sesame seeds

Method

Mixing

  1. Sift the flour into a mixing bowl. Add the dry yeast and sugar on one side of the bowl and salt on the other side. (Note: Step 1 is printed twice on the page)

Kneading

  1. Mix the dry ingredients well and incorporate the milk and eggs in small additions till it forms into a soft silky dough and knead well.
  2. Add butter and knead till the dough reaches a smooth consistency and passes the window test.

1st Proofing

  1. Prove the dough in the prover for 45 minutes or at room temperature till the dough doubles in volume.

Knock down

  1. Knock back the dough.

Pre-shaping and Portioning

  1. Cut and portion the dough into 40 g portions.
  2. Make a smooth dough ball of each of the 40 g portions.

2nd Proofing

  1. Cover with clingfilm and allow the dough balls to rest in room temperature till it doubles in volume for abound (around) 15 – 30 minutes.

Final Shaping

  1. Shape each of the dough balls into its final shape.

1st Egg Wash

  1. Brush the surface of the shaped dough with egg wash.

Final Proofing

  1. Prove the shaped doughs for the last time for another 15 - 30 minutes in the prover or at room temperature till the dough doubles in volume.

2nd Egg Wash

  1. Egg Wash the shaped doughs again and sprinkle with sesame seeds if desired.

Baking

  1. Bake at 160° C for 8 - 10 minutes.

Fat Wash

  1. Remove from oven and immediately brush with butter.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 160°C (320°F) for 8–10 minutes. Prover: 27–35°C (80.6–95°F).
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Kneaded until smooth consistency passing the window test; proofed until doubled in volume at each stage; brushed immediately with butter after baking.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. For commercial production of bread, the temperatures and proving times mentioned in the recipe must be followed.
      3. The Prover should be set between 27-35° C.
      4. For home baking, the temperatures mentioned have to be increased by 20° C, since home ovens are slightly weaker than industrial grade.
      5. To prove the dough without a prover, the dough has to rest until it doubles in volume. The timing may vary depending on how cold or warm the bread making environment is.
      6. Bun dough is versatile enriched dough, used for many of products, like burger buns, soft roll, cinnamon roll, Chelsea bun, hot cross bun, Swiss buns, sandwich bread, etc.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Oven, Proofer, Dough kneader, Chiller, Mixing bowls, Baking trays, Measuring spoon set, Sieve, Baking paper, Scissors, Pastry brush.
    • Key Learning: Kneading, Proofing, Shaping, Baking.
↑ Back to top

73. Cinnamon Rolls

Category: Viennoiserie / Enriched Breads | Prep: 45 mins (1st proof) + 15–25 mins (2nd proof) | Cook/Bake: 6–8 mins (individual) or 20–25 mins (in a frame/ring) + 8–10 mins (pecan roasting) | Yield: 650 g; 20 – 25 pieces

Ingredients

Bun Dough

  • 250 g Bread Flour
  • 5 g Yeast
  • 3 g Salt
  • 15 g Sugar
  • 100 ml Milk
  • 15 g Butter
  • 50 g Whole egg

Filling

  • 56 g Unsalted butter
  • 50 g Light brown sugar
  • 7.5 g Cinnamon powder
  • 2.5 ml Vanilla

Chocolate Filling

  • 55 g Dark Chocolate 55%
  • 45 g Butter
  • 50 g Eggs
  • 45 g Sugar
  • 30 g Light Brown Sugar
  • 40 g All Purpose Flour
  • 20 g Cocoa Powder
  • 1 g Salt

Vanilla Glaze

  • 50 g Cream
  • 75 g Confectioner’s Sugar
  • 2.5 ml Vanilla Extract
  • 12.5 g Light corn syrup
  • 20 g Butter
  • 1 g Salt

Pecan Topping

  • 100 g Roasted Pecan
  • 80 g Dark Brown Sugar
  • 40 g Butter
  • 100 g Cream
  • A pinch Salt

Method

Mixing

  1. Sift the flour into a mixing bowl and add the dry yeast and sugar on one side of the bowl and salt on the other side.

Kneading

  1. Mix the dry ingredients well and incorporate the milk and eggs in small additions till it forms into a soft silky dough and knead well.
  2. Add room temperature butter and knead till the dough reaches a smooth consistency and passes the window test.

1st Proofing

  1. Prove the dough in the prover for 45 minutes or at room temperature till the dough doubles in volume.

Knock down

  1. Knock back the dough.

Chocolate Filling

  1. In a medium bowl, over bain marie, melt the dark chocolate and the butter and set aside. Sift the flour and cocoa powder and mix into the melted chocolate and butter mixture. Add the eggs, sugar, light brown sugar and salt and make a thick, almost smooth paste.

Pre-shaping and Portioning

  1. Sheet the dough into a rectangle to about 5 - 6 mm in thickness.
  2. Spread the butter and cinnamon mix on the sheeted dough, using an offset palate knife.
  3. Tightly roll the dough up, starting from the longer side and place the rolled up dough, seam side down.
  4. Cut into even sections with a serrated knife.
  5. Place them on a baking tray at least one inch apart from each other or in individual aluminium cups (or place them closely next to each other into cake tin or frame).

2nd Proofing

  1. Prove the prepared filled rolls in the prover for 15 – 25 minutes or at room temperature till the dough doubles in volume.

Baking

  1. Bake the individual cinnamon rolls at 160° C for 6 - 8 minutes and the rolls set together in a frame/ring at 160° C for 20 - 25 minutes.

Vanilla Glaze

  1. In a medium bowl, mix the confectioner’s sugar, vanilla extract, light corn syrup and salt together. Add the cream in slowly and whisk until the cream is well incorporated. Lastly add the room temperature butter and mix well.

Pecan Topping

  1. Roast the pecans until fragrant at 150° C for about 8 - 10 minutes. Allow the pecans to cool a little and chop them up. In a saucepan, boil the cream, butter, dark brown sugar, salt and chopped roasted pecans till the sugar is melted and the topping is of a thick homogenous consistency.

Finishing

  1. Glaze the top of the cinnamon rolls with the desired toppings when the rolls cool down to room temperature.

Cooling

  1. Allow the cinnamon rolls to cool down a little and serve warm.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 160°C (320°F) for 6–8 minutes (individual) or 20–25 minutes (in a frame/ring); 150°C (302°F) for 8–10 minutes to roast pecans. Prover: 27–35°C (80.6–95°F).
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Dough kneaded to smooth consistency passing the window test; chocolate filling forms a thick, almost smooth paste; pecan topping boiled to a thick homogenous consistency.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. For commercial production of bread, the temperatures and proving times mentioned in the recipe must be followed.
      3. The Prover should be set between 27-35° C.
      4. For home baking, the temperatures mentioned have to be increased by 20° C, since home ovens are slightly weaker than industrial grade.
      5. To prove the dough without a prover, the dough has to rest until it doubles in volume. The timing may vary depending on how cold or warm the bread making environment is.
      6. Bun dough is versatile enriched dough, used for many of products, like burger buns, soft roll, cinnamon roll, Chelsea bun, hot cross bun, Swiss buns, sandwich bread, etc.
      7. For Chelsea buns; sultanas and currants are used instead of the sugar and cinnamon.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Oven, Proofer, Offset palate knife, Rolling Pin, Serrated knife, Chiller, Mixing bowls, Baking trays, Measuring spoon set, Sieve, Baking paper, Scissors.
    • Key Learning: Kneading, Proofing, Shaping, Baking.
↑ Back to top

74. French Brioche

Category: Viennoiserie / Enriched Breads | Prep: 45 mins or 1–2 hrs chiller (1st proof) + 30–40 mins (2nd proof) + 35–45 mins (final proof) | Cook/Bake: 10–12 mins | Yield: 500 g; 10 – 12 pieces

Ingredients

Brioche Dough

  • 250 g Bread Flour
  • 5 g Yeast
  • 4 g Salt
  • 35 g Sugar
  • 40 g Milk
  • 100 g Butter
  • 50 g Whole egg

Egg Wash

  • 50 g Whole Egg
  • 20 g Egg yolk
  • 50 g Cream
  • a pinch Salt

Topping

  • A/R Sugar Pearls

Method

Mixing

  1. Sift the flour into a mixing bowl and add the dry yeast and sugar on one side of the bowl and salt on the other side.

Kneading

  1. Mix the dry ingredients well and incorporate the cold milk and egg in small additions till it forms into a soft silky dough and knead well.
  2. Add room temperature butter and knead till the dough reaches a smooth consistency and passes the window test.

1st Proofing

  1. Prove the dough in the prover for 45 minutes or at room temperature till the dough doubles in volume, or for 1 – 2 hours in the chiller.

Knock down

  1. Knock back the dough.

Pre-shaping and Portioning

  1. Measure and cut the dough into 40 g portions.
  2. Make each 40 g portion a smooth and round sphere by cupping your hand and putting enough pressure on the dough to press against the work surface and make a smooth dough ball.

2nd Proofing

  1. Prove the dough balls at room temperature for 30 - 40 minutes or till the dough doubles in volume.

Final Shaping

  1. Divide the dough ball into 2 parts, one part being 1/3 rd of the dough while the other part is 2/3 rd of the dough.
  2. With the larger piece of dough, make a ring and set aside.
  3. With the smaller part of the dough, make a small sphere and taper off one end.
  4. Place the smaller piece of dough (tapered side down) within the ring made of the larger piece of dough and seal the two pieces together with your fingertips.
  5. Place them into brioche molds or on tray.

1st Egg Wash

  1. Brush the prepared and shaped doughs with egg wash using a pastry brush.

Final Proofing

  1. Prove the shaped doughs for 35 - 45 minutes in a prover or at room temperature until the dough doubles in volume.

2nd Egg Wash

  1. Brush the prepared and shaped doughs with egg wash once again and sprinkle with rock sugar if desired (completely optional).

Baking

  1. Bake at 170° C for 10 - 12 minutes or until golden brown with no fan.

Fat Wash

  1. Brush the freshly baked brioche generously with butter while they are still hot.

Cooling

  1. Allow the brioche to cool slightly and serve warm.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 170°C (338°F) for 10–12 minutes with no fan. Prover: 27–35°C (80.6–95°F).
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Soft silky dough kneaded with butter until smooth and passing the window test; baked until golden brown with no fan; brushed generously with butter while hot.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. For commercial production of bread, the temperatures and proving times mentioned in the recipe must be followed.
      3. The Prover should be set between 27-35° C.
      4. For home baking, the temperatures mentioned have to be increased by 20° C, since home ovens are slightly weaker than industrial grade.
      5. To prove the dough without a prover, the dough has to rest until it doubles in volume. The timing may vary depending on how cold or warm the bread making environment is.
      6. Tropezian tart is a multi-portion brioche loaf shaped round and baked without tin, so it becomes flat and round after baking. The top is glazed with egg wash and sprinkled with rock sugar before baking. It is cut half and filled with cream patisserie or cream diplomat.
      7. For Brioche loafs, place the shaped dough into lined loaf or other tins. They can be glazed twice (once before 2nd proving, once after). Proving and baking time is longer.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Oven, Proofer, Dough kneader, Chiller, Mixing bowls, Brioche Mould, Baking trays, Measuring spoon set, Sieve, Baking paper, Scissors, Pastry Brush.
    • Key Learning: Kneading, Proofing, Shaping, Baking.
↑ Back to top

75. Savarin

Category: Hot & Cold Desserts / Breads | Prep: 30–40 mins (proofing) | Cook/Bake: 15 mins (baking), 20–25 secs (soaking) | Yield: 620 g; 18 – 20 pieces

Ingredients

Savarin Dough

  • 250 g All purpose flour
  • 8 g Yeast
  • 6 g Salt
  • 25 g Sugar
  • 125 - 160 ml Milk
  • 125 g Butter
  • 100 g Whole egg

Stock Syrup

  • 1500 ml Water
  • 480 g Sugar
  • 2 pcs Orange slice (with peel)
  • 2 pcs Cinnamon sticks
  • 2 pcs Star Anise
  • 10 ml Vanilla Extract

Cream Chantilly

  • 150 ml Whipping cream
  • 15 g Icing sugar
  • 5 g Vanilla

Assorted Fruits

  • A/R Strawberry
  • A/R Kiwi
  • A/R Blueberry

Method

Pre-prep

  1. Brush the savarin molds with butter and dust with a light coat of flour.

Mixing

  1. In a medium sized bowl, sift the flour into a mixing bowl and add the dry yeast and sugar on one side of the bowl and salt on the other side.

Kneading

  1. Add in the milk and eggs and mix with a spatula until the mixture is smooth. It will be soft, almost runny, in consistency.
  2. Add room temperature butter and mix it in well.
  3. Transfer the dough into a piping bag.

Pre-shaping and Portioning

  1. Pipe the dough into the pre prepared savarin molds and cut the excess dough per mold with the help of scissors.
  2. Smoothen the tops of each savarin with your fingertips using a little water.

Proofing

  1. Prove the piped dough in the molds for 30 – 40 minutes in the prover or at room temperature till the dough rises a little above the level of the mold.

Baking

  1. Bake at 160°C (320°F) for 15 minutes. Take them out from the molds while they are still warm.

Stock Syrup

  1. In a medium sized pot, combine all the ingredients required for the stock syrup and simmer together till all the sugar has dissolved.
  2. Soak savarins in simmering stock syrup before presentation (20-25 seconds).

Cream Chantilly

  1. In a medium sized bowl, whip the cream, icing sugar and vanilla to medium to stiff peak and reserve in a piping bag with a star nozzle.

Finishing

  1. Serve the soaked savarins in a deep dish with fresh berries and cream chantilly.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 160°C (320°F) for 15 minutes; Prover set between 27–35°C (81–95°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Dough is soft, almost runny in consistency; proofed till dough rises a little above the level of the mold; Cream Chantilly whipped to medium to stiff peak
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Alternatively, they can be placed into cold syrup and refrigerated; this way they can be held in the syrup for 2-3 days (they need to be flipped over once or twice a day)
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Oven, Proofer, Dough kneader, Chiller, Mixing bowls, Savarin Molds, Piping Bags, Baking trays, Measuring spoon set, Sieve, Baking paper, Scissors, Pastry brush.
    • Key Learning: Kneading, Proofing, Baking, Soaking.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. For commercial production of bread, the temperatures and proving times mentioned in the recipe must be followed.
      3. The Prover should be set between 27-35°C.
      4. For home baking, the temperatures mentioned have to be increased by 20°C, since home ovens are slightly weaker than industrial grade.
      5. To prove the dough without a prover, the dough has to rest until it doubles in volume. The timing may vary depending on how cold or warm the bread making environment is.
      6. When making the stock syrup, part of the water can be replaced with lemon, passion fruit or any other fruit purees.
      7. Alternatively, they can be placed into cold syrup and refrigerated. This way they can be held in the syrup for 2-3 days. They need to be flipped over once or twice a day.
      8. Most common version of Savarin/ Babais Baba au Rhum/ Rhum baba, is when the dough is soaked in sugar syrup flavoured with dark rum.
