76. English Muffins

Category: Breads | Prep: 75–100 mins (proofing) | Cook/Bake: ~14 mins (7 mins per side) | Yield: 450 g; 10-12 pcs

Ingredients

Muffin Dough

  • 250 g All Purpose Flour
  • 5 g Sugar
  • 5 g Yeast
  • 30 g Butter (melted)
  • 150 ml Milk
  • 2 g Salt

Dusting

  • A/R Semolina

Method

Mixing

  1. Sift the flour into a mixing bowl and add in the salt, sugar and yeast.

Kneading

  1. Mix the dry ingredients well and incorporate the milk in small additions till it forms into a medium to soft dough and knead well.
  2. Add the melted butter and knead it in. The dough must pass the window test.

1st Proofing

  1. Rest the dough for 45 - 60 minutes in the prover or until the dough doubles in volume at room temperature.

Knock down

  1. Knock back the dough.

Pre-shaping and Portioning

  1. Sheet the dough to 1/2 inch thickness.
  2. Cut round discs with biscuit cutter, dab the surface with a little water and place them on tray dusted with semolina.

2nd Proofing

  1. Prove the cut and dusted disks in a prover for 30 – 40 minutes or at room temperature till the disks double in volume.

Cooking

  1. Heat up a sauté pan and cook the muffins for about 7 minutes on low heat on each side or until golden brown on the surface and still white on the sides – with the lid closed.

Cooling

  1. Transfer on to a wire rack to cool.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Low heat in a covered sauté pan (about 7 minutes per side); Prover set between 27–35°C (81–95°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Medium to soft dough passing the window test; golden brown on the surface and still white on the sides
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Oven, Proofer, Dough kneader, Chiller, Mixing bowls, Sauté Pan and lid, Measuring spoon set, Sieve, Baking paper, Scissors.
    • Key Learning: Kneading, Proofing, Shaping.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. For commercial production of bread, the temperatures and proving times mentioned in the recipe must be followed.
      3. The Prover should be set between 27-35°C.
      4. For home baking, the temperatures mentioned have to be increased by 20°C, since home ovens are slightly weaker than industrial grade.
      5. To prove the dough without a prover, the dough has to rest until it doubles in volume. The timing may vary depending on how cold or warm the bread making environment is.
      6. English muffins are classically used as the base for eggs benedict and eggs florentine, popular breakfast dishes.

77. Leek and Cheese Muffins

Category: Savory / Breads | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 12–15 mins | Yield: 650 g; 8-12 pcs

Ingredients

  • 150 g Eggs
  • 140 g All purpose flour
  • 2.5 g Baking Powder
  • 60 g Olive Oil
  • 100 ml Milk
  • 100 g Cheese (blue cheese + cheddar + Emmental + parmesan)
  • 100 g Leeks
  • 1 tsp Sage
  • 1 tsp Thyme
  • A/R Salt

Method

Pre-prep

  1. Pre heat the oven to 160°C (320°F).

Mixing

  1. Sift the flour and baking powder into a mixing bowl and add in the salt.
  2. Add in the spices, chopped leek and grated cheese and mix well.
  3. Slowly incorporate the wet ingredients into the dry mix. Mix in the eggs, milk and olive oil till it forms a homogenous thick batter.
  4. Portion the batter into muffin tins lined with paper cups.

Portioning

  1. Bake at 180°C (356°F) for 12-15 minutes or until a toothpick inserted in the center of the muffin comes out clean.

Cooling

  1. Transfer on to a wire rack to cool.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Preheat to 160°C (320°F); Bake at 180°C (356°F) for 12–15 minutes
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Homogenous thick batter; baked until a toothpick inserted in the center of the muffin comes out clean
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Sieve, Muffin tin / Frame, Baking paper, Scissors, Hand whisk, Rubber spatula, Strainer, Green Chef knife, Green Chopping board, Baking tray, Bamboo skewer, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Sieving, All in method, Baking.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. For commercial production of bread, the temperatures and proving times mentioned in the recipe must be followed.
      3. For home baking, the temperatures mentioned have to be increased by 20°C, since home ovens are slightly weaker than industrial grade.

78. Cornbread

Category: Savory / Breads | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 12–15 mins | Yield: 400 g; 8-12 pcs

Ingredients

  • 60 g Cornmeal
  • 75 g All purpose flour
  • 8 g Baking Powder
  • 1/4 tsp Salt
  • 35 g Sugar
  • 120 g Milk
  • 50 g Egg
  • 15 g Butter
  • 50 g Sweet corn

Method

Pre-prep

  1. Pre heat the oven to 160°C (320°F).
  2. Line a 18 x 6-inch loaf tin/frame with baking paper.

Mixing

  1. Sift the flour and baking powder into a mixing bowl and add sugar, corn meal, sweet corn, and salt.
  2. Slowly incorporate the wet ingredients into the dry mix. Mix in the eggs, milk and melted butter till it forms a homogenous thick batter.

Portioning

  1. Pour the batter into the prepared frame/tin.

Baking

  1. Bake at 160°C (320°F) for 12-15 minutes or until a toothpick inserted in the center of the muffin comes out clean.

Cooling

  1. Let it rest for 20 minutes before cutting.
  2. Transfer on to a wire rack to cool.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Homogenous thick batter; baked until a toothpick inserted in the center comes out clean

79. Scones

Category: Breads / Bakery | Prep: 60 mins (resting in fridge) | Cook/Bake: 10–12 mins | Yield: 540 g; 8-10 pcs

Ingredients

Scones Dough

  • 250 g All purpose flour
  • 12.5 g Baking Powder
  • 62 g Butter
  • 62 g Sugar
  • 100 ml Milk

Add Ins

  • 2.5 g Vanilla
  • 50 g Raisins
  • OR
  • 2.5 g Lemon Zest
  • 50 g Cranberries

Egg Wash

  • 40 g Egg yolk

Method

Mixing

  1. Sift the flour and baking powder into a mixing bowl and add sugar and salt.
  2. Chop cold butter and add it to the dry ingredients.
  3. Mix it with the paddle attachment in a stand mixer until the dough reaches a crumbly, sandy texture.
  4. If making the dough by hands, using the “rub in” technique incorporate the cold butter into the dry ingredients to form a crumbly, sandy texture.
  5. Add in the milk and vanilla and bring the dough together, taking care not to over-knead it.
  6. If currants or raisins are being used, add it in the end, just before the dough comes together.

Refrigerate

  1. Cover or wrap it into plastic wrap and allow the dough to rest in the fridge for 60 minutes.

Portioning

  1. Roll out the dough on gently floured surface about 10-15 mm thick.
  2. Cut it with round cutters and place them on tray upside down.

Egg wash

  1. Egg wash the surface, making sure the egg does not drop on the edges.

Baking

  1. Bake at 160°C (320°F) for 10-12 minutes.

Finishing

  1. Serve with jam and clotted cream or whipped cream.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 160°C (320°F) for 10–12 minutes
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Butter and dry ingredients mixed to a crumbly, sandy texture; rolled 10–15 mm thick; placed upside down on tray; egg washed without egg dropping on the edges
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Dough rests in fridge for 60 minutes before rolling
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Sieve, Baking tray, Baking paper, Oven mitts/gloves, Rolling pin, Round cutters, Pastry Brush.
    • Key Learning: Sieving, Rub in method, Baking.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. For commercial production of bread, the temperatures and proving times mentioned in the recipe must be followed.
      3. For home baking, the temperatures mentioned have to be increased by 20°C, since home ovens are slightly weaker than industrial grade.

80. Tawa Naan

Category: Breads | Prep: 30–40 mins (proofing) | Cook/Bake: A few mins | Yield: 355 g

Ingredients

Naan Dough

  • 250 g All purpose flour
  • 5 g Yeast
  • 2.5 g Salt
  • 5 g Sugar
  • 30 g Yoghurt
  • 80 – 100 g Water (lukewarm)
  • 15 g Butter

Finishing

  • A/R Butter/Ghee
  • A/R Coriander
  • A/R Sea Salt

Method

Mixing

  1. Sift the flour and yeast into a mixing bowl and add sugar and salt.
  2. Add in the yoghurt and melted butter and incorporate the wet ingredients into the dry.
  3. Add water gradually and make a soft dough. Knead the dough well on a lightly floured work surface.
  4. Brush the top on the dough with oil and cover it.

Proofing

  1. Rest the dough for 30-40 minutes in the prover or until the dough doubles in volume at room temperature.

Pre-shaping and Portioning

  1. Divide into 8 pieces and make them into round dough balls.
  2. Roll them into thin discs, using flour on the work surface to avoid the dough from sticking.

Cooking

  1. Cook both sides of the rolled out dough on a hot tawa for a few minutes.

Finishing

  1. Spread some butter on top and sprinkle with chopped coriander.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Cooked on a hot tawa for a few minutes per side
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Soft dough, proofed until doubled in volume, rolled into thin discs
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Sieve, Tawa, Baking paper, Baking tray, Oven mitts/gloves, Rolling pin, Pastry Brush.
    • Key Learning: Sieving, Kneading, Proofing, Baking.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. For commercial production of bread, the temperatures and proving times mentioned in the recipe must be followed.
      3. For home baking, the temperatures mentioned have to be increased by 20°C, since home ovens are slightly weaker than industrial grade.

81. Paratha (Warqi)

Category: Breads | Prep: 20–30 mins (proofing) | Cook/Bake: N/A | Yield: 355 g

Ingredients

Paratha Dough

  • 250 g All purpose flour
  • 0.5 g Baking Powder
  • 2.5 g Salt
  • 2.5 g Sugar
  • 25 g Yoghurt
  • 25 g Milk
  • 25 g Egg
  • 90 g Water
  • 15 g Vegetable Oil

Spreading

  • A/R Flour
  • A/R Butter/Ghee

Whilst Cooking

  • A/R Vegetable Oil

Method

Mixing

  1. Sift the flour into a mixing bowl and add sugar, salt and baking powder.
  2. Slowly incorporate the wet ingredients into the dry mix. Add in the yoghurt, milk, water and eggs and knead well to form a smooth dough.
  3. Lastly, add in the oil and knead the dough until all the oil absorbs completely.

Pre-shaping and Portioning

  1. Divide into 6 pieces and make them into round dough balls.
  2. Roll them into a large rectangular shape and brush with melted butter.
  3. Fold it on top of itself, folding about 1-inch pieces at one time, like a paper fan.
  4. Roll the folded stack into a circle and cover it.

Proofing

  1. Prove the pre shaped doughs for 20 – 30 minutes in a prover or at room temperature till the dough ball double in size.

Final Shaping

  1. Roll out into a round shape and brush it with butter and sprinkle with flour.
  2. Fold it in half, brush the top with butter and sprinkle with flour and fold it in half again.
  3. Roll out the quarters to about 5 mm thick triangular shape pieces.

Cooking

  1. Cook the rolled out parathas on a heated tawa/crepe pan using oil.
  2. Once they are cooked and are still hot, crush the flatbread between your hands to open up the layers.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Heated tawa/crepe pan; Prover set between 27–35°C (81–95°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Smooth dough with oil absorbed completely; rolled to 5 mm thick triangular pieces; crushed while still hot to open up flaky layers
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Sieve, Tawa / Saute pan, Rubber Spatula, Tongs, Baking Tray, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Sieving, Kneading, Proofing.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. For commercial production of bread, the temperatures and proving times mentioned in the recipe must be followed.
      3. The Prover should be set between 27-35°C.
      4. For home baking, the temperatures mentioned have to be increased by 20°C, since home ovens are slightly weaker than industrial grade.
      5. To prove the dough without a prover, the dough has to rest until it doubles in volume. The timing may vary depending on how cold or warm the bread making environment is.

82. Fruit Fritters

Category: Hot & Cold Desserts | Prep: 30 mins (batter resting) | Cook/Bake: N/A | Yield: 6 - 10 portions

Ingredients

Main

  • 200 g Fresh fruit
  • A/R Corn flour
  • 150-200 g Fritter batter
  • A/R Oil (deep frying)
  • A/R Caramel/toffee sauce
  • A/R Dusting sugar

Fritter Batter with Baking Powder

  • 50 g Corn flour
  • 25 g AP flour
  • 1 tsp / 5 g Sugar
  • 1/8 tsp / 0.5 g Salt
  • 1/4 tsp / 1 g Baking powder
  • 50 g Egg
  • 55 g Milk
  • 5 g Oil
  • 1/4 tsp / 1 g Vanilla extract

Fritter Batter with Egg Whites

  • 125 g AP Flour
  • 1 tsp / 4 g Sugar
  • 1/4 tsp / 1 g Salt
  • 155 g Milk
  • 18 g Egg yolk
  • 15 g Oil
  • 30 g Egg white

Toffee Sauce (Note: Printed ingredient list repeats main assembly items under Toffee Sauce heading)

  • 200 g Fresh fruit
  • A/R Corn flour
  • 150-200 g Fritter batter
  • A/R Oil (deep frying)

Method

Fritters

  1. For the batter, mix all the dry ingredients and blend it well.
  2. Combine all the liquid together.
  3. Add liquid gradually to the dry and whisk until smooth. Allow to rest for 30 minutes before use.
  4. If the recipe contains egg white, foam it to firm peak (just before using). Fold into the batter, use immediately.
  5. Prepare the selected fruits and cut them even pieces.
  6. Heat oil to 160-170°C (320-338°F) in a large saucepan or deep fryer.
  7. Push the fruit pieces into corn flour, and then dip them into the selected fritter batter and place into the hot oil.
  8. Fry them golden brown on medium heat.
  9. Transfer them onto kitchen towel.
  10. Dust them with icing sugar/ caster sugar/ cinnamon/ cinnamon sugar and place them onto serving dish.

Toffee Sauce

  1. For caramel sauce, bring the cream to boil.
  2. Caramelize the sugar in a large, dry saucepan. It is good, when it is smooth and runny, deep brown, not black and has the first thin white foam appearing on the surface. Take off the heat.
  3. Add the sea salt. For toffee sauce, add the butter as well.
  4. Add the cream little by little and mix it carefully after each addition.
  5. When all the cream is incorporated well, boil it again to make it smooth.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Deep fry at 160–170°C (320–338°F) on medium heat
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Fritters fried golden brown; caramel is smooth and runny, deep brown (not black) with the first thin white foam appearing on the surface
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Served freshly, just after frying/baking, warm
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Whisk, Deep saucepan, Spider / Slotted spoon, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Coating, Frying.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Fritters are pourable batter mixtures. They are deep fried, dusted with sugar and served with fruit preserves, cream or sauces. They are served freshly, just after baking, warm.
      3. Fruit fritters are fresh fruits deep fried covered with fritter batter and dusted with cinnamon sugar or sugar.
      4. They are also served with sauces.
      5. Pancakes, waffles, and crepes are similar pourable consistency batters. They are baked in Special frying pan or machine without or just a thin layer of fat.
      6. The method of the above-mentioned mixtures is the same. It is called Muffin Method.
↑ Back to top

83. Crepe Suzette

Category: Hot & Cold Desserts / Plated Dessert | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: N/A | Yield: 2 - 3 portions

Ingredients

  • 4 – 6 pcs Crepes

Sauce

  • 130 g Orange juice
  • 2 tsp Orange zest
  • 1/2 tsp Lemon juice
  • 60 g Caster sugar
  • 25 g Butter

To Serve

  • 60 g Cream Chantilly

Method

  1. Bring juice and zest to a boil in a sauce pan.
  2. Make golden brown caramel from the sugar in a separate sauce pan, take off the heat and add the butter.
  3. Mix it and add heated juice. Cook until the sugar melts.
  4. Place the crepes into the sauce 1 by 1 and quickly fold them into quarters and place onto serving dishes.
  5. Give 2-3 pcs per portion. Pour over some sauce and decorate with candied orange peel and berries. Serve hot.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: N/A (stovetop caramel and boiling juice)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Golden brown caramel with melted sugar sauce; crepes folded into quarters and served hot
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Serve hot immediately
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Whisk, Ladle, Sieve, Skillet, Rubber spatula, Tongs.
    • Key Learning: Sieving, Whisking.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Traditionally this dish contains orange liqueur, such as Cointreau and Brandy (about 1/3 of the liquid can be substituted), and boil together with the rest to remove the majority of the alcohol.
      3. Suzette Souffle Crepes are filled with orange curd and french meringue mixture and baked in hot oven. Served with Suzette sauce.

84. Crepe Batter

Category: Hot & Cold Desserts | Prep: ~2 hrs (resting in fridge) | Cook/Bake: 1–1.5 mins per side | Yield: 4 – 6 pieces

Ingredients

  • 100 g Flour (A P)
  • 1 tsp Sugar
  • 1/8 tsp Salt
  • 1 pcs Eggs
  • 200 g Milk
  • 50 g Clarified butter / oil

Method

  1. Mix dry ingredients together.
  2. Add eggs and just enough milk to get a soft dough consistency batter.
  3. Mix with a whisk until combines and lump free.
  4. While continuously mixing, add the rest of the milk and melted butter or oil gradually into the batter until getting to heavy cream consistency. If necessary, add some extra water or milk. If lumpy, pour it through a strainer. Allow it to rest for about 2 hours in the fridge before frying.
  5. Frying Crepes: Preheat 15-18 cm crepe pan on high heat. Take off the heat and brush with melted butter or oil and get rid of the excess. Pour 40-60 ml batter into the lifted pan and spread it fast on the bottom. Dump out the excess, if any. It has to be thin. Place it back to the heat and cook until shaking the pan, it moves around and the bottom is golden brown (1-1.5 minutes). Flip over and bake the other side.
  6. Slide ready crepes onto plates and continue baking them and stacking them up as they are ready. Use extra butter as necessary.
  7. Allow them to cool down and cover them. Keep them refrigerated until use.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: High heat on a 15–18 cm crepe pan (1–1.5 minutes on first side)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Heavy cream consistency batter (lump-free); thin crepe that moves around when shaking the pan and is golden brown on the bottom
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Rest batter for about 2 hours in the fridge before frying; allow cooked crepes to cool down, cover them, and keep refrigerated until use
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Whisk, Ladle, Sieve, Skillet, Rubber spatula, Tongs.
    • Key Learning: Sieving, Whisking.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Crepe batter is liquid consistency unleavened batter.
      3. Chocolate version of crepe batter can be made substituting 10-12% of flour with the same amount of cocoa powder.

85. Dark Chocolate Truffle Mixture

Category: Confectionery | Prep: Overnight rest | Cook/Bake: N/A | Yield: 15 - 20 pieces

Ingredients

  • 100 g Dark chocolate
  • 90 g Cream
  • 10 g Butter

Method

  1. Melt dark chocolate over Bain Marie at 50°C (122°F).
  2. Take the bowl off the Bain Marie and add in the soft butter and mix.
  3. In another saucepan, boil cream and cool down to 40°C (104°F). Pour the cream onto the chocolate and mix to form a ganache.
  4. If adding chili powder or chopped cherries, they can be added at this stage.
  5. Cover it with contact cling film. Allow it to rest overnight at 18°C - 20°C (64°F - 68°F) room temperature or fridge.
  6. On the next day, roll the ganache into desired size balls and coat with tempered dark chocolate and further roll into desired nuts, cocoa powder etc.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Melt dark chocolate at 50°C (122°F); cool boiled cream to 40°C (104°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Smooth emulsified ganache set firm enough to roll into balls
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Rest overnight covered with contact cling film at 18°C–20°C (64°F–68°F) room temperature or in the fridge
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Palette Knife, Scraper, Saucepan, Rubber spatula.
    • Key Learning: Making Ganache, Making Truffles.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Chili Truffles - Add chili powder or flakes to dark chocolate truffle mixture. Coat with dark chocolate and roll in cocoa powder.
      3. Cherry Truffles - Add 80g chopped amarena cherries to the dark chocolate ganache. Coat with dark [chocolate].
↑ Back to top

86. Milk Chocolate Truffle Mixture

Category: Confectionery | Prep: Overnight rest | Cook/Bake: N/A | Yield: 15 - 20 pieces

Ingredients

  • 100 g Milk chocolate
  • 65 g Cream
  • 10 g Butter

Method

  1. Melt milk chocolate over Bain Marie at 40°C (104°F).
  2. Off the Bain Marie add soft butter and mix.
  3. Boil the cream and cool to 40°C (104°F) and pour onto the chocolate and mix to form a ganache.
  4. Cover it with cling film. Allow it to rest overnight at 18°C - 20°C (64°F - 68°F) room temperature or fridge.
  5. On the next day, roll the ganache into desired size balls and coat with tempered milk chocolate and further roll into desired nuts, cocoa powder etc.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Melt milk chocolate at 40°C (104°F); cool boiled cream to 40°C (104°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Smooth ganache set firm enough to roll into balls
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Rest overnight at 18°C–20°C (64°F–68°F) room temperature or in the fridge
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Palette Knife, Scraper, Saucepan, Rubber spatula.
    • Key Learning: Making Ganache, Making Truffles.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Sea salt truffles: Add sea salt flakes to the cream of milk chocolate truffle mixture. Coat with milk or dark chocolate, dust with cocoa powder, niege décor, nut nibs, corn flake, etc….
      3. Orange or Lemon truffles: 1/2 tsp of zest of orange and lemon can be added to cream while heating and make desired choice of truffle. Coat with milk chocolate and then roll in desired crunch.

88. Gianduja Truffle

Category: Confectionery | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: N/A | Yield: 15 - 25 pieces

Ingredients

  • 100 g Milk chocolate
  • 100 g Hazelnut paste

Method

  1. Melt milk chocolate over Bain Marie at 40°C (104°F).

      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.

89. Ganache Filling (For ready Truffle Shells)

Category: Confectionery | Prep: Overnight rest | Cook/Bake: N/A | Yield: 12 - 15 pieces

Ingredients

  • 100 g White chocolate
  • 90 g Raspberry Puree
  • 50 g Butter

Method

  1. Melt milk [white] chocolate over Bain Marie at 40°C (104°F).
  2. Off the Bain Marie add soft butter and mix.
  3. Heat puree to 35°C (95°F) and pour onto the chocolate and mix to form a ganache.
  4. Pour it into the piping bag and pipe into the truffle shells.
  5. Allow it to rest overnight, next day seal them with chocolate, coat with tempered chocolate and further roll into desired nuts, or cocoa powder etc.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Melt chocolate at 40°C (104°F); heat raspberry puree to 35°C (95°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Smooth pipeable ganache filling, rested overnight before sealing and coating
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Rest piped truffle shells overnight before sealing
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Palette Knife, Scraper, Saucepan, Rubber spatula.
    • Key Learning: Making Ganache, Making Truffles.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.

91. Chocolate Medallions

Category: Confectionery | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: N/A | Yield: 15 - 20 pieces

Ingredients

  • 100 g Dark/Milk/White Chocolate
  • A/R Roasted Pistachio
  • A/R Roasted Hazelnut
  • A/R Dried Cranberry
  • A/R Dried Apricot
  • A/R Sea salt flakes
  • A/R Raisins

Method

  1. Melt the desired chocolate (dark/milk/white) over Bain Marie.
  2. Temper chocolate according to the temperature curve of each chocolate, respectively.
  3. Pour the tempered chocolate into the piping bag and pipe them 3cm disc onto a silicon mat, slightly tap the tray and place small pieces of desired nuts and fruits.
  4. Allow it to crystallize.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Temper according to the temperature curve of dark, milk, or white chocolate respectively
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: 3 cm piped chocolate discs with nuts and fruits, fully crystallized

      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.

92. Rochers

Category: Confectionery | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: N/A | Yield: 15 - 20 pieces

Ingredients

  • 100 g Dark/Milk/White Chocolate
  • A/R Roasted slivered almonds
  • A/R Cornflakes
  • A/R Rice Crispies

Method

  1. Melt the desired chocolate (dark/milk/white) over Bain Marie.
  2. Temper chocolate according to the temperature curve of each chocolate, respectively.
  3. Add slivered almond/corn flakes/rice crispies to the tempered chocolate.
  4. Gently mix with spatula and allow the chocolate to coat around it.
  5. Drop spoonful of the mixture onto a baking tray lined with baking paper using 2 spoons.
  6. Allow it to crystallize.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Temper according to the temperature curve of dark, milk, or white chocolate respectively
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Crunchy inclusions evenly coated in tempered chocolate and crystallized into spooned clusters

  • Additional Notes & Equipment:

    • Key Learning: Tempering of Chocolate.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.

94. Dark Chocolate Mousse

Category: Hot & Cold Desserts | Prep: 5–10 mins (gelatine soaking), at least 1 hr (setting) | Cook/Bake: N/A | Yield: 6 - 8 portions

Ingredients

  • 110 g Dark Chocolate
  • 110 ml Whipping Cream I
  • 110 ml Whipping Cream II
  • 1 pc Egg Yolk
  • 30 g Sugar
  • 1 pc Egg White
  • 1 pc Gelatin Sheet

Method

  1. Soak the gelatine sheet into ice cold water for 5-10 minutes.
  2. Bring cream I. Take off the heat and add drained gelatine.
  3. Pour it over the chocolate to make a ganache.
  4. Whip cream II. to soft peak and keep it in the fridge.
  5. Slightly whisk the egg yolk and pour the ganache over the top gradually, while continuously whisking.
  6. Make French meringue from the egg white and sugar.
  7. Fold semi whipped cream into the ganache base in 2 additions (1/3rd, mix and then 2/3rd) and fold it.
  8. Finally add the firm, yet smooth meringue and fold together.
  9. Pipe chocolate mousse from piping bag or pour directly to rings, glasses, or other dishes.
  10. Allow it to set for at least an hour in the fridge.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: N/A
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Cream II whipped to soft peak; firm yet smooth French meringue; light, aerated mousse set in the fridge
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Set for at least 1 hour in the fridge. Using pasteurized eggs increases shelf life; mousses can be frozen and served defrosted and chilled
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Hand mixer (whisk attachment), Rubber Spatula, Flexible Spatula, Saucepan, Piping Bags, Piping Nozzle (Star).
    • Key Learning: Making a Sabayon, Proper usage of gelatine, French Meringue, Whipping.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Gelatine can be skipped as dark chocolate is a strong setting agent.
      3. For milk and white chocolate mousse chocolate quantity needs to be increased by 10-20 %.
      4. Gelatine is more often used in milk chocolate mousse and always used at white chocolate mousse.
      5. Using pasteurized eggs shelf life can be increased. Mousses can be frozen but served defrosted and chilled.
      6. Chocolate mousses can be made with additional fruit purees as well.
↑ Back to top

95. Fruit Based Mousse

Category: Hot & Cold Desserts | Prep: 5–10 mins (gelatine soaking), at least 1 hr (setting) | Cook/Bake: N/A | Yield: 6 - 8 portions

Ingredients

  • 100 g Fruit puree (fresh and frozen)
  • 1 tsp Lemon Juice
  • 150 ml Whipping Cream
  • 1 pc Egg Yolk
  • 50 g Sugar
  • 1 pc Gelatin Sheet

Method

  1. Soak gelatine sheets into ice-cold water for 5–10 minutes.
  2. Mix fruit puree with lemon juice (if used) and keep aside.
  3. Whip cream with half of the sugar to medium peak and keep in the fridge.
  4. Whisk egg yolk with rest of the sugar and place it on the double boiler. While continuously whisking, heat it up to 35-40°C (95-104°F) until fluffy and pale to make a Sabayon.
  5. Add drained gelatine and mix until melts.
  6. Mix fruit puree to the sabayon gradually and fold in 1/3rd of whipped cream, mix smooth.
  7. Fold in the rest of whipped cream and fold until combines.
  8. Pipe the mousse from piping bag or pour directly to rings, glasses, or other dishes.
  9. Allow it to set for at least an hour in the fridge.
  10. Serve cold, decorate with cream Chantilly rosettes, fruit, sauce, macaroon, nuts, etc…

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Heat egg yolk and sugar sabayon over double boiler to 35–40°C (95–104°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Sabayon whisked until fluffy and pale; cream whipped to medium peak; mousse set for at least 1 hour in the fridge
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Set for at least 1 hour in the fridge; serve cold
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Hand mixer (whisk attachment), Rubber Spatula, Flexible Spatula, Saucepan, Piping Bags, Piping Nozzle (Star).
    • Key Learning: Making a Sabayon, Proper usage of gelatine, Whipping.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Chocolate based and Fruit based mousses can be made without eggs. Cream content might be increased in that case.
      3. For fruit mousses frozen fruits and frozen fruit purees are preferable, as less preparation is required and consistent result can be achieved, in terms of flavour.
      4. Mousses are fundamental cold mixes that can be used for cold desserts; mousse cakes and French style individual cakes and gateaux-s.
↑ Back to top

96. Tiramisu

Category: Hot & Cold Desserts | Prep: 5–10 mins (gelatin soaking) | Cook/Bake: N/A | Yield: 1 - 2 portions

Ingredients

Tiramisu Mixture

  • 1 pc Egg yolk
  • 25 g Caster sugar
  • 50 g Mascarpone
  • 50 ml Whipping cream
  • 1/2 pc Gelatin leaves

Assembling

  • 6 pcs Lady finger biscuit
  • 80 g (1 double espresso) Espresso coffee
  • 1 tsp Vanilla extract
  • A/R Cocoa powder

Method

Tiramisu Mixture

  1. Soak the gelatin leaves in ice cold water for 5-10 minutes.
  2. Whip whipping cream to medium firm peak and keep it in the fridge.
  3. Beat cream cheese [mascarpone] in a mixing bowl with a rubber spatula until smooth and creamy.
  4. Whisk egg yolk with the sugar on double boiler and heat until exceed 45- 50°C (113-122°F).
  5. Add drained gelatin and mix it until melts down.
  6. Take off the heat and whisk until cools down to lukewarm temperature.
  7. Pour sabayon into the creamy mascarpone cheese while continuously whisking and mix until smooth.
  8. Fold in the whipped cream in 2 additions and fold until combines. Pour the mixture into piping bag without nozzle.

Assembling

  1. Allow espresso coffee to cool down and soak the lady finger biscuit 1 by 1 on the way that the excess coffee is squeezed out from each piece.
  2. Place some soaked biscuit on the bottom of our (straight edge, multi-portion, individual or mini glass dish(es)) sugar side up and place the rest to a clean tray.
  3. Prepare cocoa powder with small dusting sieve.
  4. Pour half of the tiramisu mixture in the dish(es) over the lady fingers.
  5. Dust the surface with cocoa powder and put the rest of the lady fingers (sugar side up) on the top. (Note: Page 158 repeats steps 1–3 of Tiramisu Mixture at the top of the page)

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Sabayon heated on double boiler until exceeding 45–50°C (113–122°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Whipping cream at medium firm peak; mascarpone beaten smooth and creamy; sabayon whisked until lukewarm before combining
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Can be frozen when made with whipped cream and gelatin; original version was made daily, 1 day prior to serve
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Hand mixer (whisk attachment), Rubber Spatula, Flexible Spatula, Saucepan, Piping Bags, Piping Nozzle (Star), Baking Trays.
    • Key Learning: Making a Sabayon, Whipping, Soaking, Layering.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Avoid using berries to decorate it as their flavor doesn’t fit to the “cappuccino” flavor profile.
      3. Traditionally, tiramisu made from whole egg foam with mascarpone, “Kahlua” style liqueur, lady finger, bitter cocoa powder and sugar. It does not contain whipped cream and gelatin. Practicality made this change over the years, as tiramisu can be frozen this way.
      4. The original version was made daily, 1 day prior to serve.
↑ Back to top

97. Cold Set Orange Cheesecake

Category: Hot & Cold Desserts / Entremet | Prep: 5–10 mins (gelatin soaking), at least 1 hr (setting) | Cook/Bake: N/A | Yield: 2 - 4 portions

Ingredients

Biscuit Base

  • 1 pc Digestive biscuit
  • 25 g Butter

Set Cheesecake Mixture

  • 1 pc Egg yolk
  • 50 g Caster [sugar]
  • 2 tsp Orange zest
  • 75 g Full fat cheese (Philadelphia)
  • 80 g Whipping cream
  • 1 pc Egg white
  • 1 pc Gelatin leaves

Orange Jelly

  • 200 ml Orange juice
  • 2 tsp Sugar (optional)
  • 1 1/2 pcs Gelatin leaves

Method

Biscuit Base

  1. For the base, crush the biscuit and mix with the melted butter in the mixing bowl.
  2. Pour the biscuit mix into lined cake rings, glasses or other dish. Spread it briefly throughout the bottom and press it well to shape a base. Set it in the fridge.

Set Cheesecake Mixture

  1. For the cheese mousse, soak the gelatin leaves in ice cold water for 5-10 minutes.
  2. Whip whipping cream to medium firm peak and keep it in the fridge.
  3. Beat cream cheese in a mixing bowl with a rubber spatula until smooth and creamy.
  4. Whisk egg yolk with 1/2 of the sugar and the orange zest on double boiler and heat until exceed 45- 50°C (113-122°F).
  5. Add drained gelatin and mix it until melts down.
  6. Take off the heat and whisk until cools down to lukewarm temperature.
  7. Make French meringue from the egg white and 1/2 of the sugar and do the assembling immediately.
  8. Pour lukewarm sabayon to the cheese gradually while whisking and mix it smooth.
  9. Add 1/3rd of the whipping cream and mix smooth.
  10. Add 2/3rd of the whipping cream and fold until combines.
  11. In the end, fold in the firm, yet creamy French meringue.
  12. Pour mixture on the biscuit base and allow it to set for at least 1 hour in the fridge.

Orange Jelly

  1. Soak the gelatin sheet in ice cold water for 5-10 minutes. (Note: Printed as “5-q0 minutes”)
  2. Boil orange juice with sugar (if used), take off the heat and add drained gelatin. Mix until the gelatin melts.
  3. Allow it to cool down and add a thin layer to the surface of the cheese mousse using a table spoon.
  4. Set in the fridge and pour the rest on top. Set in the fridge and keep there until serve.
  5. Decorate with lemon peel or zest and fresh redcurrant or other berries and/or chocolate decoration, nuts, etc.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Sabayon heated on double boiler until exceeding 45–50°C (113–122°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Whipping cream at medium firm peak; firm yet creamy French meringue; cheesecake set in fridge for at least 1 hour before topping with cooled orange jelly in two stages
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Set in the fridge and keep refrigerated until serving
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Hand mixer (whisk attachment), Rubber Spatula, Flexible Spatula, Saucepan, Baking Trays, Ring mold, Acetate sheet, Rolling Pin.
    • Key Learning: Making a Sabayon, French Meringue, Whipping, Layering.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Using vanilla paste and lemon zest to the cheese mousse, a classic /plain cheese cake flavor can be achieved.
      3. Adding additional flavors and crunches (like at the notes after Bavarois) many different flavor of cheese cake can be prepared.
↑ Back to top

98. Cold Lemon Souffle

Category: Hot & Cold Desserts | Prep: 1–2 hrs (setting in refrigerator) | Cook/Bake: N/A | Yield: 2 - 3 portions

Ingredients

  • 1 pc Egg yolk
  • 50 g Caster sugar
  • 3 tsp Lemon juice
  • 1/2 tsp Lemon zest
  • 130 ml Whipping cream
  • 1 pc Egg white
  • 1 pc Gelatin leaves

Method

  1. Soak the gelatin sheet in ice cold water.
  2. Make sabayon using egg yolk, zest and half the sugar (25 gm) on a double boiler.
  3. Drain the soaked gelatin sheet and add it to the sabayon, remove from double boiler and add lemon juice.
  4. (Maintain the temperature look warm [lukewarm]. If it gets [cold], the gelatin sets inside and cannot be mixed smooth.)
  5. Whip the cream with 1/4 sugar to soft or medium soft peak and keep it in the fridge.
  6. Make French meringue from the egg white and 1/4 sugar, fold immediately.
  7. Add 1/3rd of semi-whipped cream into the (look warm [lukewarm]) sabayon and mix it smooth.
  8. Add the remaining semi-whipped cream and fold it.
  9. In the end, add the creamy yet firm meringue and fold just until combines.
  10. The right consistency is thick, foamy and creamy, still spreading well, not set and lumpy or runny.
  11. Pour/pipe into serving dishes, glasses and allow it to set in refrigerator for at least 1-2 hours.
  12. Garnish the sides with chopped pistachio or other nuts or crumbs, top with candied lemon peel or lemon confit and/or mint leaves, fresh berries, chocolate decoration.
  13. Keep in the fridge until serve and served chilled.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Sabayon cooked over double boiler and maintained at lukewarm temperature when folding
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Thick, foamy, and creamy consistency that still spreads well (not set and lumpy or runny)
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Set in refrigerator for at least 1–2 hours; keep in the fridge until serving and serve chilled
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Hand mixer (whisk attachment), Rubber Spatula, Flexible Spatula, Saucepan, Piping Bags, Baking Trays, Chef’s Knife, White Chopping Board.
    • Key Learning: Making a Sabayon, French Meringue, Whipping.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. For Cold Orange Soufflé use the same amount of orange zest and 6 tsp (30 ml) of orange juice, reduced into half (not more, than 15 ml).
↑ Back to top

99. Vanilla Pannacotta

Category: Hot & Cold Desserts | Prep: 4–6 hrs (refrigerating to set) | Cook/Bake: A few mins | Yield: 2 - 3 portions

Ingredients

Pannacotta

  • 100 ml Milk
  • 100 ml Cream
  • 35 g Sugar
  • 1 pc Gelatin sheet
  • 1 tsp Vanilla extract

Berry Coulis

  • 100 g Frozen berries
  • 40 g Sugar
  • 1 tsp Corn starch
  • 80 g Water

Method

Pannacotta

  1. Soak the gelatin in ice cold water and set aside.
  2. Boil together milk, cream, sugar & vanilla pod/extract.
  3. Take off the heat and add drained gelatin sheet.
  4. Stir until gelatin is dissolved.
  5. Strain the mixture and remove the foam if necessary with a skimmer.
  6. Pour it into pannacotta molds or glasses. Refrigerate for 4-6 hrs to set.
  7. Remold or leave in the glass and serve with berry coulis or any other sauce. (To remold, submerge the mold in boiling hot water for a few second and turn it over on to the serving plate.)
  8. Garnish with fresh fruit or berries, mint leaves and/or chocolate decoration.

Berry Coulis

  1. Place frozen berries and half of the water in a saucepan and bring to a boil.
  2. Blend corn starch and sugar together dry and mix with the remaining cold water.
  3. Add starch mixture to the boiling liquid and cook it until for a few more seconds.
  4. Allow it to cool down on the side.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Boil milk, cream, sugar, and vanilla; submerge mold in boiling hot water for a few seconds to remold
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Smooth, foam-free cream mixture set firm after 4–6 hours in the refrigerator; thickened glossy berry coulis
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Refrigerate for 4–6 hours to set
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Hand mixer (whisk attachment), Rubber Spatula, Flexible Spatula, Saucepan, Piping Bags, Digital thermometer, Panna cotta molds, Chef’s Knife, White Chopping Board.
    • Key Learning: Making a Sabayon, Proper usage of gelatine, Whipping, Making a coulis.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.

100. Parfait

Category: Hot & Cold Desserts | Prep: Overnight freeze (or 2–3 hrs blast freeze) | Cook/Bake: N/A | Yield: 2 - 3 portions

Ingredients

Italian Meringue

  • 30 g Sugar
  • 15 g Water
  • 1 pc Egg white

Mousse

  • 30 g Sugar
  • 15 g Water
  • 2 pcs Egg yolk
  • 250 ml Cream

Method

Italian Meringue

  1. Whisk egg white till medium peak.
  2. Heat water & sugar till it reaches 118°C (244°F) and pour over the egg white in a steady stream while whisking continuously.
  3. Refrigerate until required.

Mousse

  1. Whisk egg yolk till it become pale and fluffy.
  2. Heat water & sugar till it reaches 118°C (244°F) and pour over the egg yolk in a steady stream while whisking continuously.
  3. Whip 250ml of cream to a medium peak stage. Fold the cream to the egg yolk mixture in two additions.
  4. Finally fold the Italian meringue.
  5. Pour the mixture in multi portion or individual metal tins or silicon moulds and freeze overnight (or blast freeze for 2-3 hours and keep in normal freezer until serve).
  6. Decorate with glaze, Chantilly, chocolate, nuts, etc. Serve frozen onto frozen dishes.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Sugar syrups heated to 118°C (244°F) for both the Italian meringue and the pate a bombe (egg yolk) base
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Egg whites at medium peak; egg yolks pale and fluffy; cream whipped to medium peak; frozen solid
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Freeze overnight (or blast freeze for 2–3 hours) and keep in normal freezer until serving; serve frozen onto frozen dishes
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowl, Hand mixer (whisk attachment), Flexible Spatula, Rubber Spatula, Saucepan.
    • Key Learning: Italian Meringue, Proper usage of gelatine, Folding.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
↑ Back to top

101. Coconut Tapioca Pudding

Category: Hot & Cold Desserts | Prep: Chilling time | Cook/Bake: N/A | Yield: 2 - 3 portions

Ingredients

Tapioca Pudding

  • 1/4 cup Small pearl tapioca
  • 250 ml Coconut milk
  • A/R Water
  • 1/4 cup Palm sugar

Mango Salsa

  • 1 pc Mango fresh
  • 1/4 tsp Mango paste (Note: Method step 7 mentions lemon juice, mint and vanilla paste)
  • 1 sprig Mint

Method

Tapioca Pudding

  1. Boil water in a sauce pan, add tapioca and cook it until soft.
  2. Drain and rinse with cold water to separate each piece. Set it aside in the water.
  3. Bring coconut milk and palm sugar to boil in another sauce pan.
  4. Add stirred [strained] tapioca and boil again.
  5. Reduce the heat and simmer uncovered until it thickens and reduced. Stirring frequently.
  6. Cool and pour into serving dishes. Refrigerate.

Mango Salsa

  1. Dice mango and toss with lemon juice, mint and vanilla paste.
  2. Scoop salsa on the top of the pudding and chill until serve.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Simmer uncovered on reduced heat
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Tapioca pearls cooked until soft, rinsed to separate each piece, and simmered in coconut milk and palm sugar until thickened and reduced
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Refrigerate / chill until serving; chilling overnight gives the best flavor
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowl, Rubber Spatula, Saucepan, Strainer.
    • Key Learning: Making a pudding.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Chilling overnight gives the best flavor.

102. Cream Caramel

Category: Hot & Cold Desserts | Prep: 2–3 hrs (chilling) | Cook/Bake: 35–45 mins | Yield: 2 - 3 portions

Ingredients

Caramel Layer

  • 50 g Caster sugar II

Custard Mixture

  • 125 ml Milk
  • 15 g Caster sugar I.
  • 1 pc Whole egg
  • 1 tsp Vanilla extract

Method

Caramel Layer

  1. Place sugar II. in a heavy bottom pan and leave on slow heat to caramelize.
  2. Pour caramel as soon as ready to ramekin dishes and spread it throughout the bottom as thin as possible.

Custard Mixture

  1. Boil milk, sugar I. and vanilla together.
  2. Pour heated milk into lightly beaten egg & continue whisking.
  3. Strain the custard and set aside.
  4. Pour custard mix over the top of caramel and fill the ramekins until they are full.
  5. Bake in Bain Marie (shallow water) at 100°C (212°F) for 35-45 minutes or until firm yet shaky in the center.
  6. Chill for 2-3 hrs.
  7. Serve remolded. To remold run the knife along the sides of the custard and invert on to a plate.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Bake in Bain Marie (shallow water) at 100°C (212°F) for 35–45 minutes
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Caramel spread as thin as possible on bottom of ramekins; custard baked until firm yet shaky in the center
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Chill for 2–3 hours before remolding and serving
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowl, Fork, GN tray, Ramekins, Oven, Saucepan, Strainer.
    • Key Learning: Making a set custard, Caramelization of sugar.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Vanilla pods are scraped out and cook with the milk. Skin needs to be removed and the milk drained before pour on the egg.
      3. Vanilla extract can be used at any stage.
↑ Back to top

103. Crème Brulee

Category: Hot & Cold Desserts | Prep: 2–3 hrs (chilling) | Cook/Bake: 35–45 mins | Yield: 2 - 4 portions

Ingredients

  • 60 g Egg yolk
  • 135 ml Whipping cream
  • 65 ml Milk
  • 35 g Sugar
  • 1 tsp Vanilla extract

Finish

  • A/R Caster/Demerara Sugar

Method

  1. Whisk sugar and egg yolk with a hand whisk in a mixing bowl.
  2. Gently heat cream and milk together (up to about 55-60°C / 131-140°F), do not boil.
  3. Pour heated cream on sugar egg mixture while continuously mixing.
  4. Strain into ramekins and bake on water bath (on shallow water) at 100°C (212°F) for 35-45 minutes or until firm in the centre.
  5. Chill for 2-3 hrs.
  6. Just before serving, sprinkle generous amount of caster or a thinner layer of demerara sugar on the surface, clean the side of the dish and caramelize the sugar layer using a blow torch.
  7. Decorate with berry or a piece of Madeline or leave as it is and serve immediately.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Heat cream and milk to 55–60°C (131–140°F) without boiling; bake in shallow water bath at 100°C (212°F) for 35–45 minutes
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Custard baked until firm in the centre; topped with a caramelized sugar crust using a blow torch just before serving
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Chill for 2–3 hours; serve immediately after caramelizing the sugar top
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowl, Fork, GN tray, Ramekins, Oven, Blow torch, Saucepan, Strainer.
    • Key Learning: Making a set custard, Caramelization of sugar.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. For Lemon cream brulee, use 1 tsp of lemon zest (3 gm) and add to the egg yolk before whisking. Vanilla can be reduced to half.
      3. Vanilla cream brulee is the classic flavor.
      4. Additional flavors can be added, like coffee, cocoa, rose water, pistachio, etc.
↑ Back to top

104. Apple Crumble

Category: Hot & Cold Desserts | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 12–15 mins | Yield: 2 - 4 portions

Ingredients

Crumble Mixture

  • 25 g Flour
  • 15 g Butter
  • 10 g Light brown sugar

Fruit Base

  • 1 pc Green apple
  • 10 g Sugar
  • 10 g Butter
  • 15 g Walnut
  • 15 g Raisins

Crème Anglaise

  • 100 ml Whipping cream (Note: Method step 8 refers to pouring hot milk)
  • 20 g Egg yolk
  • 25 g Caster sugar
  • 1/2 tsp Vanilla extract
  • A/R Icing/ caster sugar (for dusting)

Method

Crumble Mixture

  1. To make the crumble mixture, rub together flour, butter & sugar, to attain a sandy texture. Keep it in the fridge until use.

Fruit Base

  1. Heat butter in a pan, add apple and sugar. Toss quickly. Add raisin.
  2. When it is ready, take off the heat and add the broken walnut.
  3. Pour the mixture in mould or other dish(es). If they will be removed from the dish, press them well. Allow them to cool down.
  4. Cover with the crumble just before baking.
  5. Bake at 180°C (356°F) for 12 - 15 minutes or until crumble browns.

Crème Anglaise

  1. Whisk egg yolk and caster sugar in a mixing bowl with a hand whisk until fluffy, smooth and pale in color.
  2. Pour hot milk [cream] into the mixture and mix it smooth.
  3. Pour the mixture back to the sauce pan and cook it until 85-86°C (185-187°F), or until thicken, fluffy, yet creamy.
  4. Take off the heat and whisk for further 1-2 minutes. Transfer to a clean container. Serve warm.
  5. Serve hot or cold. Before serving, dust the top with sugar and add cream Anglaise, cream Chantilly, or other sauce for it. It can be served with vanilla, caramel, etc ice cream.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Bake at 180°C (356°F) for 12–15 minutes; cook Crème Anglaise to 85–86°C (185–187°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Sandy texture crumble mixture baked until browned; Crème Anglaise cooked until thickened, fluffy, yet creamy
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Keep crumble mixture in the fridge until use; serve hot or cold
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Whisk, Rubber Spatula, Saucepans, Baking Trays, Ring mold, Pastry Brush, Chef’s Knife, White Chopping Board.
    • Key Learning: Making a warm dessert.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.

105. Bread and Butter Pudding / Cabinet Pudding

Category: Hot & Cold Desserts | Prep: Soaking time | Cook/Bake: 20–25 mins | Yield: 4 – 6 portions

Ingredients

  • 200 ml Milk
  • 1 tsp Vanilla extract
  • 2 pcs Egg
  • 25 g Sugar
  • 3 slices Bread
  • 25 g Raisin (optional)
  • 15 g Butter
  • 1 pinch Nutmeg

Method

  1. Trim the crust of the bread slices, cut it into smaller pieces and set aside.
  2. Boil milk with vanilla extract or pod and sugar.
  3. Lightly whisk eggs and pour the boiled milk onto it. Whisk until smooth.
  4. Arrange the bread slices in a baking dish, sprinkle raisin and pour the custard over it. Let it stand to soak.
  5. Dot with butter and bake at 150°C (302°F) for 20 – 25 minutes or until the custard is set.
  6. Serve hot or cold. Dust the top with icing sugar.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 150°C (302°F) for 20–25 minutes
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Soaked bread pieces in smooth custard baked until the custard is set
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Serve hot or cold
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Whisk, Rubber Spatula, Saucepan, Baking Trays, Bread Knife, White Chopping Board, Baking Dish.
    • Key Learning: Making a warm pudding.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.

106. Umm Ali

Category: Hot & Cold Desserts | Prep: 10 mins (soaking) | Cook/Bake: 12–15 mins (puff pastry) + 8–10 mins (final bake) | Yield: 4 – 6 portions

Ingredients

  • 50 g Baked puff pastry
  • 30 g Sugar
  • 250 g Milk
  • 20 g Almond flakes
  • 20 g Pistachio nibs
  • 40 g Raisin
  • 10 g Desiccated coconut (optional)
  • 2 tsp Rose water (optional)
  • 80 g Cream

Method

  1. Roll and cut raw puff pastry to 30x30 mm squares and bake at 180°C (356°F) for 12-15 minutes or until golden brown.
  2. In a saucepan, bring milk to boil, remove from heat and add sugar, vanilla, nuts, raisin, coconut and mix.
  3. Add crushed puff pastry and soak for 10 minutes.
  4. Mix it well with a hand whisk to smash the larger pieces and pour into a deep dish.
  5. Whip the cream to medium firm peak and pipe evenly on the top.
  6. Bake it at 160°C (320°F) for 8-10 minutes or until golden brown.
  7. Decorate with crushed pistachio and serve hot.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Puff pastry baked at 180°C (356°F) for 12–15 minutes; assembled pudding baked at 160°C (320°F) for 8–10 minutes
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Puff pastry golden brown, soaked 10 minutes and whisked to smash larger pieces; topped with medium-firm peak whipped cream and baked until golden brown
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Serve hot
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Whisk, Rubber Spatula, Saucepan, Baking Trays, Baking Dish, Chef’s Knife, White Chopping Board, Piping Bag, Hand Mixer (whisk attachment).
    • Key Learning: Making a warm pudding.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Can be portioned to individual dishes and made by portion.

107. Rice Pudding

Category: Hot & Cold Desserts | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: Simmer until tender + 5–10 mins thickening | Yield: Rice Pudding

Ingredients

  • 500 ml Whole milk
  • 35 g Short grain rice (Egyptian)
  • 25 g Granulated sugar
  • 1 tsp Vanilla extract
  • 1 pinch Ground cardamom (optional)
  • 20 g Raisins (optional)
  • 30 g Cashew nut (optional)

Method

  1. In a saucepan boil the milk and add rice.
  2. Reduce the heat and simmer until the rice is tender.
  3. Stir the milk mixture frequently to prevent the rice from sticking to the bottom of the pan.
  4. When the rice is cooked, take off the heat and add the sugar, vanilla extract, raisin and ground cardamom.
  5. Return to heat and cook until the rice pudding thickens (about 5 to 10 minutes).
  6. Remove from heat and add the nuts.
  7. Serve warm.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Boil then simmer on reduced heat
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Rice simmered until tender, then cooked 5–10 minutes with sugar and aromatics until the pudding thickens
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Serve warm
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Rubber Spatula, Saucepan, Chef’s Knife, White Chopping Board.
    • Key Learning: Making a warm pudding.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.

108. Steamed Treacle Pudding

Category: Hot & Cold Desserts | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 12–15 mins (steaming) | Yield: 4 – 6 portions

Ingredients

Bottom

  • A/R Treacle syrup
  • A/R Bread crumbs

Batter

  • 45 g Butter
  • 45 g Demerara sugar
  • 1 pc Eggs
  • 1/8 tsp Lemon zest
  • 45 g Flour
  • 1/2 tsp Baking powder
  • 1 tsp Milk

Serve

  • A/R Cream Chantilly

Method

  1. Line cake rings and pour treacle syrup (or dark corn syrup) on the bottom.
  2. Sprinkle it with generous amount of bread crumbs.
  3. For the cake batter, sieve flour and baking powder together.
  4. Cream room temperature butter with the sugar and lemon zest.
  5. Add eggs gradually and mix until smooth.
  6. Add the flour alternate with the milk and finish the mixing by spatula. Mix until smooth.
  7. Pipe or pour the batter into the prepared tin(s) and cling film it.
  8. Steam at 100°C (212°F) for 12-15 minutes.
  9. After baking and a few minutes resting, cut them around and flip them out.
  10. Serve warm (treacle sugar part up) with cream Chantilly or clotted cream.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Steam at 100°C (212°F) for 12–15 minutes
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Smooth creamed cake batter steamed under cling film, rested a few minutes, and inverted with the treacle sugar part on top
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Serve warm
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Rubber Spatula, Saucepan, Cake rings, Microplane / Zester, Sieve, Hand mixer (whisk attachment), Piping Bags.
    • Key Learning: Making a warm pudding.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.

109. Baked Alaska / Norweigan Omelet

Category: Hot & Cold Desserts | Prep: Freeze until baking | Cook/Bake: 20–30 secs | Yield: 6 – 8 portions

Ingredients

  • 500 ml Italian meringue
  • 35 g Sugar
  • 25 g Water

Assembling

  • 50 g Thin sheet sponge cake
  • 200 g Ice cream

Method

  1. Whisk egg whites to soft peak stage.
  2. Cook sugar and water to soft ball stage or 118°C (244°F).
  3. Pour in a thin stream over the whisked egg, continuously whisking.
  4. Continue whisking until cold.
  5. Lay a disk of sponge on a baking tray.
  6. Place ice cream on to the sponge cake, leaving a rim of one-fourth inch.
  7. Cover with sponge and the meringue.
  8. Decorate with candied fruits and nuts. Keep in the freezer until baking.
  9. Just before serve, bake at 240°C (464°F) for 20-30 seconds or until browning.
  10. Alternatively, can be burnt with blow torch.
  11. Serve immediately.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Sugar syrup cooked to 118°C (244°F / soft ball stage); baked at 240°C (464°F) for 20–30 seconds (or browned with a blow torch)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Italian meringue whisked until cold; exterior browned while ice cream center remains frozen
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Keep in the freezer until baking; serve immediately after browning
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Rubber Spatula, Saucepan, Cake rings, Microplane / Zester, Hand mixer (whisk attachment), Piping Bags, Piping Nozzle, Blow torch.
    • Key Learning: Italian Meringue, Baking.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Can be flambéed with heated alcohol.

111. Poached Pear

Category: Hot & Cold Desserts / Plated Dessert | Prep: Chilling time | Cook/Bake: 20–30 mins (poaching) | Yield: 3 – 6 portions

Ingredients

  • 2 pcs Pears (peeled)
  • 500 ml Berry juice
  • 2 pcs Star anise
  • 1 pc Cinnamon stick
  • 1 tsp Corn starch

Filling

  • 4 tbsp Cream cheese
  • 2 tbsp Nuts (chopped)

Method

  1. For the filling, mix together, chopped nuts and cheese.
  2. Heat the juice along with star anise and cinnamon to 70°C (158°F).
  3. Core the pear, keeping the stalk for presentation.
  4. Poach the pears in the juice for 20 -30 min, making sure that the liquid doesn’t boil.
  5. Remove from the poaching liquid and chill. Stuff the cavity with nuts & cheese mixture.
  6. Serve with the berry sauce.
  7. For the berry sauce, thicken the poaching liquid with a corn slurry (corn starch and water).

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Poach in berry juice heated to 70°C (158°F) for 20–30 minutes without boiling
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Tender poached pears with stalk intact, chilled and stuffed with cream cheese-nut filling, served with thickened berry sauce
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Chill pears after removing from poaching liquid
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Deep Saucepan, Tongs, Slotted Spoon, Wire Rack, Baking Tray.
    • Key Learning: Poaching stone fruit, Making a berry sauce.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.

112. Baked Lemon Souffle

Category: Hot & Cold Desserts | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 10–12 mins | Yield: 1 - 2 portions

Ingredients

Base

  • 22 g Bread flour
  • 22 g Butter
  • 125 ml Milk
  • 30 g Caster Sugar I.
  • 45 g Eggs yolk
  • 1 tsp Vanilla
  • 1/2 tsp Lemon zest

French Meringue

  • 75 g Egg white
  • 15 g Caster sugar II.
  • 1 pinch Cream of tartar

Serve

  • A/R Icing Sugar

Method

  1. Grease soufflé dish and sprinkle the base with sugar and set in refrigerator.
  2. Preheat oven to 170°C (338°F).
  3. Make a panada with the bread flour, milk, sugar I, vanilla and lemon zest.
  4. Cool the panada completely and then add in the egg yolk and butter and mix well with a whisk.
  5. Separately, make French meringue from the egg white, caster sugar II and cream of tartar.
  6. Fold the meringue into the panada in 2 – 3 additions and fold until smooth and fluffy.
  7. Pour in the soufflé mixture and bake at 170 – 175°C (338 – 347°F) with no fan for 10-12 minutes or until the soufflé stops rising and is golden brown.
  8. Sprinkle with icing sugar and serve warm with crème anglaise.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Preheat to 170°C (338°F); bake at 170–175°C (338–347°F) with no fan for 10–12 minutes
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Smooth and fluffy folded batter; baked until the soufflé stops rising and is golden brown
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Serve warm immediately
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Microplane / Zester, Saucepan, Flexible Spatula, Rubber Spatula, Ramekin, Pastry Brush, Baking Tray.
    • Key Learning: Panada, French Meringue.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.

113. Clafoutis

Category: Hot & Cold Desserts | Prep: 2 hrs (cherry marination) | Cook/Bake: N/A | Yield: 1 - 2 portions

Ingredients

Cherry Preparation

  • 22 g Fresh soured cherry (pitted)
  • 22 g Caster sugar
  • 125 ml Kirsh (optional)

Clafoutis Mixture

  • 20 g A P flour
  • 15 g Almond powder
  • 1/8 tsp Salt
  • 1 pc Egg
  • 1 pc Egg yolks
  • 50 g Caster sugar
  • 1 tsp Vanilla essence
  • 1/2 tsp Lemon zest
  • 5 g Butter
  • 80 ml Milk
  • 80 ml Whipping cream

Serve

  • A/R Caster Sugar (Note: Printed as “ICaster Sugar”)

Method

  1. Marinate the cherries, sugar and Kirsch for 2 hours.
  2. Brush the inside of the baking dish with butter and sprinkle evenly with sugar.
  3. Shake the dish so as to cover the complete surface.
  4. Melt butter in a saucepan till just turning a hazelnut color, strain and allow it to cool (beurre noisette).
  5. Mix flour and salt in bowl.
  6. Make a well in the middle, add eggs, sugar, vanilla and lemon zest.
  7. Whisk until get a smooth consistency. (Note: Printed method ends at step 7 on page 183)

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: N/A (not stated in printed method)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Butter melted to a hazelnut color (beurre noisette); batter whisked to a smooth consistency
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Marinate cherries for 2 hours prior to assembly
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Rubber Spatula, Cake rings, Baking Tray, Hand mixer (whisk attachment).
    • Key Learning: Creaming, Baking.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.

114. Banoffee Pie

Category: Hot & Cold Desserts / Entremet | Prep: 1 day prior (overnight cooling of dulce de leche) | Cook/Bake: 4–6 hrs (boiling condensed milk can) | Yield: 10 - 12cm dia. Tartlet shells, (2 tartlet shells)

Ingredients

A) Base

  • 70 g Digestive biscuit
  • 35 g Butter

B) Filling

  • 130 g Condensed milk
  • 1 pcs Banana
  • A/R Demarara sugar

C) Chantilly cream

  • 100 g Whipping cream
  • 10 g Icing sugar
  • 1/4 tsp Vanilla paste

D) Top

  • A/R Chocolate shavings

Method

For the Dulce de leche

  1. 1 day prior to production, place condensed milk (closed can) in deep water and boil for 4-6 hours slowly.
  2. During cooking, keep adding water, making sure the can is covered with water all the way, at all times.
  3. Allow to cool down overnight.

For the Base

  1. Mix broken digestive biscuit with melted butter and press down this mixture into the 100-120 mm round individual tart tins.
  2. Distribute it evenly and press well with spoon or spatula. Set in freezer.
  3. To remold, heat the bottom of the tin with blowtorch and pop the base out. Keep in the fridge.

For the Filling

  1. Take out condensed milk from the can when cold. It should be caramel color. This is dulce de leche.
  2. Beat it with spatula until smooth and pipe it into the base.
  3. Peel and slice banana and lay them on silver non stick trays.
  4. Sprinkle a generous amount of demarara sugar on the surface and caramelize it with a blowtorch.
  5. Place the caramelized slices on the dulce de leche and allow to set in the chiller.

Assembly and Finishing

  1. Spread or pipe Chantilly cream on top.
  2. Decorate with chocolate flakes or shavings.
  3. Keep in the chiller until ready to serve.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Boil closed condensed milk can slowly in deep water for 4–6 hours; caramelize banana slices and warm tart tins with a blowtorch
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Condensed milk cooked to a caramel-colored dulce de leche and beaten smooth; banana slices caramelized with demerara sugar
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Cool dulce de leche overnight; set base in freezer then keep in fridge; keep finished pie in the chiller until ready to serve
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Whisk, Aluminium foil, Cling film, Tart molds, Baking trays, Oven mitts/gloves, Saucepan, Rubber Spatula, Piping Bag, Hand Mixer, Whisk Attachment, Chef’s Knife, Chopping Board, Blowtorch.
    • Key Learning: Creaming method, Making Dulce de Leche.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. The name banoffee is a combination of the words “banana” and “toffee”.
      3. Banoffee pie is a dessert of English Origin and made of bananas, a rich caramel sauce, topped with whipped cream served on a crushed biscuit and butter base tart shell.
      4. Cold liquids such as milk or water can also be used to bind the pastry.
      5. Sheeting the pastry between two sheets of cling film or baking paper, make it easier to handle.
↑ Back to top

115. Blood Orange Sable, Plated Version

Category: Plated Dessert / Pastry | Prep: 30–45 mins (optional dough rest) | Cook/Bake: 10–12 mins | Yield: 10 - 12cm dia. Tartlet shells, (2 tartlet shells)

Ingredients

Blood orange sable; the plated version is made of 4 components: A) Sable Breton, B) Blood Orange Cream, C) Blood Orange Jelly, D) Italian Meringue

A) Sable Breton

  • 130 g Butter
  • 52 g Icing sugar
  • 1 pcs Egg yolk
  • 105 g Flour
  • 18 g Almond powder
  • 1/8 g Salt
  • ½ tsp Vanilla extract

B) Blood Orange Cream (Cremeaux)

  • 100 g Blood orange juice
  • 30 g Sugar
  • 1 pcs Egg yolk
  • 7 g Corn flour
  • ¾ pcs Gelatin leaves bronze
  • 30 g Whipping cream

C) Blood Orange Jelly

  • 150 g Blood Orange juice
  • 36 g Sugar
  • 1.5 pcs Gelatin leaves bronze
  • 9 g Corn starch

D) Italian Meringue

  • 46 g Egg white
  • 120 g Caster sugar
  • 36 g Water

Method

For the Sable Breton

  1. Make the breton dough with creaming method and pipe it in non-stick tin pans (50 x 100 mm).
  2. Bake at 160°C for 10–12 minutes or until golden brown.
  3. As soon as came out of the oven, place a baking paper and tray on top and turn it upside down. Remove the tin.

For the Blood Orange Cremeaux

  1. Using all ingredients apart from gelatin and whipping cream, make cream as cream patisserie and add soaked and drained gelatin while it is hot.
  2. Cling film contact and allow to cool down.
  3. Beat it until smooth and fold in the whipped cream, as a cream diplomat.
  4. Fill to piping bag and pipe in silicon mould (20 x 90 mm). Blast freeze and remold.

For the Italian Meringue

  1. Make it just before plating.
  2. Place it to piping bag with nozzle.

For the Blood Orange Jelly

  1. Soak gelatin in cold water.
  2. Boil blood orange juice with sugar. Add in the corn starch mixed with little water.
  3. Add in the soaked gelatin and mix well.
  4. Pour into the moulds and set in the freezer.

Assembly and Finishing

  1. Assemble dessert on the plate.
  2. Decorate with orange jelly and roasted slivered almonds.
  3. Serve immediately.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 160°C (320°F) for Sable Breton; boil for Blood Orange Jelly and Cremeaux base
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Golden brown, flaky and crumbly texture for Sable Breton; smooth diplomat-style cremeaux; set jelly
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Serve immediately once plated; Cremeaux and Jelly components are blast-frozen/set in freezer prior to assembly
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. In French, Sable means “sandy” and Breton refers to the region in the North of Brittany, where the sable cookie is from.
      3. As the name indicates, they are butter cookies that are flaky and crumbly texture.
      4. Sable breton dough can be rested in the fridge for 30–45 minutes and roll to 5 mm sheet.
      5. It is cut to the size of the tin (9 ½ x 4 cm), washed with egg yolk and bake in the tin egg washed-side up. After baking it is handled the same way.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Whisk, Aluminium foil, Cling film, Baking trays, Oven mitts/gloves, Saucepan, Rubber Spatula, Piping Bag, Hand Mixer, Whisk Attachment, Baking Paper, Silicon Mold (20x90mm), Silicon Mold (hemi sphere, 2cm), Offset Spatula.
    • Key Learning: Creaming method, Making a Cremeaux, Making a Jelly.
↑ Back to top

116. Linzer Tart

Category: Pastry | Prep: 25–35 mins (chilling & freezing) | Cook/Bake: 10–12 mins (blind bake) + 15–20 mins (final bake) | Yield: 10 - 12cm dia. Tartlet shells, (2 tartlet shells)

Ingredients

Components: A) Linzer Dough, B) Raspberry Jam, C) Finishing

A) Linzer Dough

  • 50 g Butter
  • 32 g Icing Sugar
  • 20 g Egg
  • ¼ tsp Salt
  • 15 g Roasted hazelnut powder
  • 80 g Flour
  • ¼ tsp Cinnamon powder
  • 1/8 tsp Baking powder
  • 1/8 tsp Lemon Zest

B) Raspberry Jam

  • 55 g Raspberries puree frozen
  • 55 g Raspberry whole frozen
  • 85 g Sugar
  • 1 tbsp Lemon Juice

C) Finishing

  • 12.5 g Egg Wash
  • 5 g Hazelnut, chopped
  • A/R Icing Sugar
  • A/R Raspberry Jam

Method

For the Linzer Dough

  1. Make the dough using the creaming method and place in the chiller, wrapped, for 20–30 minutes.

For the Raspberry Jam

  1. Boil fruit, lemon juice and fruit puree with the sugar on low heat until it reaches 106°C.
  2. Spread it and allow to cool.

For the Assembly

  1. Roll 2/3rd of the dough to 4–5 mm thin and line the tins with it. Dock them and place the freezer for 5 minutes.
  2. Blind bake them at 160°C for 10–12 minutes and allow them to cool in the tin.
  3. Spread the jam on top.
  4. They can be garnished with chopped hazelnut or walnut.
  5. Roll the left over dough to 2–3 mm and cut to 1 cm wide lines.
  6. Place half of the lines on top, leaving about 1 cm gap in between each other. Place the rest of lines across of the first level.
  7. Wash with egg and sprinkle with chopped hazelnut.
  8. Bake at 160°C for 15–20 minutes.

For the Finishing

  1. Straight after baking, it can be brushed with raspberry jam or dusted with niege décor, when cold.
  2. Keep on room temperature covered until serve.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Jam cooked to 106°C (223°F); Blind bake at 160°C (320°F) for 10–12 mins; Final bake at 160°C (320°F) for 15–20 mins
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Short, crumbly pastry tart shell with a golden lattice top
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Keep at room temperature covered until serving
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Linzer Tart is a traditional Austrian pastry. It is named after the city of Linz in Austria.
      3. It is a short, crumbly pastry tart shell, filled with a berry jam, covered with a lattice of dough strips. The pastry is brushed with egg wash and garnished with sliced nuts.
      4. It is a popular holiday treat in Austria, Hungary, Switzerland and Germany, often eaten at Christmas dinners.
      5. Some bakeries around the world make the Linzer tortes as small tarts or as cookies.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Whisk, Aluminium foil, Cling film, Baking trays, Oven mitts/gloves, Saucepan, Rubber Spatula, Piping Bag, Hand Mixer, Whisk Attachment, Sugar Thermometer, Rolling Pin, Offset Spatula, Baking Tray, Pastry Brush.
    • Key Learning: Creaming method, Making a Fruit Jam, Linzer Tart weaving technique.
↑ Back to top

117. Pecan Pie

Category: Pastry / Plated Dessert | Prep: 15–20 mins (chilling) | Cook/Bake: 10–12 mins (base) + 8–10 mins (Jaconde) + 6–8 mins (filling) | Yield: 20 X 20cm. 10 portions

Ingredients

Pecan Pie is made of 5 components: A) Sweet pastry base, B) Jaconde, C) Pecan Filling, D) Caramelized pecan, E) Whipped Caramel Sauce

A) Sweet Pastry Base

  • 200 g Flour
  • 100 g Butter
  • 40 g Icing sugar
  • ½ tsp Salt
  • 1 pcs Egg
  • 1 tsp Vanilla extract

B) Jaconde

  • 57 g Egg
  • 47 g Icing Sugar
  • 47 g Almond Powder
  • 12 g Flour
  • 60 g Egg whites
  • 19 g Sugar
  • 10 g Butter

C) Pecan Filling

  • 175 g Brown sugar
  • 75 g Butter
  • 5 pcs Eggs
  • 125 g Honey
  • 10 ml Vanilla extract
  • 200 g Pecan

D) Caramelized Pecan

  • 20 g Water
  • 100 g Sugar
  • 25 g Glucose syrup
  • A/R Pecan

E) Whipped Caramel Sauce

  • 100 g Caster Sugar
  • 80 g Glucose
  • 100 g Cream
  • 60 g Condensed milk
  • 5 ml Vanilla
  • 160 g Butter
  • 1 g Salt

Method

For the Sweet Pastry Base

  1. Make sweet short pastry with rub-in method and chill it for 15–20 minutes.
  2. Roll it to 3–4 mm thick and dock it. Freeze it for a few minutes and bake it at 160°C for 10–12 minutes or until golden brown.
  3. Cut with rectangular individual cake frame (30 x 90 mm) as soon as it comes out of the oven.
  4. Allow to cool.

For the Jaconde

  1. Sieve together icing sugar and flour, whisk with almond powder.
  2. Add the whole eggs and foam it until fluffy.
  3. In a separate bowl, make a french meringue from egg white and sugar.
  4. Add this meringue to the paste in 2 additions and fold it together.
  5. Add the melted butter last.
  6. Spread the Jaconde mixture to the lined baking sheets.
  7. Bake sheets at 160°C for 8–10 minutes.

For the Pecan Filling

  1. Melt butter and mix together with all the rest. Add pecan in the end.
  2. Distribute filling equally to the tins over the base and spread briefly.

For the Assembly

  1. In the same frame that the sweet pastry was baked, pour in the pecan filling and smooth it out to make an even layer.
  2. At 160°C for 6–8 minutes or until the mixture sets. Demold while warm. Keep in room temperature until required to be served.

For the Whipped Caramel Sauce

  1. Make caramel with the caster sugar and glucose.
  2. Pour hot cream over it off the heat and add condensed milk, vanilla and salt.
  3. When the mixture reaches 50°C start incorporating butter in small pieces using a hand blender.

For the Finishing

  1. Plate the pies with whipped caramel sauce, piped French meringue and decorate with caramelized pecan.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 160°C (320°F) for Sweet Pastry Base (10–12 mins), Jaconde (8–10 mins), and Pecan Filling (6–8 mins); 50°C (122°F) for emulsifying butter into Whipped Caramel Sauce
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Golden brown pastry base; fluffy Jaconde sponge; set pecan filling
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Demold while warm and keep at room temperature until required to be served
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. It is a classic pie of American origin. Pecan Pies are most popularly served at holiday meals in the United States.
      3. Most pecan pie recipes include salt and vanilla. Other popular additions for flavour include: chocolate and bourbon whiskey.
      4. Pecan pies are usually served with ice cream or whipped cream.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Whisk, Aluminium foil, Cling film, Baking trays, Oven mitts/gloves, Saucepan, Rubber Spatula, Piping Bag, Hand Mixer, Whisk Attachment, Rolling Pin, Square Frame (30 x 90 mm), Offset spatula, Pastry Brush.
    • Key Learning: Making a Jaconde, Making Caramelized nut garnishes.
