118. Puff Pastry (Advanced Application)

Category: Pastry / Viennoiserie | Prep: 55 mins (15 min + 20 min + 20 min rests) | Cook/Bake: N/A (base dough) | Yield: 300 g

Ingredients

  • 200 g Flour (AP)
  • ½ tsp Vinegar
  • ½ tsp Salt
  • 1 tsp Sugar
  • 120 g Water
  • 10 g Butter (melted)
  • 100 g Croissant Butter

Method

  1. Make elastic semi-soft dough using flour, salt, sugar, melted butter and water.
  2. Rest for 15 min.
  3. Roll the croissant butter to a flat square slab.
  4. Roll the dough to form a rough square, ensuring that the sides are thinner than the center and it is big enough to envelope the margarine slab.
  5. Place the block of margarine diagonally in the center and fold the corners of the dough over the margarine like an envelope, tucking the edges to completely enclose the butter.
  6. Roll the dough to about ¾ inches thick and approximately three times as long as wide. Fold the bottom 1/3rd & the top 1/3rd to the center. This is a single/three fold.
  7. Rest the dough covered in the chiller for about 20 minutes.
  8. Roll the dough to about ¾ inches thick and approximately three times as long as wide.
  9. Fold the bottom 1/4th & the top 1/4th to the center and then fold from the center. This is a double/book/four fold.
  10. Rest the dough in the chiller for 20 minutes wrapped in cling film (to ensure that the dough doesn’t dry out, else cracks will develop on the surface).

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: N/A (unbaked laminated dough for advanced applications)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Elastic semi-soft dough with smooth, crack-free surface enclosing interleaved layers of butter and dough
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Rest wrapped in cling film in the chiller so the surface does not dry out or crack
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Puff pastry is a light and flaky pastry made with the lamination method which involves enclosing a block of butter in sheeted dough and folding it over itself to achieve interleaved layers of butter and dough.
      3. The layers of butter melt when puff pastry dough is baked and this provides for leavening of the dough when the water in the butter turns to steam during the baking process.
      4. The dough is given 2 single and 2 double fold in this recipe. The folding methods may differ. The most often used alternates are 5 or 6 single folds or 3 doubles instead of the above.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Cling film, Pastry Brush, Rolling Pin, Dough Sheeter.
    • Key Learning: Lamination.
↑ Back to top

119. Butter Cream

Category: Pastry / Confectionery | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: Cook syrup to 121°C | Yield: 150 g

Ingredients

  • 100 g Sugar (handwritten note next to ingredient: 400)
  • 20 g Water (handwritten note next to ingredient: 80)
  • 3.5 g Glucose syrup (handwritten note next to ingredient: 14)
  • 50 g Egg (handwritten note next to ingredient: 200)
  • 30 g Egg white (handwritten note next to ingredient: 120)
  • 1/8 tsp Salt
  • ¼ tsp Vanilla extract
  • 225 g Butter (handwritten note next to ingredient: 900)

Method

  1. Cook sugar, water and glucose syrup to 121°C.
  2. Whip whole eggs with vanilla and add the sugar syrup.
  3. Whip until it is lukewarm and add chopped butter little by little.
  4. Mix until combined. Keep in chiller until use.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 121°C (250°F) for sugar syrup
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Whipped until lukewarm before adding butter; smooth, combined emulsion without melting the butter
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Keep in chiller until use
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Butter cream is most commonly used as a filling or used to decorate cakes.
      3. There are 2 types of meringue based butter cream; Italian and Swiss. The meringue has to cool down considerably (warm or cool to the touch) and only then the butter is added in so that it does not melt when it is beaten in.
      4. A French version of butter cream is made with egg yolks.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Whisk, Cling film, Baking trays, Rubber Spatula, Piping Bag, Hand Mixer.
    • Key Learning: Making buttercream.

120. Cream Patissiere (Advanced Application)

Category: Pastry | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: Bring to a boil | Yield: 215 g

Ingredients

  • 75 ml Milk
  • 75 ml Cream
  • 35 g Sugar
  • 10 g Corn starch
  • ½ tsp / 2 g Vanilla
  • 1 pc / 18 g Egg yolk
  • 5 g Butter

Method

  1. Mix the milk and cream together and bring ¾ of the mixture to a boil in a saucepan.
  2. To a mixing bowl add sugar, cornstarch, vanilla, egg yolk, and 1/4 of cold milk mixture and mix until combined.
  3. Pour the hot mixture on the egg yolk-sugar mixture, whisking continuously.
  4. Pour it back in the saucepan and bring it to boil, while continuously mixing.
  5. Cook until it bubbles right in the center and pour into a clean mixing bowl.
  6. Keep whisking until it is lukewarm and then mix in butter.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Boil (100°C / 212°F) until it bubbles right in the center
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Smooth, thickened pastry cream; whisked until lukewarm before butter is incorporated
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Cover with a cartouche or cling film touching the surface and refrigerate until used; whisk until smooth before use
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Cover the Crème Pâtissière with a cartouche or cling film touching the surface and refrigerate until used.
      3. Whisk until smooth before use.
      4. Pastry cream is versatile and can be easily flavoured with the classic vanilla, liquor, coffee, chocolate and even fruit purees.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Measuring jugs, Mixing bowls, Sauce pan, Rubber spatula, Whisk, Cling film.
    • Key Learning: Boiling, Whisking.
↑ Back to top

121. Mille-feuilles Slice

Category: Pastry / Plated Dessert | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 20–25 mins + 10 mins | Yield: 11 X 4cm. 2 portions

Ingredients

A) Base

  • 200 g Puff Pastry

B) Cream Mousseline Filling

  • 125 g Butter cream
  • 40 g Pastry cream
  • 40 g Whipped cream

C) Feuilletine Crunch

  • 125 g Praline paste
  • 40 g Milk chocolate
  • 25 g Butter
  • 65 g Paille feuilletine

D) Finishing

  • 80 g Cream Chantilly

Method

For the Base

  1. Roll 120 x 250 x 3 mm rectangular sheet from puff pastry.
  2. Dock both side and place baking paper on the bottom and top and place a tray on top.
  3. Bake at 180°C for 20–25 minutes.
  4. Take the tray off and bake for 10 minutes more.

For the Cream Mousseline Filling

  1. Beat cream patisserie until smooth.
  2. Whip cream.
  3. Mix butter cream smooth and fluffy and add the cream patisserie little by little.
  4. Fold in whipped cream.
  5. Put the mousseline in piping bag fitted with 8 mm nozzle.

For the Feuilletine Crunch

  1. Mix praline paste and melted chocolate until smooth.
  2. Add soft (not melted) butter.
  3. Fold in paille feuilletine.
  4. Spread it to a 4–5 mm thick rectangular shaped sheet (120 x 90 mm).
  5. Set it in the chiller just until set.
  6. Cut to 110 x 40 cm [mm] pcs (2 pcs). Place back to the freezer to get rock hard.

For the Assembly

  1. Keep the following ready and in the chiller until required to serve:
    • Feuilletage (baked puff pastry)
    • Mousseline (piped)
    • Feuilletine crunch
    • Mousseline (piped)
    • Feuillatage
    • Mousseline (piped)
    • Feuillatage

For the Finishing

  1. Place mille-feuille slice on the plate as per picture.
  2. Pipe cream Chantilly on the top with flat nozzle.
  3. Decorate with chocolate, fresh berries, praline or nuts.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 180°C (356°F) for 20–25 minutes weighted with a tray, plus 10 minutes uncovered
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Flat, crisp baked feuilletage; smooth and fluffy mousseline; rock-hard frozen feuilletine crunch
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Keep components in the chiller until required to serve
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. A mille-feuille is traditionally made of three layers of puff pastry with pastry cream between the layers.
      3. The top pastry layer is also sometimes piped beautifully with the pastry cream and then garnished with a chocolate drizzle, cocoa powder and even sliced toasted nuts.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Whisk, Aluminium foil, Cling film, Baking trays, Oven mitts/gloves, Saucepan, Rubber Spatula, Piping Bag, Hand Mixer, Whisk Attachment, Rolling Pin, Bread Knife, Offset Spatula.
    • Key Learning: Making Cream Mousseline, Advanced application of Puff Pastry, Layering.
↑ Back to top

122. Sausage Rolls

Category: Savory / Pastry | Prep: 5–10 mins (resting) | Cook/Bake: 20–25 mins | Yield: 4 portions

Ingredients

  • 160 g Puff Pastry
  • 4 pcs Frankfurter
  • 25 g Egg wash

Method

  1. Roll puff pastry to 2–3 mm thickness.
  2. Place the sausage in the middle and egg wash 1 side.
  3. Turn the dough over the sausage and press to seal on the side.
  4. Slit the top of the pastry with a sharp knife (3–5 times) and egg wash the top.
  5. Place on baking sheets and rest for 5–10 minutes.
  6. Bake it at 180°C for 20–25 minutes and serve warm.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 180°C (356°F) for 20–25 minutes
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Puffed, golden brown flaky pastry casing around sausage
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Serve warm after baking
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. A sausage roll is a savoury snack popular worldwide. It is essentially a sausage wrapped in a pastry dough (like puff pastry) and baked.
      3. They are sold at various places from bakeries to cafes.
      4. It gained popularity in the 1800s as a cheap, quick and easy street food in the UK and slowly came to be known as a “British snack” even though most reports say that the modern version of the sausage roll may have originated in France.
      5. Sausages can be cut half, getting a smaller size. In some regions and countries, sausage roll is filled with raw minced meat (mixed with spices and egg).
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Baking trays, Baking Paper, Paring Knife, Oven mitts/gloves, Rolling Pin, Pastry Brush.
    • Key Learning: Advanced application of Puff Pastry.

123. Cream Horns

Category: Pastry | Prep: Chill until firm and egg wash is dry | Cook/Bake: 12–15 mins | Yield: 8 - 10 portions

Ingredients

  • 200 g Puff pastry sheet
  • 1 pc Egg wash
  • 50 g Caster sugar
  • 110 g Cream Chantilly

Method

  1. Roll the chilled and rested dough to a rectangular shape of 2–3 mm thickness. Cut this rectangle into 2–3 cm wide long strip, about 15–20 cm long.
  2. Wash them with egg wash and wrap the strips around a metal roll or cone mold, making sure that the side washed with egg is on the outside. When wrapping the strip around the cone, each subsequent layer must overlap its previous to ensure a tight and beautiful cone.
  3. Chill the prepared cones in the fridge, until the dough is firm and the egg wash is dry.
  4. Brush the cones again with egg wash, roll the surface in caster sugar and place them onto a baking sheet.
  5. Bake them at 180–200°C for 12–15 minutes or until golden brown.
  6. Remove the roll or cone molds while they are hot and allow the pastry to cool down.
  7. Fill cream chantilly into a piping bag with a star nozzle and pipe into the prepared cones.
  8. Serve chilled, can be dusted with icing sugar.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 180–200°C (356–392°F) for 12–15 minutes
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Tightly overlapped cone shape, golden brown and sugar-crusted
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Serve chilled
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. A cream horn is a pastry made of puff pastry baked on a cone or “horn” shape that is filled with whipped cream.
      3. Optionally, raspberry or strawberry jam can be spread inside (to prevent the dough get soggy) before filling.
      4. Can be filled with savory fillings. In this case we skip the caster sugar layer on the top.
      5. The shape is made by wrapping the puff pastry around a cone shaped mould and baking it. Once it is baked and the mould is removed the cream is piped in and it is ready to be served.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Chef’s Knife, Pastry Brush, Cone Mold, Baking trays, Oven mitts/gloves, Aluminium Foil, Piping Bag, Star Nozzle.
    • Key Learning: Advanced application of Puff Pastry.

124. Gallette des Rois / Almond Pethiviers

Category: Pastry | Prep: Chill until hard | Cook/Bake: 30–35 mins | Yield: 4 - 5 portions

Ingredients

A) Pastry

  • 200 g Puff pastry sheet
  • 25 g Egg Wash

B) Almond Cream / Frangipane

  • 50 g Butter
  • 50 g Icing sugar
  • 50 g Eggs
  • 12 g Flour
  • 50 g Almond powder

Method

For the Pastry

  1. Roll puff pastry to 2–3 mm thickness.
  2. Cut 1 smaller and 1 larger disc with plain round cutters.

For the Almond Cream / Frangipane

  1. Cream butter and icing sugar until fluffy and pale in colour.
  2. Beat the eggs and in a separate bowl, mix the flour and almond powder.
  3. Add the egg and flour, alternating with each other, in 3 additions to the butter and icing mixture. Mix until just combined and place the cream into a piping bag.

For Assembly and Finishing

  1. Brush smaller disc with egg.
  2. Pipe the almond cream on it.
  3. Shape the other disc and place on the top of the almond cream.
  4. Crimp the side and chill it.
  5. When hard, lightly score the surface with a paring knife in the classic pethivier pattern.
  6. Bake at 180°C for 30–35 minutes or until done.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 180°C (356°F) for 30–35 minutes
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Fluffy, pale almond cream; puffed golden pastry scored in the classic Pithiviers spiral pattern
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Literally translating to “Cake fit for a king”, Galette de Rhoix, is typically made in France for the celebration of three kings, celebrated on the 18th of January.
      3. In most pethiviers, a figurine is placed in the filling before it is covered with the second layer of pastry. After the cake is cut, it is tradition that whoever finds this figurine wins a prize.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Whisk, Aluminium foil, Cling film, Baking trays, Oven mitts/gloves, Rubber Spatula, Piping Bag, Hand Mixer, Whisk Attachment, Paring Knife, Chef’s Knife, Pastry Brush.
    • Key Learning: Advanced application of Puff Pastry.
↑ Back to top

125. Vol au Vent / Bouche

Category: Savory / Pastry | Prep: Freeze cut discs before baking | Cook/Bake: 25–30 mins | Yield: 4 - 6 portions

Ingredients

A) Pastry

  • 200 g Puff pastry sheet
  • 25 g Egg Wash

B) Savoury Filling

  • 50 g White onion
  • 30 g Leek
  • ½ pcs Red pepper
  • ½ pcs Green pepper
  • ½ pcs Yellow pepper
  • 3 pcs Gherkins
  • 6 pcs Mushroom
  • 60 g Veal or beef bacon
  • 60 g Butter
  • 2 tbsp Flour
  • A/R Salt
  • A/R Black pepper
  • 300 ml Cream
  • 1 tbsp Mustard
  • ½ spring Parsley

Method

For the Pastry

  1. Roll puff pastry to 2–3 mm thickness.
  2. Cut with 60 mm plain round cutter and freeze it.
  3. Cut the middle of every other disc with a 30 mm plain round cutter and stick 1 full disc and 1 ring together with egg wash.
  4. Brush the top with egg wash and bake at 160°C to 25–30 minutes or until the pastries turn a golden brown on the surface.
  5. Allow them to cool down.

For the Savoury Filling

  1. Dice the bacon, gherkin and peppers.
  2. Slice the leek and chop the onions.
  3. Melt butter in a large sauce pan and add onion and salt.
  4. Cook for a little while and add bacon.
  5. Add the leek, peppers, gherkins and cook on low heat until all the moisture evaporates.
  6. Add the parsley, flour and mix well. Take the saucepan off the heat and add cream and mix.
  7. Bring the saucepan back to the stove and cook on low heat.
  8. Add mustard, pepper and more salt if required.
  9. Set aside until the pastries are ready to be filled.
  10. Before serving, boil again and add more cream if it becomes too thick.

For Assembly and Finishing

  1. Fill the Vol au Vent with the savoury filling.
  2. Serve immediately.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 160°C (320°F) for 25–30 minutes
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Hollow, light puff pastry casing turned golden brown on the surface; creamy savoury filling
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Fill and serve immediately
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Literally translates to “windblown” to describe the lightness of the hollow casing of puff pastry.
      3. Usually served as an appetizer or a small snack filled with a savoury chicken, meat, seafood or vegetable filling.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Whisk, Aluminium foil, Cling film, Baking trays, Oven mitts/gloves, Saucepan, Rubber Spatula, Round cutters, Paring Knife, Pastry Brush.
    • Key Learning: Advanced application of Puff Pastry, Making a savoury filling.

126. Choux Pastry (Advanced Application)

Category: Pastry | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 15 mins + 5 mins + 7–8 mins (27–28 mins total) | Yield: 20 - 25 portions

Ingredients

  • 100 g Water
  • 40 g Butter
  • 5 g Sugar
  • 2 g Salt
  • 60 g Flour (AP)
  • 80-100 g Eggs

Method

  1. In a saucepan bring (chopped) butter, water, sugar and salt to a boil.
  2. Take off the heat and add flour & stir it until smooth. Place back to the heat and cook it mixing vigorously (so that there are no lumps) until the mixture forms dough and leaves the sides of the pan (about a minute).
  3. Transfer the mixture in to a mixing bowl and mix it with a spatula for about a minute, until cools down a bit. Add the beaten eggs little by little, until get the right consistency (might not need all the eggs).
  4. Pipe the mixture on to non stick trays with a piping bag fitted with (10mm) plain or star nozzle.
  5. Preheat the oven to 170°C and start with a water injection for 30 seconds. Then, bake at 170°C for 15 minutes with fan off and then for a further 5 minutes, with the vent open for 5 minutes (or until cooked).
  6. Continue the cooking on the same temperature (160°C) for next 7–8 minutes with opened vent (alternatively, open the door slightly) in order to make the crust crispy and get rid of the majority of the water.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 170°C (338°F) with 30-second water injection for 15 mins (fan off) + 5 mins (vent open), followed by 160°C (320°F) for 7–8 mins with open vent
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Panade leaves sides of pan without lumps; baked shells must be very crisp so they do not become soggy and soft when filled
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Top of the row [raw] product can be made smooth using a fork dipped in cold water.
      3. The choux pastry must be baked very crisp or it will become soggy and soft when filled. If the pastry is not crisp, return to the oven and continue to bake. The initial heat must be high to puff the product, then the heat must be reduced to help the product to crisp, without becoming too brown.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring jug, Mixing bowl, Baking tray, Baking Paper, Oven mitt/gloves, Sauce pan, Rubber spatula, Pastry brush, Piping Bag, Flat tip nozzle.
    • Key Learning: Making Choux Pastry, Leavening through Steam.
↑ Back to top

127. Gateau St Honore

Category: Entremet / Pastry | Prep: Freeze base before baking | Cook/Bake: 30–35 mins | Yield: 20 - 25 portions

Ingredients

A) St Honore Base

  • 200 g Pate choux
  • 1 ea Puff Pastry disc (16 cm)
  • 150 g White compound
  • A/R Orange Colour

B) Filling

  • 1 pcs Orange segments

C) Orange Chiboust

  • 1 orange Orange Zest
  • 75 ml Milk
  • 75 ml Cream
  • 35 g Sugar 1
  • 1 ea Egg Yolk
  • 10 g Corn flour
  • ½ pc Gelatin Sheet
  • 5 g Butter

D) Italian Meringue

  • 25 g Caster sugar
  • 1 pc Egg whites
  • 10 g Water

E) Cream Chantilly

  • 200 g Whipping Cream
  • 20 g Icing Sugar
  • 1 tsp Vanilla Extract

F) Finishing

  • A/R Dark Chocolate Decoration

Method

For the St Honore Base

  1. Roll puff pastry to 2–3 mm thick sheet, dock it and cut it with 160 mm ring.
  2. Freeze it.
  3. Pipe pate choux on top with 10 mm nozzle. Freeze it.
  4. Bake it at 180°C for 30–35 minutes with pastry grid.
  5. Pipe small round choux-s from the left over pate choux on the small choux Silpat and bake it as usual. Allow to cool down.

For the Orange Chiboust

  1. Make a cream patisserie as usual and add soaked and drained gelatin in the end. Allow to cool it down.
  2. Make the Italian Meringue and add it to the smooth cream patisserie in 2 additions and fold together.
  3. Place a little portion to the piping bag and fill the choux buns with it. Pipe the rest into the center of the base.
  4. Spread it and allow it to cool down.

For the Assembly and Finishing

  1. Fill the choux buns with orange chiboust.
  2. Melt white compound and color with orange coloring.
  3. Dip the choux buns in them and allow to set.
  4. Stick the choux buns to the edge of the base using the orange colored compound chocolate.
  5. Place the cake on a plate.
  6. Place orange segments on the top of chiboust and pipe cream Chantilly to the surface to cover all the filling, using a Saint-Honore nozzle.
  7. Decorate with dark chocolate decoration.
  8. Keep in the chiller until serve.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 180°C (356°F) for 30–35 minutes with pastry grid for base; small choux buns baked as usual
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Crisp puff and choux base; light, airy Orange Chiboust; set compound coating on choux buns
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Keep in the chiller until serving
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. It is a dessert that is named after the French Saint (Saint Honore) who was a patron of French desserts and pastries.
      3. This dessert was created in the mid 1800s at the Chiboust bakery in Paris.
      4. Classically, this gateau is made up of a puff pastry base with a ring of choux pastry piped along the edge. Small choux buns are dipped in caramelized sugar and attached on the baked base and filled with chiboust cream.
      5. The base is also filled with chiboust cream and traditionally finished with cream Chantilly or whipped cream using a special St. Honore piping tip.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Whisk, Rubber Spatula, Cling film, Baking trays, Oven mitts/gloves, Saucepan, Pastry Brush, Piping Bag, Hand Mixer, Whisk Attachment, St Honore piping tip.
    • Key Learning: Advanced application of Puff pastry, Advanced application of Choux Pastry, Making Cream Chibouste, Piping.

128. Gianduja Religieuse

Category: Pastry | Prep: Freeze craquelin discs before baking | Cook/Bake: Small buns: 18–19 mins total; Large buns: 23 mins total | Yield: 2 portions

Ingredients

A) Chocolate Craquelin / Sablage / Crustinion

  • 18 g Butter
  • 22 g Light brown sugar
  • 2.5 g Cocoa powder
  • 20 g Flour

B) Choux Pastry

  • 100 g Pate choux

C) Dark Chocolate Ganache

  • 50 g Dark chocolate 70%
  • 50 g Cream

D) Cream Diplomat Gianduja

  • 200 g Cream Patisserie
  • 100 g Cream Chantilly
  • 40 g Gianduja Paste

E) Cream Chantilly (for decoration)

  • 75 ml Whipping Cream
  • 10 g Icing Sugar
  • ½ tsp Vanilla Extract

F) Finishing

  • 35 g Whole roasted hazelnut

Method

For the Chocolate Craquelin / Sablage / Crustinion

  1. Mix all ingredients together until it forms a paste.
  2. Roll out to 1–2 mm thickness in between baking paper or cling film.
  3. Cut 6 discs of 30 mm diameter and 6 discs of 50 mm diameter and freeze.

For the Choux Pastry

  1. Pipe 6 large and 6 small choux buns on separate trays.
  2. Place the smaller chocolate sablage disc on the top of the smaller choux buns and the larger discs onto the larger choux buns.
  3. Bake the choux buns at:
    • Small: 200°C for 4 minutes, 20 sec water sprinkling; 160°C for 7 minutes; then 160°C for 7–8 minutes, vent open.
    • Large: 200°C for 6 minutes, 30 sec water sprinkling; 180°C for 5 minutes; then 160°C for 12 minutes, vent open.

For the Dark Chocolate Ganache

  1. Scald the cream and pour over the dark chocolate and allow the mixture to stand for a minute.
  2. Then, mix thoroughly and make sure it is of smooth texture and consistency.
  3. If the chocolate does not melt through, place the mixture on a bain marie to help the chunks of chocolate and melt and form a smooth mixture.

For the Cream Diplomat Gianduja

  1. Make cream patisserie and cream chantilly.
  2. Beat the cream patisserie until smooth and add gianduja paste.
  3. Mix in a little cream chantilly first and then fold in the rest.
  4. Place the cream diplomat to piping bag with a 10 mm nozzle.

For the Cream Chantilly (for decoration)

  1. Make Chantilly for decoration and place it to piping bag with the Saint-Honore nozzle.

For the Assembly

  1. Cut a round disc on the bottom of each choux with a sharp paring knife and keep it next to the buns.
  2. Place buns upside down.
  3. Pipe chocolate ganache 1/3 rd of the choux buns and allow to set in the fridge.
  4. Place 5 pieces roasted hazelnut in the large buns and 3 pieces in the small ones.
  5. Pipe cream diplomat on ganache until overruns the top.
  6. Knock them gently and spread the cream flat.
  7. Place the cut bottom back and make it flat.
  8. Set them in the fridge.

For the Finishing

  1. Place the large buns on serving plate (sablage side up).
  2. Pipe Chantilly cream on top of the choux bun, from the side to the centre until it form a complete circle. It should look almost like a collar of a gown.
  3. Place the small buns in the middle of the piped chantilly. Place 1 piece of hazelnut on the top with a little ganache.
  4. Keep in the chiller until serve.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Small buns: 200°C (392°F) for 4 mins with 20s steam, 160°C (320°F) for 7 mins, 160°C (320°F) for 7–8 mins vent open. Large buns: 200°C (392°F) for 6 mins with 30s steam, 180°C (356°F) for 5 mins, 160°C (320°F) for 12 mins vent open
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Crisp craquelin-topped choux buns; smooth ganache and gianduja diplomat cream; piped Chantilly collar resembling a nun’s habit collar
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Keep in the chiller until serving
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Religieuse is a classic French dessert composed of two choux pastry buns.
      3. The pastry is named and designed after nuns. This dessert took its final form (that we recognize today) in the early 18th century, in the kitchens of Chef Marie-Antoine Careme.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Whisk, Cling film, Baking trays, Oven mitts/gloves, Saucepan, Rubber Spatula, Piping Bag, Round Nozzle 10mm, St Honore Nozzle, Hand Mixer, Whisk Attachment.
    • Key Learning: Making a Sablage, Making a flavoured cream diplomat, Piping, Filling.
↑ Back to top

129. Paris Brest

Category: Pastry | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 23 mins total (5 mins + 8 mins + 10 mins) | Yield: 2 portions

Ingredients

A) Paris Brest Shells

  • 60 g Pate choux
  • 10 g Egg wash
  • 2 g Slivered almonds

B) Praline Cream

  • 200 g Whipping cream
  • 20 g Icing sugar
  • 30 g Praline paste

C) Finishing

  • A/R Icing sugar / Niege Décor

Method

For the Paris Brest Shells

  1. Pipe pate choux, with a 10 mm nozzle, into rings.
  2. Egg wash the top and sprinkle with sliced almonds.
  3. Bake at:
    • 200°C for 5 minutes, 30 seconds water sprinkling
    • 160°C for 8 minutes; then
    • 160°C for 10 minutes, venting valve open

For the Praline Cream

  1. Make cream chantilly and slowly incorporate the praline paste.
  2. Place the praline cream in a piping bag with a star nozzle.

For the Finishing

  1. When the pate choux rings have cooled down, cut it in half and pipe in the praline cream.
  2. Place the top on and dust it with icing sugar.
  3. Keep in the chiller until serve.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 200°C (392°F) for 5 mins with 30s water sprinkling; 160°C (320°F) for 8 mins; 160°C (320°F) for 10 mins with venting valve open
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Puffed, crisp wheel-shaped choux ring topped with toasted almonds; light praline-flavoured Chantilly cream
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Keep in the chiller until serving
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Paris brest is a French dessert made of choux pastry and a praline flavoured cream filling.
      3. The shape of this pastry is that of a ring to represent a “wheel” to commemorate a bicycle race which was started in the early 1900s.
      4. The dessert became popular with the bicyclists due to its high calorific value and finally became popular with everyone all over France.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Whisk, Cling film, Baking trays, Oven mitts/gloves, Rubber Spatula, Piping Bag, Round Nozzle 10mm, Star Nozzle, Hand Mixer, Whisk Attachment, Sifter, Pastry Brush.
    • Key Learning: Piping, Filling, Making a flavoured cream chantilly.

130. Churros

Category: Pastry / Plated Dessert | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: Deep fry at 150°C until puffed and golden brown | Yield: 25 – 30 pcs

Ingredients

A) Churros Dough

  • 65 ml Water
  • 60 ml Milk
  • 50 g Butter
  • 5 g Sugar
  • ¼ tsp Salt
  • 60 g Flour (AP)
  • 50 g Eggs

B) Chocolate Sauce

  • 150 ml Cream
  • 150 ml Milk I.
  • 50 ml Milk II.
  • 50 g Sugar
  • 25 g Cocoa powder
  • ½ tsp Corn starch
  • 100 g Vanilla extract
  • ¼ tsp Nescafe

C) Finishing

  • A/R Oil (for deep frying)
  • A/R Cinnamon Sugar

Method

For the Churros Dough

  1. In a saucepan bring (chopped) butter, milk, water, sugar and salt to a boil.
  2. Take off the heat add flour & stir it until smooth. Place back on the heat and cook it mixing vigorously (so that there are no lumps) until the mixture forms a dough and leaves the sides of the pan (about a minute).
  3. Transfer the mixture in to a mixing bowl and mix it with a spatula/paddle for about a minute, until cools down a bit.
  4. Alternatively, it can be spread on the working surface lined with cling film for a few minutes.
  5. Transfer back to the bowl when cold.
  6. Add the beaten eggs little by little, until you get the right consistency (might not need all the eggs).
  7. Heat deep frying oil to 150°C (checked with thermometer).
  8. Pipe 100–120 mm long pieces to the oil and cut the ends with scissors dipped in the hot oil.
  9. Cook both sides until puffs up and has a golden brown color.
  10. Make sure that the temperature of the oil is maintained on the required temperature.

For the Chocolate Sauce

  1. Boil milk I., cream, Nescafe and vanilla in a large saucepan.
  2. Whisk together corn starch, cocoa powder, sugar and milk II. [printed as milk I.] in a mixing bowl.
  3. Pour hot liquid to the cocoa mixture, mix until combines and pour it back to the saucepan.
  4. Boil it and cook for a few seconds while continuously mixing.
  5. Take off the heat and add chocolate.
  6. Mix until melts.
  7. Keep warm until serve or cool it down and keep refrigerated for no longer, than 5 days.

For the Finishing

  1. Place the hot fried churros on a kitchen towel to drain off any excess oil and then roll them in cinnamon sugar while hot.
  2. Serve immediately with chocolate sauce.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 150°C (302°F) deep-frying oil temperature (checked with thermometer)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Puffed up and golden brown on both sides; rolled in cinnamon sugar while hot
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Serve churros immediately; chocolate sauce can be kept refrigerated for no longer than 5 days
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Churros are a popular fried dough dessert in Spanish and Portuguese cuisine.
      3. They are classically served and eaten with chocolate sauce, dulce de leche or café con leche.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Whisk, Piping Bag, Star Nozzle, Cling film, Baking Paper, Saucepan, Spider, Rubber Spatula, Hand Mixer, Whisk Attachment.
    • Key Learning: Making a fried dessert, Piping.

131. Short bread with run outs

Category: Pastry / Confectionery | Prep: Chill dough twice + dry icing overnight | Cook/Bake: 8–12 mins | Yield: 25 – 30 pcs

Ingredients

A) Shortbread Cookies

  • 62 g Butter
  • 25 g Icing sugar
  • 30 g Corn flour
  • 65 g A P flour
  • 1 pinch Salt
  • 1/8 tsp Vanilla extract
  • 1/8 tsp Lemon zest

B) Royal Icing

  • 30 g Egg white
  • 160-170 g Extra fine icing sugar
  • 1 tsp Lemon juice

Method

For the Shortbread Cookies

  1. Cream soft butter, vanilla, lemon zest and icing sugar.
  2. Sieve corn flour and flour together and add the salt.
  3. Add dry mix to the butter and mix until it is smooth.
  4. Roll it to 5–7 mm thick sheet in between 2 baking paper and chill.
  5. Cut dough to shapes and chill again.
  6. Bake them at 160°C for 8–12 minutes.

For the Royal Icing

  1. Mix egg white and lemon juice and start to mix with paddle on the lowest speed.
  2. Gradually add as much sugar as it takes to get to a piping consistency.
  3. Liquid food coloring can be added.
  4. Transfer to a clean container and seal with cling film.

For the Finishing

  1. Pour little quantity of royal icing in piping bag and pipe a line around the cookies, following the motive.
  2. Allow them to dry.
  3. Some more egg white can be added to the rest of the royal icing to make it softer, almost runny.
  4. Pipe the runny icing inside the frames and allow them to dry overnight.
  5. Keep them air tight.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 160°C (320°F) for 8–12 minutes
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Smooth, unleavened shortbread cookie; piped royal icing border with flooded runny icing center dried overnight
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Keep in an airtight container once dried overnight
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Traditionally of Scottish origin, unlike most other biscuits and cookies, shortbread does not have any leavening agents.
      3. It is most commonly associated with Christmas festivities and the holiday season.
      4. Royal icing is made of egg whites and icing sugar and is used to decorate cookies, cakes and gingerbread houses.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Whisk, Aluminium foil, Cling film, Baking trays, Oven mitts/gloves, Saucepan, Rubber Spatula, Baking Paper, Hand Mixer, Whisk Attachment.
    • Key Learning: Creaming Method, Making Royal Icing, Piping, Flooding.
↑ Back to top

132. Checkerboard Cookies

Category: Pastry | Prep: ~2 hours (chilling doughs & assembled block) | Cook/Bake: 8–12 mins | Yield: 25 – 30 pcs

Ingredients

A) Vanilla Base

  • 125 g Flour
  • 75 g Butter
  • 60 g Icing sugar
  • 1 pcs Egg yolk
  • 1 tsp Vanilla extract

B) Chocolate Base

  • 110 g Flour
  • 15 g Cocoa powder
  • 75 g Butter
  • 60 g Icing sugar
  • 1 pcs Egg yolk
  • ½ tsp Vanilla extract

Method

For the Vanilla Base

  1. To make the vanilla base, cream the butter, sifted icing sugar, and vanilla. Once light and fluffy, add egg yolk and mix well.
  2. Add in the sifted flour and combine to form a smooth dough; wrap in cling film and rest in the refrigerator.

For the Chocolate Base

  1. Similarly make the chocolate base. (Sift the flour and cocoa powder together until uniformly mixed, before adding to the creamed mixture).

For the Assembly and Finishing

  1. Sheet both the doughs to 1 cm thick rectangular shapes, brush one surface with egg white, and stick both the layers together.
  2. Chill in the refrigerator for about an hour, or until firm.
  3. Using a parallel wheel pastry cutter, mark straight lines and cut 1 cm wide strips.
  4. Lay the first strip with the chocolate dough on the top, then lay the next strip down with the vanilla side up and stick them together with egg white.
  5. Lay the next strip down with the chocolate dough on the top and stick it to the first two strips.
  6. Build the next two [printed as tow] layer similarly, repeating the same process and alternating the colours to form a long square block.
  7. Wrap in cling film and refrigerate once again until firm.
  8. Cut the long block into 8 mm thick slices and lay down on a lightly greased or lined tray.
  9. Bake in the preheated oven at 160°C for 8–12 minutes.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 160°C (320°F) for 8–12 minutes
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Crisp, clean alternating checkerboard grid of vanilla and chocolate dough slices
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Checkerboard cookies are called such since they have alternate strips of vanilla and chocolate dough giving it the appearance of a checkerboard.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Whisk, Cling film, Baking trays, Oven mitts/gloves, Chef’s Knife, Rubber Spatula, Chopping Board, Pastry Brush, Hand Mixer.
    • Key Learning: Making a checker board pattern, Creaming Method, Baking.

133. Almond Financier

Category: Pastry | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: Toast almonds at 160°C; Bake financiers at 160°C | Yield: 40 pcs

Ingredients

  • 75 g Butter
  • 125 g Roasted Almond powder
  • 125 g Egg white
  • 60 g Icing sugar
  • 37.5 g All-purpose flour
  • 60 g Caster Sugar

Method

  1. Melt the butter in a sauce pan and cook until it turns brown in color to make Beurre Noisette; strain and cool.
  2. Spread the almond powder on a baking tray and toast until light golden brown in a preheated oven at 160°C.
  3. Place egg white in a bowl and whisk.
  4. Add sifted flour and icing sugar and roasted almond powder to the egg white and mix well with a whisk.
  5. Temper the brown butter with some financier mixture, and then fold it into the financier mixture.
  6. Pipe the mixture in silicone or buttered metal financier moulds.
  7. Bake in a preheated oven at 160°C, until pale golden in colour.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 160°C (320°F) for toasting almond powder and baking financiers
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Browned butter (Beurre Noisette); light golden brown toasted almond powder; baked until pale golden in colour
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Almond powder could be replaced with pistachio powder or hazelnut powder for variation.
      3. The name “financier” is derived from the rectangular mold which resembles a bar of gold.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Whisk, Cling film, Baking trays, Financier Mold, Oven mitts/gloves, Saucepan, Rubber Spatula, Hand Mixer, Whisk Attachment.
    • Key Learning: Browning butter, Baking.

Ingredients

  • 115 g Sugar
  • 1.5 g Lemon zest
  • 2 g / ½ tsp Vanilla
  • 115 g All-purpose flour

  • A/R Butter spray for mold

Method

Note: In the printed recipe book (page 131 / PDF page 221), the Method column duplicates the Almond Financier steps verbatim as follows:

  1. Melt the butter in a sauce pan and cook until it turns brown in color to make Beurre Noisette; strain and cool.
  2. Spread the almond powder on a baking tray and toast until light golden brown in a preheated oven at 160°C.
  3. Place egg white in a bowl and whisk.
  4. Add sifted flour and icing sugar and roasted almond powder to the egg white and mix well with a whisk.
  5. Temper the brown butter with some financier mixture, and then fold it into the financier mixture.

135. Rhubarb Crumble Bar

Category: Pastry | Prep: Chill dough and rhubarb layer | Cook/Bake: 10–15 mins (base at 160°C) + 10–12 mins (crumble at 180°C) | Yield: 20cm x 20cm x 1cm / 12-16 pcs / 940 g

Ingredients

A) Shortbread Base

  • 125 g Butter
  • 50 g Icing sugar
  • 2 g / ½ tsp Vanilla
  • 2 g Lemon zest
  • 130 g All-purpose flour
  • 60 g Cornstarch
  • 1/8 tsp Salt

B) Rhubarb Mixture

  • 110 g Rhubarb puree
  • 110 g Strawberry puree
  • 180 g Sugar
  • 2 g / ½ tsp Vanilla

C) Pistachio Crumble

  • 45 g Butter
  • 45 g Light brown sugar
  • 45 g All-purpose flour
  • 45 g Pistachio powder

Method

For the Shortbread Base

  1. Preheat the oven to 160°C.
  2. Cream softened butter, icing sugar, vanilla, and lemon zest, until pale and fluffy.
  3. Sift flour and cornstarch together and mix in the salt.
  4. Add dry ingredients to the creamed mixture and mix to form a soft dough.
  5. Sheet the dough to a 5–7 mm thick sheet in between 2 sheets of baking paper and chill.
  6. Peel off the upper sheet of baking paper and place the lower sheet with the dough on a baking tray.
  7. Press the frame into the sheet of dough and bake in the preheated oven for 10–15 minutes.

For the Rhubarb Mixture

  1. Place rhubarb puree, strawberry puree, sugar, and vanilla in sauce pan and cook to 106°C.
  2. Pour the mixture on the cooked short bread and spread it evenly.
  3. Place in the refrigerator to set, before putting the crumble on top.

For the Pistachio Crumble

  1. Preheat the oven to 180°C.
  2. Chop cold butter to small cubes and refrigerate or freeze.
  3. Add all the crumble ingredients together and mix together with the paddle attachment or with finger tips to bread crumb texture.
  4. Sprinkle the mixture on top of the rhubarb layer and bake in the preheated oven for 10–12 minutes or until lightly golden brown.
  5. Allow it to cool down and remove from the frame by running a paring knife along the inner edge of the frame and cut into small neat squares using a chef’s or bread knife.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Shortbread Base: 160°C (320°F) for 10–15 mins; Rhubarb Mixture cooked to 106°C (223°F); Pistachio Crumble baked at 180°C (356°F) for 10–12 mins
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Bread-crumb texture for pistachio crumble; baked until lightly golden brown; neat squares when cooled
  • Storage / Shelf Life: N/A
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Shortbread bread could be replaced with short crust pastry.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Zester, Microplane, Mixing bowls, Rubber spatula, Rolling pin, 20 cm x 20 cm square frame, Baking paper, Aluminum foil, Sauce pan, Candy thermometer, Chef knife, Paring knife, White chopping board.
    • Key Learning: Zesting, Sifting, Creaming method, Baking, Rub in method, Using a candy thermometer, Cutting.
↑ Back to top

136. Lemon Meringue Cubes

Category: Pastry / Plated Dessert | Prep: 45 mins (30 min + 15 min dough rests) + freeze custard | Cook/Bake: 15–20 mins (blind bake at 160°C) + few seconds at 300°C (or torch) | Yield: 20cm x 20cm x 1.5cm / 12-16 pcs / 1050 g

Ingredients

A) Short Crust Pastry Base

  • 150 g All-purpose flour
  • 100 g Butter
  • 50 g Sugar
  • 20 g Egg yolk

B) Lemon Custard

  • 110 g Caster Sugar
  • 180 g Egg
  • 110 g Cream
  • 90 g Lemon juice
  • 1 pcs Lemon Zest
  • [ILLEGIBLE: 110 g] Butter (handwritten addition next to Lemon Zest; also note gelatine leaves referenced in Method step 1)

C) Italian Meringue

  • 60 g Egg white
  • 30 g Water
  • 125 g Sugar

Method

For the Short Crust Pastry Base

  1. Preheat the oven to 160°C.
  2. Sift flour into a large mixing bowl, add sugar and salt and mix.
  3. Add butter and rub in.
  4. Add egg as required to form a soft (but not sticky) dough.
  5. Place the dough in the refrigerator for half an hour to harden.
  6. Sheet the dough to the 20 cm x 20 cm frame size, dock it, and rest for another 15 minutes in the refrigerator.
  7. Blind bake in the frame for 15–20 minutes or until golden brown.
  8. Allow it to cool inside the frame.

For the Lemon Custard

  1. Soak gelatine leaves in cold water.
  2. Place egg, egg yolk, sugar, lemon juice in a mixing bowl and cook to 82°C over a bain marie.
  3. Take the mixture off the bain marie. Squeeze out the excess water and add in the soaked gelatine leaves; stir until dissolved.
  4. Using an electric hand mixer whisk the mixture. When the mixture reaches 45°C start adding the cubes of cold butter gradually.
  5. Pour in the frame and freeze to set.

For the Italian Meringue

  1. Make the Italian meringue and fill it in a piping bag set with large plain or star nozzle.
  2. Pipe the meringue on top of the lemon custard and cover it all the way.
  3. Place to a 300°C oven for a few second, until the top is golden brown.
  4. Allow it to cool down completely.

For the Finishing

  1. Once the lemon custard has set and the meringue has been piped and torched/baked to a golden brown, cut cubes with a chef’s knife using hot water to warm and clean the blade before each cut.
  2. Keep in the chiller until required to serve.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Short Crust Base: 160°C (320°F) for 15–20 mins; Lemon Custard cooked to 82°C (180°F) over bain marie, butter emulsified at 45°C (113°F); Meringue browned at 300°C (572°F) for a few seconds or torched
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Golden brown shortcrust base; smooth set lemon custard; golden brown torched/baked Italian meringue peaks
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Keep in the chiller until required to serve
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Zester/Microplane, Lemon squeezer, Mixing bowl, Rolling pin, Cling film, 20 cm x 20 cm square frame, Baking paper, Aluminum foil, Baking tray, Sauce pan, Hand Whisk, Rubber spatula, Electric Hand mixer, Piping bag, Piping nozzle, 7 mm star or plain, Chef knife.
    • Key Learning: Sifting, Rub in method, Rolling, Docking, Baking, Italian meringue, Piping, Using blow torch, Cutting, Bain marie.

137. Vanilla Marshmallows

Category: Confectionery | Prep: Rest overnight | Cook/Bake: Cook syrup to 117°C | Yield: 25 – 30 pcs

Ingredients

  • 120 g Water
  • 10 g Glucose syrup
  • 250 g Sugar
  • 40 g Egg white
  • 8 pcs Gelatin leaves (BZ)
  • 1 pcs Vanilla Pods

Method

  1. Line frame with baking paper on the bottom and sprinkle with generous amount of corn starch.
  2. Soak gelatin leaves in ice cold water.
  3. Cook water, glucose syrup, and sugar to 117°C.
  4. Remove from the heat and allow the temperature to fall down to 108°C.
  5. Start to whip egg white on maximum speed.
  6. Add syrup to egg white slowly while whisking.
  7. Add drained gelatin and mix on high speed for 1 minute and add vanilla.
  8. Foam until the temperature goes down to 45°C.
  9. Spread marshmallow mixture into the prepared frame while still warm and spread it briefly and make it flat.
  10. Sprinkle more corn starch on top and cover it with another sheet of baking paper.
  11. Allow to rest it overnight.
  12. Cut to 25 x 25 mm squares and roll them in corn starch. Keep them dry and cool place until serve.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Cook sugar syrup to 117°C (243°F), cool to 108°C (226°F) before pouring into egg whites, and whip until cooled to 45°C (113°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Light, foamed marshmallow set overnight and cut into 25 x 25 mm squares coated in corn starch
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Keep in a dry and cool place until serving
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. A variety of other dry ingredients can be used to coat marshmallows, like desiccated coconut, or niege décor.
      3. Making other flavor of marshmallow, water can be substituted partly or fully with fruit pure and coloring can be used. Lemon juice can be added additionally if fruit flavor is required.
      4. For lemon marshmallow, 1/3–1/2 of the water can be substituted with lemon juice and lemon zest can be folded in when ready.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowl, Cling film, Stand Mixer, Whisk Attachment, Baking paper, Aluminum foil, Baking tray, Sauce pan, Rubber spatula, Scissors, Piping bag, Piping nozzle, 7 mm star or plain, Chef knife, White chopping board, Digital thermometer, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Sifting, Piping, Cutting, Making a Marshmallow.

138. Pâte de Fruit; Passion fruit

Category: Confectionery | Prep: Rest overnight at room temperature | Cook/Bake: Cook syrup to 135°C; cook puree mixture to 108°C | Yield: 50 pcs

Ingredients

  • 250 g Passion fruit puree
  • 282 g Sugar I.
  • 62 g Glucose syrup
  • 50 ml Water
  • 10 g Pectin Jaune / Yellow
  • 27 g Sugar II
  • 10 g Citric Acid / Lemon Juice
  • A/R Caster Sugar

Method

  1. Mix sugar II. with the pectin jaune, dry and set aside.
  2. Cook sugar I., glucose and water to 135°C.
  3. In a separate large sauce pan, boil passion fruit puree.
  4. Add pectin sugar mixture to the boiling puree. Boil it again.
  5. Add sugar syrup to the fruit puree, when reached 135°C and cook to 108°C while continuously stirring.
  6. Once reaches 108°C, take off the heat and add citric acid or lemon juice to stop the jelly from cooking.
  7. Pour mixture to automatic fennel [funnel] / sauce dispenser and distribute it equally to fruit pate moulds (or other small volume silicon moulds).
  8. Rest fruit pate on room temperature overnight.
  9. To serve, remold fruit de pate and roll them to caster sugar.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Sugar I, glucose, and water cooked to 135°C (275°F); combined fruit puree mixture cooked to 108°C (226°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Firm, set fruit jelly demolded and coated in caster sugar
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Rest at room temperature overnight before demolding and rolling in caster sugar
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Replacing the fruit puree to other fruit, other flavors and color of pate de fruit can be made:
        • Apricot - Apricot puree,
        • Peach - Peach puree,
        • Strawberry - Strawberry puree,
        • Raspberry - Raspberry puree,
        • Green apple - Apple puree and green food coloring, etc.
        • For berry flavor, use 70% berry puree and 30% water.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Lemon squeezer, Mixing bowl, Cling film, Sauce Dispenser, Semi sphere silicon molds, Sauce pan, Rubber spatula, Sugar thermometer.
    • Key Learning: Using a sugar thermometer.

139. Peanut Butter Fudge

Category: Confectionery | Prep: 35 mins (cooling & 30 min crystallization) | Cook/Bake: Cook to 117°C | Yield: 15cm x 15cm frame

Ingredients

  • 197 g Sugar
  • 15 g Glucose syrup
  • 62 g Invert sugar
  • 25 g Heavy cream
  • 70 g Evaporated Milk
  • 1/8 tsp Salt
  • 90 g Peanut Butter
  • 35 g Fondant

Method

  1. Combine the sugar, glucose syrup, invert sugar, heavy cream, and evaporated milk in a saucepan. Cook to 117°C, stirring constantly.
  2. Mix in the salt.
  3. Pour the hot batch onto a marble slab. Place the remaining ingredients on top of the syrup.
  4. Allow the mixture to cool to 50°C. Using a scraper, agitate until the mixture turns creamy and opaque, about 5 minutes.
  5. Deposit onto a baking paper.
  6. Place another piece of baking paper on top of the fudge.
  7. Using a rolling pin, flatten the top to 15 x 15 cm square.
  8. Allow the fudge to crystallize before removing the baking paper, about 30 min at room temperature.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Cook sugar/milk mixture to 117°C (243°F); cool on marble slab to 50°C (122°F) before agitating
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Creamy and opaque after ~5 minutes of agitation on marble slab; crystallized firm square after 30 minutes at room temperature
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Crystallize at room temperature for 30 minutes
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Fudge is a type of confectionary made by mixing sugar, butter and milk together and heating it to 117°C and then beating the mixture down till it cools and reaches a smooth and creamy consistency.
      3. Fruits nuts and other candies and sweets are sometimes added inside or on top as a garnish and to provide textural contrast.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Lemon squeezer, Mixing bowl, Rolling pin, Baking paper, Baking tray, Sauce pan, Bench scrapers, Rubber spatula, Chef knife, White chopping board, Digital thermometer.
    • Key Learning: Crystallization of fudge, Cutting.

140. Nougat de Montélimar

Category: Confectionery | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: Nuts heated at 120°C; Honey to 116°C–120°C; Sugar syrup to 140°C | Yield: 12 – 15 pcs

Ingredients

  • 30 g Caster Sugar I.
  • 50 g Egg white
  • 380 g Sugar II.
  • 120 g Glucose syrup
  • 100 ml Water
  • 230 g Honey
  • 50 g Cocoa butter
  • 130 g Whole shelled pistachio
  • 250 g Whole unblanched almonds
  • 150 g Roasted hazelnut
  • 100 g Chopped dried apricot

Method

  1. Heat and hold nuts at 120°C.
  2. Heat honey in a sauce pan to 116°C.
  3. Simultaneously cook sugar II, glucose and water in a saucepan towards 140°C.
  4. When honey reaches 116°C, start whipping egg whites with sugar I. (French meringue).
  5. When honey reaches 120°C pour it to the meringue while continuously whipping.
  6. Put the cocoa butter for melting, and set aside.
  7. When sugar reaches 140°C, add it to the meringue while continuously whipping for further 5 minutes.
  8. Add melted cocoa butter in the meringue mixture and whip for another 2–3 minutes.
  9. Stop whipping and fold nuts and apricot in.
  10. Remove the nougat dough onto silicon mat, shape using a 9 inch square frame.
  11. Roll it flat and smooth using a baking paper and allow it to cool down.
  12. Sprinkle bottom and top with corn starch and cut into small square or bars.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Hold nuts at 120°C (248°F); heat honey to 116°C (241°F) then 120°C (248°F); cook sugar II syrup to 140°C (284°F)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Chewy white nougat studded with warm nuts and dried apricot, rolled flat and smooth
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Dust top and bottom with corn starch once cooled and cut into squares or bars
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Nougat is a type of confectionary made of sugar, nuts and egg whites. The characteristic “chewy” texture is what sets this sweet treat apart from its contemporaries.
      3. There are two types of Nougat, white nougat made with egg whites (which gives it that chewy texture) and brown nougat, made without egg whites (which has more of a crunchy texture).
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowl, Rolling pin, Cling film, Baking paper, Baking tray, Sauce pan, Hand Whisk, Rubber spatula, Electric Hand mixer, Chef knife, White chopping board, Digital thermometer.
    • Key Learning: Making Nougat, Rolling, Baking, Cutting.
↑ Back to top

141. Dark chocolate - coated sugar honeycomb

Category: Confectionery | Prep: Cool undisturbed until cold | Cook/Bake: Cook caramel to 157°C | Yield: 12 – 15 pcs

Ingredients

  • 225 g Caster Sugar
  • 55 g Honey
  • 85 g Glucose syrup
  • 40 g Water
  • 10 g Bicarbonate of Soda
  • 480 g 55% Dark Chocolate

Method

  1. Line a high frame or baking sheet with baking paper.
  2. Place water, glucose syrup, honey and sugar in a sauce pan and heat it on medium heat.
  3. Bring to boil, while stirring occasionally.
  4. When boiling and the “first foam” has disappeared, brush around the side of the pan using a clean pastry brush.
  5. Cook it until reach 157°C or a golden brown color.
  6. Add the sieved bicarbonate of soda to the caramel and mix with a whisk for a few second to dissolve it.
  7. Pour it to the baking paper and allow it to cool down undisturbed.
  8. When cold, brake [break] it to small pieces.
  9. Temper dark chocolate and mix in the broken honey comb.
  10. Spread it to plastic acetate sheet or baking paper and allow it to set. Break it to serve.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 157°C (315°F) caramel temperature
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Golden brown caramel that foams into an airy, crisp sponge candy upon adding bicarbonate of soda; coated in tempered dark chocolate
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Allow chocolate coating to set completely before breaking to serve
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Honeycomb and sponge candy is made from boiling a sugar and “golden syrup” and adding baking soda to create the iconic texture.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowl, Rolling pin, Cling film, Baking paper, Baking tray, Sauce pan, Hand Whisk, Rubber spatula, Electric Hand mixer, Digital thermometer.
    • Key Learning: Making Honeycomb.

142. Milk Chocolate Coffee Truffle

Category: Confectionery | Prep: 5 mins (steeping) + ~20 mins (cooling to 25°C) + crystallization | Cook/Bake: Bring cream to a boil | Yield: 12 – 15 pcs

Ingredients

  • 65 g Cream
  • 10 g Coffee beans
  • A/R Milk
  • 15 g Glucose syrup
  • 105 g Milk Chocolate
  • 12 - 15 pcs Dark chocolate truffle shells
  • A/R Milk chocolate (tempered)

Method

  1. Bring to boil the heavy cream and coffee. Remove from heat, cover, and steep for 5 minutes.
  2. Strain the pre moistened cheese cloth. Wring the coffee in the cheese cloth to ensure maximum extraction. Return the cream to its original volume by adding milk.
  3. Add the glucose syrup to the flavored cream mixture. Bring to boil.
  4. Pour the hot cream mixture over the chopped chocolates and allow sitting for 60 sec, allowing the chocolate to melt.
  5. Using a spoon, or spatula, stir in the mixture in vigorous small circles in the center of the bowl until it emulsifies.
  6. Stir outward in large circles to spread the emulsion throughout the bowl, checking to see that all chocolate has melted. If necessary place the ganache over a warm water bath to melt the chocolate.
  7. Stream in the liqueur, stirring the mixture until homogenous.
  8. Pour the ganache into a bowl. Place plastic wrap directly on the surface of the ganache. Allow the ganache to rest at room temperature until it reaches 25°C or slightly lower, approximately 20 min. The cooled ganache should be of a thick but fluid consistency to properly fill the shells.
  9. Agitate the ganache lightly by placing it on a marble slab and working it gently to induce crystallization.
  10. Using a disposable pastry bag with small opening cut in the tip; fill the shells with ganache to within 3mm of the top.
  11. Allow the ganache filling to crystallize at a room temperature until the top solid.
  12. Seal the molds with the milk chocolate and remold when the chocolate is crystallized.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Boil cream; cool ganache to 25°C (77°F) or slightly lower before agitating and piping
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Smooth, homogenous, emulsified ganache cooled to a thick but fluid consistency; crystallized top before sealing with tempered milk chocolate
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Crystallize at room temperature
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Palette Knife, Scraper, Saucepan, Rubber spatula, Digital Thermometer.
    • Key Learning: Making Ganache, Making Truffles.
↑ Back to top

143. Dark Chocolate Ginger Bonbon or Truffle

Category: Confectionery | Prep: 5 mins (steeping) + ~20 mins (cooling to 25°C) + ~20 mins (chilling/crystallizing) | Cook/Bake: Bring cream to a boil | Yield: 12 – 15 pcs / 20cm x 20cm frame

Ingredients

For Filling

  • 105 g Cream
  • 1 tbsp Fresh Ginger
  • A/R Milk
  • 30 g Glucose Syrup
  • 100 g 55% Dark Chocolate I
  • 15 g Butter
  • A/R 55% Dark Chocolate

For Frame

  • 210 g Cream
  • 2 tbsp Fresh Ginger
  • A/R Milk
  • 60 g Glucose Syrup
  • 320 g 55% Dark Chocolate I
  • 30 g Butter
  • A/R 55% Dark Chocolate

Method

  1. Combine the cream with the chopped ginger and bring to a boil. Remove from heat, cover and let steep for 5 minutes.
  2. Strain the ginger out of the cream using a pre moistened cheese cloth.
  3. Return the cream to its original weight by adding milk.
  4. Add the glucose syrup to the flavored cream mixture. Bring to boil.
  5. Pour the hot cream mixture over the chopped chocolates and allow sitting for 60 sec, allowing the chocolate to melt.
  6. Using a spoon or spatula stir in the mixture in vigorous small circles in the center of the bowl until it emulsifies.
  7. Stir outward in large circles to spread the emulsion throughout the bowl, checking to see that all chocolate has melted. If necessary place the ganache over a warm water bath to melt the chocolate.
  8. Massage the butter into the ganache taking care that no lumps of butter remain unincorporated.
  9. Pour the ganache into a bowl. Place plastic wrap directly on the surface of the ganache. Allow the ganache to rest at room temperature until it reaches 25°C or slightly lower, approximately 20 min. The cooled ganache should be of a thick but fluid consistency to properly fill the shells.
  10. Agitate the ganache lightly by placing it on a marble slab and working it gently to induce crystallization.
  11. Using a disposable pastry bag with small opening cut in the tip; fill the lined molds to within 3mm of the top.
  12. Allow the ganache filling to crystallize at a room temperature until the top solid.
  13. Seal the molds with the dark chocolate.
  14. Allow the caps to crystallize at room temperature, and then refrigerate until the chocolate pulls away from the inside of the molds. Approximately 20 min.
  15. Place a piece of stiff cardboard over each mold and invert it to release the finished chocolate.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Boil cream; cool ganache to 25°C (77°F) or slightly lower; keep chocolate molds at 25°C (77°F) before using
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Smooth, lump-free emulsified ginger ganache; glossy molded bonbons that pull away cleanly from the molds after ~20 minutes of refrigeration
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Crystallize at room temperature, then refrigerate ~20 minutes to release from molds
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. The chocolate molds should be wiped with cotton and kept at 25°C in oven before using.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Palette Knife, Scraper, Saucepan, Rubber spatula.
    • Key Learning: Making Ganache, Making Truffles.
↑ Back to top

144. Passion-Vanilla Milk Chocolate Truffle Frame

Category: Confectionery | Prep: 1 hour (vanilla layer) + overnight (passion fruit layer) + overnight/5 min chill (dipping) | Cook/Bake: Bring cream and glucose to a boil | Yield: 20cm x 20cm frame, 12 – 15 pcs

Ingredients

A) Vanilla Milk Chocolate Ganache

  • 90 g Cream
  • 60 g Glucose Syrup
  • ½ pc Vanilla Bean
  • 10 g Butter, soft
  • 230 g Milk Chocolate

B) Passion fruit ganache

  • 50 g Cream
  • 35 g Glucose Syrup
  • 50 g Passion fruit puree
  • 15 g Butter, soft
  • 260 g White Chocolate

Method

For the Vanilla Milk Chocolate Ganache

  1. Combine the cream and glucose syrup in a saucepan. Scrape the vanilla bean into the cream mixture, and then drop in the scraped pod. Bring to a boil.
  2. Remove from heat and pluck out bean pod. Allow cream mixture to cool to 40°C.
  3. Massage the butter into the melted milk chocolate, taking care that no lumps remain.
  4. Pour the cream mixture over the chocolate using paddle, stir in small outward circles, to emulsify.
  5. Spread the mixture into a 12x12cm frame on a heavy plastic sheet. Allow to sit at room temperature, uncovered, until firm, about 1 hour.

For the Passion fruit ganache

  1. Combine the cream and glucose syrup in a saucepan. Bring to boil.
  2. Remove from the heat and add the reduced puree.
  3. Allow the mixture to cool to 40°C.
  4. Massage the butter into the melted white chocolate, taking care that no lumps remain.
  5. Pour the 40°C cream mixture over the chocolate using paddle, stir in small outward circles, to emulsify.
  6. Pour the mixture on top of vanilla ganache. Cover with the plastic wrap and allow it to crystallize overnight.
  7. Remove from the frame and peel the plastic off the slab.
  8. Pre coat the vanilla ganache side with the dark chocolate.
  9. Cut into rectangles on a guitar.
  10. Dip each center in the dark chocolate, and place on a structured transfer sheet on each piece.
  11. For best shine allow the piece to crystallize overnight before removing the transfer sheets. If that is not possible, refrigerate the finished and set pieces for 5 minutes, and then remove the transfer sheet.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Boil cream and glucose mixtures; cool both cream mixtures to 40°C (104°F) before emulsifying with chocolate
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Two-layered firm slab cut cleanly on a guitar cutter, enrobed in dark chocolate with high gloss from transfer sheet
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Crystallize slab overnight; after dipping, crystallize overnight (or refrigerate 5 minutes) before removing transfer sheets
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Palette Knife, Scraper, Saucepan, Rubber spatula, Guitar, Square Frame 12x12cm, Plastic sheet.
    • Key Learning: Making Chocolate Frames, Making Ganache, Layering, Using a Guitar.
↑ Back to top

145. Marbled Praline Feuilletine Frame

Category: Confectionery | Prep: Set in frame a few minutes before cutting | Cook/Bake: Melt over bain-marie | Yield: 20cm x 20cm frame, 12 – 15 pcs

Ingredients

A) Feuilletine Mixture

  • 121 g Milk Chocolate
  • 30 g Cocoa Butter
  • 150 g Praline Paste
  • 97 g Feuilletine

B) Chocolate Coating

  • 50 g White chocolate
  • 50 g Cocoa butter
  • 25 g Dark chocolate 55%
  • 25 g Cocoa butter

Method

  1. Melt the Milk chocolate over baine-marie and combine with praline paste and melted cocoa butter.
  2. Mix in the feuilletine, spread the batch into 200 x 200 mm frame placed on parchment paper. Allow it to set.
  3. Pre coat one side of the slab with mixture of white chocolate and melted cocoa butter and allow it to set.
  4. Spread 1/8” layer of the same mix on top of the slab. Using a paper cone, immediately filigree thin parallel lines of dark chocolate and cocoa butter mixture.
  5. Working quickly before the chocolate sets, run a paring knife back and forth, perpendicular to the filigree, to create a feathered pattern.
  6. Let it set for few minutes and cut into squares on guitar.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Melt chocolates and cocoa butter over bain-marie
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Crisp praline-feuilletine slab with a feathered white and dark chocolate marbled top, cut into neat squares on a guitar
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Let set for a few minutes before cutting on guitar
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Palette Knife, Scraper, Saucepan, Rubber spatula, Guitar, Square Frame 12x12cm, Plastic sheet.
    • Key Learning: Making Chocolate Frames, Creating a feathered pattern, Layering, Using a Guitar.

146. Sea Salt Toffee Filling

Ingredients

  • 75 ml Cream
  • 25 g Butter
  • 100 g Sugar
  • ¼ tsp Sea Salt Flakes

Method

  1. Place sugar in a saucepan.

  4. Add cream slowly and continue stirring to get a smooth mixture.
  5. Cool and use as filling.

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Gentle heat until sugar is melted and golden in colour
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Golden caramel emulsified into a smooth, fluid toffee filling
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Cool completely before using as a filling for molded chocolates/bonbons

147. Saffron, Cardamom Filling

Category: Confectionery | Prep: 15 mins (steeping) + ~20 mins (cooling to 25°C) + ~20 mins (chilling) | Cook/Bake: Heat cream infusion to 75°C; melt chocolate at 40°C | Yield: N/A (not stated on page; standard 15–25 bonbon mold batch)

Ingredients

  • 150 gm Cream
  • A/R Saffron
  • 4-5 pcs Cardamom
  • A/R Milk
  • 30 gm Glucose syrup
  • 230 gm White chocolate
  • 10 gm Butter, soft
  • A/R White chocolate tempered for pre-coating and dipping

Method

  1. Combine the cream with the glucose, saffron and crushed cardamom and bring to 75° Celsius remove the heat, cover and let steep for 15 minute.
  2. Melt chocolate at 40°C. Add in the butter and pour the infusion over it.
  3. Using a spoon, or spatula, stir in the mixture in small circles in the center of the bowl until it emulsifies.
  4. Pour the ganache into the bowl. Place plastic wrap directly on the surface of the ganache. Allow the ganache to rest at room temperature until it reaches 25° Celsius or slightly lower, approximately 20 minute.
  5. Using a disposable pastry bag with small opening cut in the tip; fill the lined moulds to within 2 mm of the top.
  6. Allow the ganache filling to crystallize at a room temperature until the top solid.
  7. Seal the moulds with the dark [or tempered white] chocolate.
  8. Allow the caps to crystallize at room temperature, and the refrigerator until the chocolate pulls away from the inside f [of] the moulds. Approximately 20 min.
  9. Place a pieces of stiff card board over each mould and invert the mould to release the finished chocolate.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Heat cream infusion to 75°C (167°F); melt white chocolate at 40°C (104°F); cool ganache to 25°C (77°F) or slightly lower
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Smooth, emulsified saffron-cardamom white chocolate ganache; crystallized top before sealing; cleanly demolded bonbons after ~20 minutes in the refrigerator
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Crystallize at room temperature, then refrigerate ~20 minutes to release from moulds
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Saucepan, Rubber spatula.
    • Key Learning: Making Salted toffee filling (printed verbatim on page 156).
