148. Bagels

Category: Breads | Prep: 3–4 hours (Polish at 35°C) + overnight (chiller maturation) + 30–60 mins (final proof) | Cook/Bake: Poach 2–3 mins; Bake at 210°C for 10–12 mins | Yield: 12 x 50 g

Ingredients

Bagel Dough

  • 200 g Bread flour
  • 3 g Instant Yeast
  • 12 g Sugar
  • 8 g Salt
  • 0 – 20 g Water

Polish

  • 200 g Bread flour
  • 3 g Instant Yeast
  • 220 g Water

Poaching

  • A/R Bicarbonate of Soda
  • A/R Salt

Topping

  • A/R Sesame seeds

Method

For the Polish

  1. Mix all the ingredients and knead until just combined.
  2. Put for proving for 3–4 hours (35°C).

For the Bagel Dough

  1. Add yeast, sugar, flour and water to the polish and knead it until it comes together and becomes smooth and elastic.
  2. Put for proving/maturation overnight to the chiller.
  3. Cut into 12 pieces (50 gm) and shape them to balls.
  4. Cover them and proof for 30–60 minutes or until doubled in volume.

For the Poaching and finishing

  1. Boil water with salt and bicarbonate of soda.
  2. Place the bagels into the water, while making a whole [hole] in the middle.
  3. Simmer for 2–3 minutes.
  4. Take out, transfer to a wire rack and place them onto trays.
  5. Sprinkle them with sesame seeds immediately and bake them right away.
  6. Bake them at 210°C for 10–12 minutes or until golden brown. Allow them to cool down on wire grids.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Prover set at 35°C / 95°F (or 27–35°C per Notes); Poach at a simmer for 2–3 minutes; Bake at 210°C (410°F) for 10–12 minutes (increase by 20°C for home ovens)
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Smooth and elastic dough; doubled in volume after proofing; poached and baked to a golden brown crust
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Cool properly on wire grids before slicing because internal heat continues the baking process
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. For commercial production of bread, the temperatures and proving times mentioned in the recipe must be followed.
      3. The Prover should be set between 27–35 °C.
      4. For home baking, the temperatures mentioned have to be increased by 20°C, since home ovens are slightly weaker than industrial grade.
      5. To prove the dough without a prover, the dough has to rest until it doubles in volume. The timing may vary depending on how cold or warm the bread making environment is.
      6. It is important to cool the bread properly after baking before slicing into it because the internal heat contained within the loaf/roll accounts for part of the baking process.
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Oven with steam, Proofer, Dough kneader, Mixing bowls, Baking trays, Measuring spoon set, Sieve, Baking paper, Scissors, Bread knife, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Kneading, Proofing, Shaping, Poaching, Baking.
↑ Back to top

149. Croissant and Danish Dough

Category: Viennoiserie | Prep: 45 mins rest per fold + 1–2 hrs (or overnight) 1st proofing + 1–2 hrs 2nd proofing | Cook/Bake: 8–15 mins (varies by shape) | Yield: 650 g; 8-12 pcs

Ingredients

Dough

  • 82.5 g Caster Sugar
  • 210 g Water
  • 210 g Milk
  • 16.5 g Instant Yeast
  • 750 g Bread Flour
  • 18 g Salt
  • 60 g Unsalted Butter
  • 420 g Butter Croissant

Egg Wash for Croissant

  • 80 g Egg yolk
  • 100 g Egg whole
  • 100 g Milk

Method

Mixing

  1. Sift the bread flour into a mixing bowl. Add the dry yeast and sugar on one side of the bowl and salt on the other side.

Kneading

  1. Mix the dry ingredients well and incorporate the water in small additions till it forms into a medium to soft dough.

1st Proofing

  1. Rest for at least an hour in the chiller, better to rest the dough overnight in the chiller/freezer for retarded fermentation.

Butter Slab

  1. Meanwhile, shape the butter to a square slab.

Lamination

  1. Roll the dough to form a rough square, ensuring that the sides are thinner than the centre and is big enough to envelope the butter slab.
  2. Place the block in the center and fold the corners of the dough over the butter like an envelope, tucking the edges to completely enclose the butter.
  3. Roll the enveloped dough to about 10mm thick and approximately thrice as long as it is wide. Do a single fold, and rest for 45 minutes.
  4. Chill the dough completely wrapped in cling film (to ensure that the dough doesn’t dry out, else cracks would develop on the surface).
  5. Repeat the step 7 & 8 twice, turning the dough by 90 degree each time (Refer to the diagram, for a clearer understanding of the folding and lamination technique).

For Croissant

  1. Sheet the dough to ½ cm thickness and cut into long rectangles 6 inches in width. Cut into equal triangles with 6 inch base.
  2. To shape the croissant, roll the base towards the tip with one hand while gently pulling the tip with other.
  3. Brush with egg wash gently with the use of a safe pastry brush making sure not to puncture the dough.
  4. Proof the croissant covered lightly with oiled cling film till it doubles on volume, till the butter layers can be seen and the shaped croissants have a jiggle. Prove for 1 – 2 hours at 25° C.
  5. Brush with egg wash.
  6. Bake at 160° C for 10 – 12 minutes.

For Pain au Chocolat

  1. Use the excess croissant dough (leftover from the lamination process and add cocoa powder to it as required to make a stiff dough and chill it.
  2. Roll this dough out thin to 3mm in thickness.
  3. Brush the sheeted laminated dough with water to act as a glue and place the thin rolled out cocoa powder infused dough on top and cut it to the size of the sheeted laminated dough.
  4. Roll the dough to ½ cm thickness and cut out 14cm X 8cm rectangles.
  5. To shape the pain au chocolat, score the coloured side of the dough with a baker’s blade and place the dough rectangle, coloured side down. Place a chocolate stick vertical alongside the longer end of the rectangle and roll that side over. Place another chocolate stick and roll the dough once again and seal with egg wash.
  6. Bake at 170 °C for 10 – 15 minutes.

For Braided Rolls

  1. Repeat same steps as Pain au chocolat till step 3.
  2. Roll out the dough to ½ cm thickness and cut into 23cm X 7cm rectangles.
  3. Cut the dough into 3 strips – not all the way through, leaving 1 – 2 cm of the dough intact.
  4. Braid it, keeping the coloured side on the bottom.
  5. Roll the braid and place it in a mould, with the end of the braid at the bottom of the mould.
  6. Bake at 170 °C for 8 – 12 minutes and cool it down.
  7. Fill/ inject with dulce de leche or chocolate ganache.

For Double Twist

  1. Roll the dough into ½ cm thickness.
  2. Cut the sheeted dough into 28 cm X 5 cm rectangle.
  3. Spread the hazelnut filling and fold it. Now the dough should be 14 cm X 5 cm.
  4. Cut in the centre lengthwise leaving 1 – 2 cm intact.
  5. Twist the 2 strips from the inside, twice.
  6. Egg wash it and allow to prove.
  7. Egg wash once more and bake at 170 °C for 8 – 12 minutes.

For Cinnamon Snail

  1. Sheet the dough to ½ cm thickness.
  2. Spread the pastry cream and sprinkle cinnamon powder and raisins.
  3. Tightly roll the dough starting from the longer side.
  4. Cut into 1 inch even sections with a serrated knife.
  5. Place them on a silicon mold or baking tray.
  6. Egg wash them.
  7. Prove them for 1.5 – 2 hours till the butter starts separating.
  8. Egg wash once again.
  9. Bake at 180 °C for 8 – 12 minutes.

For Windmill Danish

  1. Sheet the danish dough to ½ cm thickness and cut into 10 cm square.
  2. Make 4 diagonal cuts 5 long from corner towards centre.
  3. Lightly egg wash the centre. Lift one point from each corner and fold it carefully into centre.
  4. Prove for an hour and half, then Pipe pastry cream in the centre, brush with egg wash and bake at 180° C for 8 - 12 minutes.
  5. Once cooled a little, decorate with fresh fruit.

For Pear Danish

  1. Sheet the dough ½ cm thickness.
  2. Cut into 9 cm X 9 cm square.
  3. Pipe the pastry cream horizontally in the center of the square.
  4. Half the pear and thinly slice it, about ½ cm in thickness.
  5. Arrange the slices of pear neatly on top of the pastry cream.
  6. Brush the Danish with egg wash and prove the prepared danishes.
  7. Egg wash again and sprinkle the crumble on top of the pear.
  8. Bake at 180°C for 8 – 10 minutes.

For Apricot Danish

  1. Sheet the danish dough to ½ cm thickness and cut into 10 cm square.
  2. Pipe pastry cream in a line, diagonally across the square, leaving the corners.
  3. Place half a poached apricot at each end of pastry cream.
  4. Fold in the other two corners to overlap the centre.
  5. Prove for an hour and half.
  6. Brush with egg wash, sprinkle pearl sugar and bake at 180 °C for 8 - 12 minutes.

For Bear Claw

  1. Sheet the danish dough to ½ cm thickness and cut into 10 cm squares.
  2. Pipe pastry cream or coconut on one half of the square, and fold the other half over it.
  3. Prove for an hour and a half, brush with egg wash and bake at 180 °C for 8 - 12 minutes.

Cooling

  1. Remove from the oven and rest on a wire rack.

Croissant 3-Day Schedule & Worksheet (Pages 253–254 / Book Pages 167–168)

  • Croissant Day 1:
    1. Making the dough
    2. Cling film the dough let it prove till it doubles
    3. Put in the freezer overnight
  • Croissant Day 2:
    1. Thaw the dough till it defrosts
    2. Prepared the laminated butter slab _cm x _cm
    3. Lamination with English method (seal butter in dough) Chill
    4. Roll out to ___ cm x ___ cm Straight to do Single turn—– chill for 45 minute
    5. Rotate 90 degree
    6. Roll out to ___ cm x ___ cm
    7. Lamination can be done in one of 2 ways:
      1. 1 single fold, 1 double fold OR
      2. 3 single folds
    8. Lamination to Double Fold
    9. After done the Double fold, store in the freezer
  • Croissant Day 3:
    1. Dividing Shaping Proofing Baking
    2. Roll out to ___ cm x ___ cm
    3. Divide and shape the croissant take ___ x ___ cm each
    4. First coat of egg wash
    5. Proof to perfection (indication 2 hours)
    6. Second coat of egg wash
    7. Bake for 12 to 15minute for 170° C celcius
    • For Croissant Cutting Ratio Diagram: y × x = 3 : 1

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Croissant: 160°C (320°F) for 10–12 mins (or 170°C / 338°F for 12–15 mins per Day 3 schedule); Pain au Chocolat: 170°C (338°F) for 10–15 mins; Braided Rolls & Double Twist: 170°C (338°F) for 8–12 mins; Cinnamon Snail, Windmill Danish, Apricot Danish, Bear Claw: 180°C (356°F) for 8–12 mins; Pear Danish: 180°C (356°F) for 8–10 mins. Proofing at 25°C (77°F).
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Medium to soft dough; proofed until doubled in volume, butter layers are visible, and shaped croissants have a jiggle; baked until golden and flaky.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Dough can be rested overnight in chiller/freezer for retarded fermentation; after double fold on Day 2, store in the freezer.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Oven with steam, Proofer, Dough kneader, Mixing bowls, Baking trays, Measuring spoon set, Sieve, Baking paper, Scissors, Bread knife, Pastry Brush, Dough Sheeter, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Kneading, Proofing, Shaping, Poaching, Baking, Lamination.
    • Lamination Diagrams (Page 252 / Book Page 166): Shows single fold (tri-fold with open seam noted) and double fold / book fold (steps 1 and 2 folding outer edges in towards the seam, then folding in half).

150. Croissant and Danish Fillings

Category: Viennoiserie Fillings | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: Few seconds after boiling (for cooked fillings) | Yield: N/A

Ingredients

Coconut Filling

  • 250 g Coconut Milk
  • 75 g Sugar
  • 25 g Corn Flour
  • 2 g Lemon Juice
  • 50 g Desiccated Coconut
  • 3 g Salt

Cheese Filling

  • 50 g Butter
  • 80 g Icing sugar
  • 25 g All purpose
  • 100 g Cream cheese
  • 40 g Egg yolk
  • 25 g Milk Powder
  • 5 g Lemon juice
  • 5 g Lemon zest
  • 2 g Salt
  • 5 g Vanilla

Hazelnut Filling

  • 100 g Sugar
  • 200 g Milk
  • 200 g Hazelnut Powder
  • 20 g Honey
  • 2 g Lemon juice
  • 2 g Cinnamon powder
  • 20 g Bread Crumbs

Chocolate Filling

  • 50 g All purpose
  • 250 g Water
  • 32 g Cocoa Powder
  • 100 g Sugar
  • 20 g Butter
  • 35 g Milk Powder
  • 4 g Salt
  • 2 tbsp Condensed milk

Pistachio Filling

  • 62.5 g Pistachio Powder
  • 62.5 g Almond Powder
  • 125 g Icing sugar
  • 10 g Rose water

Almond Filling

  • 135 g Butter
  • 165 g Icing sugar
  • 165 g Almond Powder
  • 10 g All purpose flour
  • 100 g Whole eggs

Almond Syrup

  • 250 g Water
  • 100 g Sugar
  • 20 g Almond powder
  • 5 g Vanilla Paste
  • 15 g Orange Blossom

Almond Crumble

  • 25 g All purpose flour
  • 25 g Light brown sugar
  • 25 g Butter
  • 25 g Almond Powder

Method

Coconut Filling

  1. Mix the coconut milk, salt, sugar, desiccated coconut together and bring to a boil.
  2. Mix corn flour and a little water, and mix until smooth.
  3. Pour the corn flour mixture to the boiling liquid at one go, while continuously mixing.
  4. Cook it for a few seconds, (until it starts boiling from the middle) and take off the heat.
  5. Transfer the coconut cream to a clean bowl and start whisking slowly for about a minute until cools down bit, then add the lemon juice.
  6. Keep refrigerated. Beat it until smooth before use.

Cheese Filling

  1. In the bowl of a stand mixer fitted with the paddle attachment, cream the butter with the sugar until light and add the eggs. Beat well and the add lemon juice, lemon zest and vanilla extract. Cream until the mixture is creamy.
  2. Add the dry ingredients and cream cheese.
  3. Put in a piping bag, ready to use.

Hazelnut Filling

  1. Mix the hazelnut powder, sugar, milk, honey together and bring to a boil.
  2. Add the cinnamon powder, lemon juice, bread crumbs and mix until smooth.
  3. Cook it for a few seconds, (until it starts to boil from the middle) and take it off the heat.
  4. Keep refrigerated. Beat it until smooth before use.

Chocolate Filling

  1. Mix the water, sugar, cocoa powder, all-purpose flour, milk powder and bring to a boil.
  2. Add the salt and condensed milk while continuously mixing in the butter.
  3. Cook it for a few seconds, (until it starts to boil in the middle) and take off the heat.
  4. Keep refrigerated. Beat it until smooth before use.

Pistachio Filling

  1. Mix all the ingredient to form a thick paste and make small logs (about 1 inch long).

Almond Filling

  1. In the bowl of a stand mixer fitted with the paddle attachment, cream the butter with the sugar until light and add the eggs and beat until it is of a creamy texture.
  2. Add the dry ingredients.
  3. Reserve in a piping bag.

Almond Syrup

  1. Put all the ingredients in a sauce pan and bring to boil.

Almond Crumble

  1. Rub the cold butter in with the flour and light brown sugar and mix to form a crumble like texture.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Bring cooked fillings (Coconut, Hazelnut, Chocolate) and Almond Syrup to a boil (100°C / 212°F) and cook for a few seconds until boiling from the middle.
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Smooth, creamy texture for Coconut, Cheese, Hazelnut, Chocolate, and Almond fillings; thick paste formed into 1-inch logs for Pistachio Filling; crumble-like texture for Almond Crumble.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Keep Coconut, Hazelnut, and Chocolate fillings refrigerated and beat until smooth before use; reserve Cheese and Almond fillings in piping bags.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment: Used as fillings and toppings for Croissant and Danish Dough variations (Pages 255–257 / Book Pages 169–171).
↑ Back to top

151. Kugelhopf / Kuglof

Category: Breads / Viennoiserie | Prep: 70–85 mins proofing | Cook/Bake: 25–30 mins | Yield: 50 – 60 pieces

Ingredients

Kugelhopf Dough

  • 125 g Bread flour
  • 125 g AP flour
  • 60 g Sugar
  • 2 g Salt
  • 100 g Milk
  • 7 g Instant Yeast
  • 50 g Eggs
  • 20 g Egg yolks
  • 100 g Butter
  • 2 g Lemon Zest
  • 60 g Raisin

Roll in

  • 35 g Butter
  • 35 g Walnut
  • 40 g Dark brown sugar
  • 1/8 tsp Cinnamon
  • 7 g Cocoa powder

Method

  1. Knead this rich fermented dough.
  2. Knead the raisins in at the end, after butter, just before it is ready.
  3. Put for first proofing to 30°C for 35-45 minutes, or until doubles in volume.
  4. Brush a kuglof tin with butter and keep in the chiller.
  5. Knock the dough back and roll about 10 mm thick rectangular shape.
  6. Spread melted butter on top.
  7. Sprinkle with chopped walnuts, brown sugar, cinnamon powder and cocoa powder.
  8. Roll up, like cinnamon roll and place it into the tin.
  9. Prove for 2nd time at 30°C for about 35-40 minutes, or until doubles in volume.
  10. Bake at 160°C for 25 -30 minutes.
  11. Turn it upside down on a wire rack and remove the tin and dust with icing sugar.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Proof at 30°C (86°F) (Prover set between 27–35°C / 80.6–95°F); Bake at 160°C (320°F) for 25–30 minutes (increase by 20°C / 36°F for home ovens).
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Dough doubles in volume during 1st and 2nd proofing; cooled properly on a wire rack before slicing.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Cool properly on a wire rack before slicing.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Oven with steam, Proofer, Dough kneader, Mixing bowls, Baking trays, Measuring spoon set, Sieve, Baking paper, Scissors, Bread knife, Oven mitts/gloves, Rolling Pin.
    • Key Learning: Proofing, Baking.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. For commercial production of bread, the temperatures and proving times mentioned in the recipe must be followed.
      3. The Prover should be set between 27-35 °C.
      4. For home baking, the temperatures mentioned have to be increased by 20°C, since home ovens are slightly weaker than industrial grade.
      5. To prove the dough without a prover, the dough has to rest until it doubles in volume. The timing may vary depending on how cold or warm the bread making environment is.
      6. It is important to cool the bread properly after baking before slicing into it because the internal heat contained within the loaf/roll accounts for part of the baking process.

152. Baguette, with Biga

Category: Breads | Prep: 15–18 hrs biga fermentation + 1–2 hrs temper + ~1.5–2.5 hrs proofing | Cook/Bake: 12–15 mins (in 3 phases) | Yield: 1000 g, 3 – 4 loaves

Ingredients

Biga

  • 200 g Bread Flour
  • 3 g Yeast
  • 130 g Water
  • 4 g Salt

Baguette

  • 500 g Bread flour
  • 330 g Biga
  • 2 g Yeast
  • 7 g Salt
  • 320 g Water

Method

Pre prep

  1. Mix all the ingredients for the preferment (biga) and keep in a plastic, non reactive container overnight to allow it to ferment in the refrigerator (about 15 – 18 hours).
  2. Take out the Biga 1 – 2 hours before use so that it comes to room temperature.

Mixing

  1. Sift the bread flour into a mixing bowl and add the bread flour on top. Add the dry yeast and salt.

Kneading

  1. Mix the dry ingredients well and incorporate the water and the biga in small additions till it forms into a medium dough.

1st Proofing

  1. Form a ball, cover and place it in the proofer at 35°C for 30-40 minutes or room temperature for 1 - 1.5 hours or until doubles in volume.
  2. Stretch and fold the dough in 45 minute intervals, 2 times.

Pre shaping

  1. Portion the dough into 150g portion and roll up and form a batard shape.

2nd Proofing

  1. Cover and allow the dough to rest at room temperature for 10 – 15 minutes.

Final Shaping

  1. Shape the batard into baguette shape.

Final Proofing

  1. Place formed dough logs into baguette tin and prove for 15 - 20 minutes in the prover or until almost double in volume at room temperature.

Scoring

  1. Dust the top of the baguette loaves with flour and make 3-4 long cuts on the surface with sharp knife or baker’s blade. Make sure they are not pressed.

Final Proofing

  1. Complete the proving at room temperature for further 10 - 15 minutes.

Baking

  1. Bake in 3 phases.
    1. 220°C for 3-4 minutes, 40 - 60 sec water sprinkling;
    2. 200°C for 4-5 minutes;
    3. 180°C for 5-6 minutes, venting valve opened.

Cooling

  1. Remove from the oven and rest on a wire rack.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: 1st Proofing at 35°C (95°F) (Prover set between 27–35°C / 80.6–95°F); Bake in 3 phases: 220°C (428°F) for 3–4 mins with 40–60 sec water sprinkling, 200°C (392°F) for 4–5 mins, and 180°C (356°F) for 5–6 mins with venting valve opened.
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Medium dough; doubles in volume during 1st proofing; almost double in volume before scoring; crisp crust after 3-phase steam and vented baking.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Biga ferments 15–18 hours in the refrigerator in a plastic, non-reactive container; cool baked loaves on a wire rack before slicing.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Oven with steam, Proofer, Dough kneader, Mixing bowls, Baking trays, Measuring spoon set, Sieve, Baking paper, Scissors, Bread knife, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Kneading, Proofing, Shaping, Baking, Preferments.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. For commercial production of bread, the temperatures and proving times mentioned in the recipe must be followed.
      3. The Prover should be set between 27-35 °C.
      4. For home baking, the temperatures mentioned have to be increased by 20°C, since home ovens are slightly weaker than industrial grade.
      5. To prove the dough without a prover, the dough has to rest until it doubles in volume. The timing may vary depending on how cold or warm the bread making environment is.
      6. It is important to cool the bread properly after baking before slicing into it because the internal heat contained within the loaf/roll accounts for part of the baking process.

153. Ciabatta, with Polish

Category: Breads | Prep: Up to 15 hrs polish fermentation + 1–2 hrs temper + ~4.5–6 hrs proofing/folding | Cook/Bake: 11–15 mins (in 3 phases) | Yield: 650 g; 8-12 pcs

Ingredients

Polish (starter)

  • 475 g Bread flour
  • 475 g Water
  • 3 g Instant yeast

Ciabatta Dough

  • 950 g Polish
  • 780 g Bread Flour
  • 3 g Yeast
  • 475 g Water
  • 40 g Olive oil
  • 25 g Salt

Method

Pre prep

  1. Mix the polish ingredients, in a small bowl and let it rest overnight, in the refrigerator, covered, or for up to 15 hours. It will expand and become bubbly.
  2. Take out the Polish 1 – 2 hours before use so that it comes to room temperature.

Mixing

  1. Knead polish, flour, yeast, sugar and water in order to get an elastic, soft dough.

Kneading

  1. Once the wall of the bowl is clean, add the salt and the olive oil.
  2. Knead on slow speed until smooth again and mix it on the fast speed until the dough separates from the bowl.
  3. Scrape it on a generously floured surface with a plastic scraper, fold the sides onto the top from 4 sides, dust with flour.

1st Proofing

  1. Cover and allow it to prove for about 30-40 minutes in the prover OR until the dough doubles in volume at room temperature.
  2. Stretch and fold the dough in 45 minute intervals, 4 times.

Final Proofing

  1. Bulk ferment the dough for 1 hour in the prover, or at room temperatures for around 2 – 3 hours.

Pre shaping and Portioning

  1. Cut to equal pieces, using enough flour to make sure that the dough does not stick and place on the tray.

Baking

  1. Bake in 3 phases:
    1. 200° C for 3 minutes, 2 minutes water sprinkling;
    2. 180° C for 4-6 minutes;
    3. 180° C for 4-6 minutes with venting valve open.

Cooling

  1. Remove from the oven and rest on a wire rack. (Note: Numbered as step 13 in source text following step 11 a–c).

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Prover set between 27–35°C (80.6–95°F); Bake in 3 phases: 200°C (392°F) for 3 mins with 2 mins water sprinkling, 180°C (356°F) for 4–6 mins, and 180°C (356°F) for 4–6 mins with venting valve open.
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Polish expands and becomes bubbly; dough is elastic, soft, and separates from the bowl when kneaded on fast speed; doubles in volume during proofing.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Polish rests overnight in the refrigerator, covered, for up to 15 hours; cool baked bread on a wire rack before slicing.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Oven with steam, Proofer, Dough kneader, Mixing bowls, Baking trays, Measuring spoon set, Sieve, Baking paper, Scissors, Bread knife, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Kneading, Proofing, Shaping, Preferments, Baking.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. For commercial production of bread, the temperatures and proving times mentioned in the recipe must be followed.
      3. The Prover should be set between 27-35 °C.
      4. For home baking, the temperatures mentioned have to be increased by 20°C, since home ovens are slightly weaker than industrial grade.
      5. To prove the dough without a prover, the dough has to rest until it doubles in volume. The timing may vary depending on how cold or warm the bread making environment is.
      6. It is important to cool the bread properly after baking before slicing into it because the internal heat contained within the loaf/roll accounts for part of the baking process.

154. Lean Dough

Ingredients

  • 1000 g Bread flour

  • 20 g Salt
  • 550 g Water

Method

      4. For home baking, the temperatures mentioned have to be increased by 20°C, since home ovens are slightly weaker than industrial grade.

155. Salt Dough

Category: Breads / Decorative Dough | Prep: 30 mins rest + 10 mins rest after egg wash | Cook/Bake: 30–40 mins | Yield: 1500 g

Ingredients

  • 1000 g Bread flour
  • 100 g Salt
  • 600 g Water

Method

  1. Knead a soft dough from flour and water.
  2. When the bowl and attachment are clean and the dough is elastic, add the salt and knead for a few seconds to combine.
  3. Shape it to into a bowl and proof it until doubles in volume.
  4. Cut, roll and shape as required.
  5. Proof for second time and bake.
  6. Cover tightly with plastic wrap and allow it to rest for about 30 minutes at room temperature.
  7. Shape to the desired shape. Popular shapes:
    1. Basket
    2. Weaving
    3. Leaf shape
    4. Ring and ball shape.
  8. Egg wash and allow to rest for 10 minutes. Bake at 160-180°C depending on shape and size for long enough time to get crispy (30-40 minutes).

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Prover set between 27–35°C (80.6–95°F); Bake at 160–180°C (320–356°F) depending on shape and size for 30–40 minutes.
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Soft, elastic dough before adding salt; baked long enough to get crispy.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Cover tightly with plastic wrap during resting; cool properly after baking.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Oven with steam, Proofer, Dough kneader, Mixing bowls, Baking trays, Measuring spoon set, Sieve, Baking paper, Scissors, Bread knife, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Kneading, Proofing, Shaping, Baking.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. For commercial production of bread, the temperatures and proving times mentioned in the recipe must be followed.
      3. The Prover should be set between 27-35 °C.
      4. For home baking, the temperatures mentioned have to be increased by 20°C, since home ovens are slightly weaker than industrial grade.
      5. To prove the dough without a prover, the dough has to rest until it doubles in volume. The timing may vary depending on how cold or warm the bread making environment is.
      6. It is important to cool the bread properly after baking before slicing into it because the internal heat contained within the loaf/roll accounts for part of the baking process.

156. Crunchy Chocolate - Passion Fruit Gateaux

Category: Entremet / Gateaux | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 10–12 mins | Yield: 14 cm round cake / 2375 g

Ingredients

Component Overview: Crunchy chocolate cake is made up of 5 components as under: a) Chocolate Cake, b) Feuilletine Layer, c) Passion Fruit Cremeux, d) Chocolate Cream Frosting, e) Dark Chocolate Mirror Glaze, f) Assembling and Finishing.

A. Chocolate Cake (14cm x 3 rings)

  • 48 g Hot water (handwritten annotation: 480)
  • 20 g Cocoa powder (handwritten annotation: 200)
  • 63 g All-purpose flour (handwritten annotation: 630)
  • 2.5 g / ½ tsp Bicarbonate of soda (handwritten annotation: 25)
  • A pinch Salt (handwritten annotation: +-)
  • 113 g Sugar (handwritten annotation: 1130)
  • 25 g Egg, whole (handwritten annotation: 250)
  • 1 g / ¼ tsp Vanilla (handwritten annotation: 50 [crossed out/written over])
  • 65 g Vegetable oil (handwritten annotation: 650)
  • 63 ml Buttermilk (handwritten annotation: 630)

B. Feuilletine Layer (10 cm ring)

  • 10 g Milk chocolate (handwritten annotation: 100)
  • 32 g Praline paste (handwritten annotation: 320)
  • 6 g Butter (handwritten annotation: 60)
  • 16 g Feuilletine (handwritten annotation: 160)

C. Passion Fruit Cremeux (10 cm ring) (handwritten margin note: 10x)

  • ½ pc. Gelatine leaves, bronze (handwritten annotation: 20 pc)
  • 18 g / 1 pcs. Egg yolk (handwritten annotation: 180)
  • 3 g Sugar (handwritten annotation: 30)
  • 18 g Passion fruit puree (handwritten annotation: 180)
  • 37 g Cream (handwritten annotation: 370)
  • 18 g White chocolate (handwritten annotation: 180)

D) Chocolate Cream Frosting

  • 125 g Dark chocolate 70%
  • 125 g Cream I.
  • 125 g Cream II.
  • 15 g Icing sugar

E) Dark Chocolate Mirror Glaze

  • 6.6 g / 2 pcs. Gelatine leaves
  • 70 g Water
  • 140 g Sugar
  • 40 g Whipping cream
  • 40 g Cocoa powder
  • 20 g Dark chocolate 55%

F) Assembling and Finishing

  • 100 g Dark compound chocolate
  • 50 g Mix berries
  • As required Chocolate garnishes, (Macaroon shells)

Method

A. Chocolate Cake

  1. Preheat the oven to 160° C.
  2. Boil the water and pour over the cocoa powder. Mix using a hand whisk and cool.
  3. Sift together flour and bicarbonate of soda, and then add salt and sugar.
  4. Mix cooled cocoa liquid, eggs, vanilla, vegetable oil, and buttermilk together.
  5. Add liquid to the dry ingredients and mix just until smooth. Do not over mix.
  6. Equally divide cake batter into 3 x 14 cm diameter lined cake pans and spread evenly.
  7. Bake in a preheated oven for 10-12 minutes or until done.
  8. Allow to remain in the pan for 5 minutes, then turnout and cool on a cooling rack.

B. Feuilletine Layer

  1. Melt chocolate, and add praline paste and butter to it.
  2. Fold in feuilletine.
  3. Spread it to a 4-5 mm thick round shaped disc, using a 10 cm diameter cake ring.
  4. Set it in the freezer.

C. Passion Fruit Cremeux

  1. Soak gelatine leaves.
  2. Whisk egg yolk and sugar in a bowl.
  3. Heat the passion fruit puree and cream in a separate saucepan and pour onto the egg yolk and sugar mixture.
  4. Pour the mixture back into the saucepan and cook to 82° C, stirring continuously.
  5. Strain the anglaise onto the white chocolate and mix.
  6. Add in the gelatin and pour into the 10cm ring and freeze.

D) Chocolate Cream Frosting

  1. Boil cream I. and pour over the chocolate.
  2. Mix well to make a ganache and set aside.
  3. Whip cream II. with icing sugar to firm peak.
  4. Fold whipped cream in to the cooled ganache in 2 additions and refrigerate to chill.

E) Dark Chocolate Mirror Glaze

  1. Soak gelatine leaves in ice cold water and allow it to bloom. Drain when softened.
  2. Boil water, cream, sugar and cocoa powder together.
  3. Take off the heat and add chocolate. Mix until it dissolves.
  4. Squeeze out excess water from the soaked gelatine leaves and add it to the mixture once it reaches a temperature of less than 60°C.
  5. Strain into a container and store in the chiller with contact clingfilm till required.

F) Assembling and Finishing

  1. Melt dark compound chocolate and spread a thin layer of melted compound on bottom of one cake layer, and after setting place the cake upside down on a cake board to avoid sticking.
  2. Spread a thin layer of frosting, place the feuilletine disc in the center, and pipe the frosting around to the height of the disc.
  3. Cover with the second layer of cake, spread a thin layer of frosting, place the passion fruit crèmeux disc in the center, and pipe the frosting around to the height of the disc.
  4. Cover with the third layer of cake and neatly finish the top and sides with an even layer of frosting.
  5. Allow to set in the refrigerator or freezer.
  6. Place the cake on a pastry wire grid and pour the glaze over the cake.
  7. Spread fast and using a long palette knife slice off from the wire grid, transfer to the presentation board, and refrigerate to set.

Decorating

  1. Side: Place dark chocolate disc (or any other chocolate shape, macaroon shell) on the side equi-distance from each other.
  2. Top: Chocolate garnishes, berries, sugar flowers.
  3. Refrigerate until served.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Bake Chocolate Cake at 160°C (320°F) for 10–12 minutes; Cook Passion Fruit Cremeux anglaise to 82°C (179.6°F); Add gelatine to Mirror Glaze below 60°C (140°F).
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Cake batter mixed just until smooth; Feuilletine spread to a 4–5 mm thick disc; Cream II whipped to firm peak; Mirror glaze smooth and glossy.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Store mirror glaze in chiller with contact clingfilm until required; refrigerate finished gateau until served.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Measuring jug, Sieve, Mixing bowls, Rubber spatula, Strainer, Sauce pan, Digital thermometer, Palette knife, Electric hand mixer, Hand whisk, Spoons, Piping bag, Turn table, Baking tins round, 3 x 20 cm diameter, Cake rings 2 x 16 cm, Cake board, Aluminum foil, Cling film, Baking paper, Scissors, Oven mitts/ gloves, Cooling rack, Turn table, Paring knife/ bamboo skewer.
    • Key Learning: Boiling, All in method, Baking, Folding, Whipping, Frosting, Assembling of the cake, Glazing, Finishing and decorating.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. The passion fruit puree can be substituted for any other fruit flavor.
      3. Crushed cornflakes or rice crispies can be used if feuilletine is not available.
      4. Other nut pastes such as pistachio paste, peanut butter, nutella can be used instead of praline paste for variation in flavor.

157. Honey Cake

Category: Gateaux / Layered Cake | Prep: 15–20 mins dough rest + 24 hrs refrigeration before serving | Cook/Bake: 4–5 mins | Yield: 1 x 16 cm dia. X 10 cm height / 800 g

Ingredients

Component Overview: Honey cake is made up of 2 components as under: a) Honey Dough Sheets, b) Honey Cream Frosting, c) Assembling and Finishing.

A. Honey Dough Layers

  • 42 g Honey
  • 60 g Caster sugar
  • 15 g Molasses
  • 15 g Butter
  • 75 g Egg, whole
  • 200 g All-purpose flour
  • 2.5 g / ½ tsp Bicarbonate of soda

B. Honey Cream Frosting

  • 350 g Whipping cream
  • 50 g Honey
  • 20 g Caster Sugar

C. Assembling and Finishing

  • As required Crumbs made with baked trimmings
  • As required Chocolate garnishes
  • As required Mix Berries

Method

A. Honey Dough Layers

  1. Preheat the oven to 160° C.
  2. Melt honey, caster sugar, molasses, and butter on a bain marie until the sugar dissolves.
  3. Add in the baking powder and allow it to activate and form small bubbles in the sugar mixture.
  4. Take the pan off the heat and slowly incorporate the eggs and mix after each addition, until well combined.
  5. Take the bowl off the saucepan, sift the flour and add it to the mixture. Mix until it forms a sticky dough.
  6. If need be, place the dough directly onto a clean work surface and knead until it forms a dough.
  7. Allow the dough to rest in the refrigerator for 15-20 minutes.
  8. Divide the dough equally into 8 pieces of 50 grams each.
  9. Sheet them to 1-2 mm thin round sheets of 16 cm diameter. Use a 16 cm ring to cut into precise circles.
  10. Place the sheets on lined baking trays and dock.
  11. Place the trimmings on another lined tray and dock.
  12. Bake in the preheated oven for 4-5 minutes or until light golden brown.

B. Honey Cream Frosting

  1. Whisk whipping cream, honey and icing sugar to a medium peak stage.
  2. Refrigerate until used.

C. Assembling and Finishing

  1. Place one honey biscuit layer on the base and spread 45 g of honey cream frosting evenly to cover the entire surface.
  2. Repeat with all the 8 layers of biscuit.
  3. Finish the cake by evenly spreading the remaining frosting on the top and side, and set in the freezer.
  4. Blitz the left over biscuit in the food processor.
  5. Cover the cake with the crumbs evenly and refrigerate for 24 hrs before serving.
  6. Decorate with chocolate garnishes and mix berries as desired.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Bake at 160°C (320°F) for 4–5 minutes.
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Sugar mixture forms small bubbles when leavening activates; dough sheets rolled 1–2 mm thin and baked until light golden brown; frosting whisked to medium peak stage; cake rested 24 hours in refrigerator for correct texture.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Refrigerate frosting until used; set frosted cake in freezer, coat with crumbs, and refrigerate for 24 hours before serving.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Sieve, Mixing bowls, Sauce pan, Rubber spatula, Rolling pin, Pastry docker or fork, Cake rings 16 cm, Electric hand mixer, Palette knife, Food processer, Paring knife, Cake board, Turn table, Baking tray, Baking paper, Scissors, Cling film, Oven mitts/gloves, Turn table, Paring knife/ bamboo skewer.
    • Key Learning: Melting method, Sifting, Rolling, Docking, Baking, Blitzing, Whipping, Assembling, Finishing.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. The flavor of the honey is very important to the outcome of the cake.
      3. This variation of honey cake is layered with a honey cream frosting. The traditional filling uses sour cream and honey and variations include buttercream frosting made with dulce de leche, custard etc.
      4. Honey cake has to rest in refrigerator for 24 hrs to have the correct texture.
↑ Back to top

158. Madeira Cream Cheese Gateaux

Category: Gateaux / Layered Cake | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 12–15 mins | Yield: 16 cm dia. X 10 cm height / 8 – 10 portions / 1300 g

Ingredients

Component Overview: Madeira Cream Cheese Frosting Cake is made up of 3 components as under: A) Orange Marmalade, B) Orange Pound Cake/Madeira Cake, C) Vanilla Cream Frosting, D) Assembling and Finishing.

A. Orange Marmalade

  • 15 g (2 oranges) Orange (segments)
  • 5 g Orange zest
  • 100 g Sugar I.
  • 1 g / ¼ tsp Pectin jaune
  • 20 g Sugar II.

B. Madeira Cake

  • 115 g Butter
  • 115 g Caster sugar
  • 5 g Orange zest
  • 4 g / 1 tsp Vanilla
  • 100 g / 2 pcs. Egg, whole
  • 115 g All-purpose flour
  • 5 g / 1.25 tsp Baking powder
  • 15 g Almond powder
  • 40 ml Orange juice

C. Vanilla Cream Frosting

  • 288 g Milk
  • 1 tsp Vanilla
  • 54 g Flour
  • 270 g Butter
  • 150 g Icing Sugar

D. Assembling and Finishing

  • 50 g Candied orange
  • 100 g Almond flakes toasted

Method

A. Orange Marmalade

  1. Cook orange segments and orange zest with sugar I. until the majority of the liquid evaporates.
  2. Mix sugar II. with pectin jaune together (dry).
  3. Pour sugar and pectin mixture in to the hot jam and mix until smooth.
  4. Cook to 106° C using a candy thermometer.
  5. Allow it to cool down before using.

B. Madeira Cake

  1. Preheat the oven to 160° C.
  2. Line 3 x 16 cm diameter cake rings with baking paper and aluminum foil.
  3. Sift together flour and baking powder and then mix in almond flour.
  4. In a large mixing bowl, cream together butter, sugar, and orange zest with an electric mixer, until fluffy.
  5. Add vanilla and beaten egg, little by little and mix until smooth.
  6. Fold in dry ingredients and orange juice, alternate with each other in 2 - 3 additions and mix until just combined.
  7. Measure and divide mixture equally into the three prepared rings.
  8. Bake in the preheated oven, for 12 – 15 minutes or until done.
  9. Allow the cakes to cool in the ring for 5 minutes and then transfer to cooling rack to cool.

C. Vanilla Cream Frosting

  1. Mix the milk, vanilla and flour in a saucepan & cook till thick.
  2. In a separate bowl, cream together butter and sieved icing sugar until fluffy.
  3. Cool down the above paste and slowly incorporate it into the creamed butter mixture and mix only until just combined. Do not over mix, as it can curdle.

D. Assembling and Finishing

  1. To assemble, place one layer of cake on a cake board, spread half the marmalade evenly and top with a thin layer of cheese frosting.
  2. Repeat with the second layer and place the last layer of cake on top.
  3. Cover the cake evenly with a thin layer of frosting.
  4. Fill the remaining frosting in a piping bag and finish the cake.
  5. Decorate with candied orange slices and toasted sliced almond as required.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Cook Orange Marmalade to 106°C (222.8°F); Bake Madeira Cake at 160°C (320°F) for 12–15 minutes.
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Marmalade cooked until majority of liquid evaporates and reaches 106°C; butter and sugar creamed until fluffy; milk-flour paste cooked until thick and incorporated into creamed butter only until just combined (do not over mix, as it can curdle).
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Cool cakes in rings for 5 minutes before transferring to cooling rack.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Measuring jug, Mixing bowls, Zester / Microplane, Sieve, Citrus juicer, Sauté pan, Rubber spatula, Candy thermometer, Electric hand mixer, Hand whisk, Piping bag – star nozzle, Palette knife, Cake board, Plastic scraper, Cake rings 3 x 16 cm, Baking paper, Aluminum foil, Scissors, Oven mitts/ gloves, Baking tray, Cooling rack, Turn table, Paring knife/ bamboo skewer.
    • Key Learning: Making marmalade, Creaming method, Sieving, Whipping, Baking, Assembling, Piping and decorating.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Bottled orange marmalade can also be used.
↑ Back to top

159. Opera Cake

Category: Entremet / Gateaux | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 8–12 mins | Yield: 27 cm x 15 cm x 6 cm rectangle / 2350 g

Ingredients

Component Overview: Opera Cake is made up of 5 components as under: A) Vanilla Jaconde, B) Coffee Syrup, C) Dark Chocolate Ganache, D) Coffee Butter Cream, E) Mirror Glaze, F) Assembling and Finishing.

A. Vanilla Jaconde

  • 93.5 g Icing sugar
  • 93.5 g Almond powder
  • 22.5 g All-purpose flour
  • 112.5 g Egg, whole
  • 118 g Egg white
  • 37.5 g Sugar
  • 18.75 g Butter, melted

B. Coffee Syrup

  • With Espresso:
    • 125 g Espresso coffee
    • 15 g Sugar
  • With Instant Coffee Powder:
    • 120 g Water
    • 5 g Instant Coffee Powder
    • 15 g Sugar

C. Dark Chocolate Ganache

  • 75 g Dark chocolate 55%
  • 75 g Cream

D. Coffee Butter Cream

  • 50 g Sugar
  • 20 g Water
  • 1.5 tsp Glucose syrup
  • 25 g Egg, whole
  • 10 g Egg white
  • A pinch Salt
  • 1 g / ¼ tsp Vanilla
  • 114 g Butter (room temperature/ 21° C)
  • 1.5 tsp Coffee paste/Trablit

E. Mirror Glaze

  • 1 pc. / 3.3 g Gelatine leaf, bronze
  • 35 g Water
  • 70 g Sugar
  • 20 ml Cream
  • 20 g Cocoa powder
  • 10 g Dark chocolate 55 %

F. Assembling and Finishing

  • 40 g Melted dark chocolate
  • 1 sheet Gold leaf

Method

A. Vanilla Jaconde

  1. Preheat the oven to 160° C.
  2. Sift icing sugar, flour and whisk in the almond powder.
  3. Add the whole eggs and foam it until fluffy.
  4. Make a French meringue with the egg whites and sugar.
  5. Add meringue to the almond paste in 2 additions and fold it together.
  6. Temper the butter with little batter and fold it into the jaconde mixture.
  7. Spread Jaconde mixture onto the lined baking sheets 50 cm x 30 cm.
  8. Bake in the preheated oven for 8-12 minutes.
  9. After baking and cooling, cut the sheet into 6 rectangles (14cm x 16cm).

B. Coffee Syrup

  1. In a saucepan, bring coffee and sugar to a boil and allow to cool down.

C. Dark Chocolate Ganache

  1. Place the dark chocolate in a mixing bowl.
  2. Heat cream to 85° C and pour over the dark chocolate; mix to form a ganache.
  3. Cover with cling wrap and refrigerate until used.
  4. Each layer should have 20g of ganache.

D. Coffee Butter Cream

  1. Cook sugar, water, and glucose syrup to 118° C.
  2. Simultaneously foam egg, egg white, and salt; when the syrup has reached the right temperature, pour it in a thin stream, continuously whisking, similar to an Italian meringue.
  3. Continue to whisk, until the temperature comes down to lukewarm.
  4. Incorporate room temperature butter little by little. Mix until smooth.
  5. Add coffee paste to get a light coffee color and flavor.
  6. Each layer should have 30 g of coffee buttercream.

E. Mirror Glaze

  1. Soak gelatine leaf in ice cold water and allow it to bloom.
  2. Place all the ingredients together in sauce pan except gelatine and bring to a boil.
  3. Take off the heat, squeeze out the excess water from the soaked gelatine and mix it in once the mixture is less than 60 °C and strain.
  4. Allow to cool down.

F. Assembling and Finishing

  1. Place one sheet of jaconde on the cake board and brush the surface liberally and uniformly with coffee syrup, using a pastry brush.
  2. Spread 20 g of ganache on it, freeze until set firm.
  3. Spread 30 g of coffee butter cream on the ganache and cover with the second sheet of jaconde.
  4. Repeat the same process, with the remaining 5 layers.
  5. After finishing the uppermost layer, cover with baking paper and place a baking sheet on it, pressing very gently, to get an even flat surface and freeze until hard.
  6. To finish, peel off baking paper, spread mirror glaze on the surface in an even thin layer and freeze again until firm.
  7. Trim the sides with a sharp knife, using boiling hot water and a clean towel to keep the blade hot and clean, this helps get a clean edge when cut.
  8. Decorate with piped melted chocolate and gold leaf.
  9. Keep refrigerated until served.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Bake Vanilla Jaconde at 160°C (320°F) for 8–12 minutes; Heat cream for Ganache to 85°C (185°F); Cook sugar syrup for Coffee Butter Cream to 118°C (244.4°F); Butter at room temperature 21°C (69.8°F); Add gelatine to Mirror Glaze below 60°C (140°F).
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Whole eggs foamed until fluffy; smooth buttercream with light coffee color; even flat surface pressed gently before glazing; clean cut edges revealing distinct 20 g ganache and 30 g coffee buttercream layers.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Cover ganache with cling wrap and refrigerate until used; freeze between layers until firm; keep finished Opera Cake refrigerated until served.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Sieve, Planetary mixer with whisk attachment, Electric hand mixer, Hand Whisk, Sauce pan, Rubber spatula, Baking trays, Baking paper, Scissors, Oven mitts/gloves, Palette knife, Chef knife, Digital thermometer, Pastry brush.
    • Key Learning: Whisking, Sieving, Foaming, French meringue, Italian meringue, Folding, Baking, Boiling, Assembling of the cake, Glazing, Cutting.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Opera cake can be decorated with “OPERA” written on the top with melted chocolate. Other chocolate garnishes can be used too.
      3. Using a blast freezer in between the layering speeds up the process.
↑ Back to top

160. Nougat Peach Slice

Category: Gateaux / Patisserie Slice | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 7–8 mins (almonds) + 10–12 mins (sponge) | Yield: 27 cm x 15 cm x 6 cm rectangle / 2350 g (10 slices of 10 cm x 4 cm)

Ingredients

Component Overview: Nougatine Peach Slices is made up of 5 components as under: A) Nougatine, B) Almond Sponge, C) Cream Patisserie, D) Nougatine Cream, E) Peach Jelly, F) Assembling and Finishing.

A. Nougatine

  • 105 g Sliced almond
  • 210 g Sugar
  • 82 g Glucose syrup
  • 55 g Water

B. Almond Sponge

  • 112 g Almond paste 65%
  • 40 g Icing sugar
  • 45 g Egg, whole (cold)
  • 75 g Egg yolk (cold)
  • 34 g Cornstarch
  • 34 g All-purpose flour
  • 100 g Egg white
  • 40 g Caster sugar
  • 34 g Butter

C. Cream Patisserie

  • 112 g Cream
  • 112 g Milk
  • 54 g Sugar
  • 15 g Cornstarch
  • 30 g Egg yolk
  • 1 g / ¼ tsp Vanilla
  • 7 g Butter

D) Nougatine Cream

  • 270 g Cream patisserie
  • 5 g / 1 ½ pcs. Gelatine leaves (bronze)
  • 94 g Crushed nougatine
  • 112 g Whipping cream

E. Peach Jelly

  • 225 g Canned peach/puree
  • 5 g / 1 ½ pcs. Gelatine leaves, bronze
  • 54 g Sugar
  • 13 g Cornstarch
  • 75 g Peach (fresh, diced)

F. Assembling and Finishing

  • 100 g Cream Chantilly

Method

A. Nougatine

  1. Preheat the oven to 150° C.
  2. Toast sliced almond in the oven at 150° C for 7-8 minutes or until light golden brown.
  3. Cook sugar, glucose syrup, and water to caramelize.
  4. Wash around the sides of the saucepan with a clean brush dipped in hot water to remove the splatter.
  5. Mix toasted nuts in to the caramelized sugar and pour it onto a silicone mat; cover it with another mat or with a sheet of baking paper and roll it to 1-2 mm thickness. If gets cold and cannot be rolled any more, put the mat back in the oven (200° C) to heat up and melt the caramel a bit to complete the rolling to the desired thickness.
  6. Using a chef’s knife, cut the nougatine to 10 cm wide strips.
  7. Heat as required and cut the strips into 4 cm wide rectangular pieces. Arrange them on baking paper or silicone mat sheet next to each other, but not touching. Crush the trimmings.
  8. Keep in dry place or air tight container until used.

B. Almond Sponge

  1. Preheat the oven to 160° C.
  2. Place almond paste, icing sugar, cold whole egg, and egg yolk, in a food processor and pulse until smooth.
  3. Pour the marzipan mixture in to a large mixing bowl and whisk with an electric hand mixer until pale in color.
  4. Sift cornstarch and flour together, and fold in to the mixture.
  5. Make a French meringue with the egg white and caster sugar and melt the butter separately.
  6. Fold in the meringue in to the marzipan base in 2 additions and add the melted butter.
  7. Fold until just combined.
  8. Line and seal 3 cake frames 20 cm x 20 cm, with baking paper and aluminum foil, and place on baking trays.
  9. Weigh and equally divide the mixture into the 3 frames (approx. 150 g).
  10. Spread the mixture briefly and evenly and bake in the preheated oven for 10-12 minutes.
  11. Allow them to cool down in the frame before cutting them out.

C) Cream Patisserie

  1. Mix the milk and cream together and bring the ¾ of the mixture to a boil in a saucepan.
  2. To a mixing bowl add sugar, cornstarch, vanilla, egg yolk, and 1/4 of cold milk mixture and mix until combined.
  3. Pour the hot mixture on the egg yolk- sugar mixture, whisking constantly.
  4. Pour it back in the sauce pan and bring it to boil, while continuously mixing.
  5. Cook till it bubbles right in the center and pour into a clean mixing bowl.
  6. Keep whisking till it’s lukewarm and then mix in butter.

D. Nougatine Cream

  1. Soak gelatine leaves in ice cold water.
  2. To the warm cream patisserie and add the drained gelatine leaves and mix well.
  3. In a clean mixing bowl, whip cream to a medium peak.
  4. Add the crushed nougatine.
  5. Fold in the whipped cream into the cream patisserie, in 2 additions.
  6. Refrigerate until used.

E. Peach Jelly (To be made just before assembling)

  1. Drain and blend canned peaches to puree.
  2. Soak gelatine leaves in ice cold water.
  3. Boil the peach puree. Mix the sugar and cornstarch first, then add it to the puree; bring to a boil, till it starts boiling from the middle.
  4. Take off the heat; add soaked and drained gelatine leaves and diced peaches.
  5. Use immediately for assembly.

F. Assembling and Finishing

  1. Line and seal a 20 cm x 20 cm frame with cling film and aluminum foil and place it on a baking tray.
  2. Place 1 sheet of sponge inside the frame evenly spread 350 g of nougatine cream on top.
  3. Place the second layer of sponge.
  4. Mix the hot jelly until smooth and pour it on the sponge. Spread evenly.
  5. Cover jelly with the last layer of sponge and spread the rest of the cream on it.
  6. Freeze until set firm.
  7. Cut the cake out of the frame and slice it to 10 slices (10 cm x 4 cm).
  8. Place a nougatine plaque on the top of each piece and decorate with piped cream Chantilly.
  9. Keep refrigerate until served.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Toast almonds at 150°C (302°F) for 7–8 minutes; Reheat nougatine at 200°C (392°F) if needed to roll; Bake Almond Sponge at 160°C (320°F) for 10–12 minutes.
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Almonds toasted until light golden brown; nougatine rolled to 1–2 mm thickness; marzipan mixture whisked until pale in color; cream patisserie cooked until bubbling right in the center; whipping cream whipped to medium peak.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Keep nougatine in a dry place or airtight container until used; refrigerate nougatine cream until used; keep finished slices refrigerated until served.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring jug, Measuring spoon set, Sauce pan, Silicone mat, Mixing bowls, Food processor, Baking tray, White chopping board, Chef knife, Pastry brush, Sieve, Rubber spatula, Electric hand mixer, Paring knife, Baking paper, Aluminum foil, Scissors, Hand Whisk, Palette knife, Baking frame 3 x 20 cm x 20 cm, Strainer, Blender, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Toasting, Caramelizing, Rolling, Making nougatine, Pulsing, Sifting, Whisking, Dicing, Foaming, French meringue, Melting, Whipping, Folding, Baking, Boiling, Tempering, Assembling of the cake, Cutting.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. To make cherry or apricot nougatine slice, replace the fruit puree in the jelly recipe.
↑ Back to top

161. Fan Gateaux (Legezo)

Category: Gateaux / Entremet | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 8 mins (Chocolate Sponge) + 8–10 mins (Vanilla Jaconde) | Yield: 1 x 18 cm dia. x 10 cm height/ 1300 g

Ingredients

Component Overview: Fan Gateaux is made up of 4 components as under: A) Chocolate Sponge, B) Vanilla Joconde, C) Chocolate Cream Frosting, D) Cream Chantilly.

A. Chocolate Sponge

  • 40 g All-purpose flour
  • 10 g Cocoa powder
  • 100 g / 2 pcs. Egg, whole
  • 1 g / ¼ tsp Vanilla
  • 50 g Sugar

B. Vanilla Jaconde

  • 93.5 g Icing sugar
  • 22.5 g All-purpose flour
  • 93.5 g Almond powder
  • 112.5 g Egg, whole
  • 1 g / ¼ tsp Vanilla
  • 118 g Egg white
  • 37.5 g Sugar
  • 19 g Butter (melted)

C. Chocolate Cream Frosting

  • 250 g Dark chocolate, 70%
  • 250 g Cream I.
  • 250 g Cream II.
  • 30 g Icing sugar

D. Cream Chantilly

  • 100 g Whipping cream
  • 10 g Icing sugar
  • 1 g / ¼ tsp Vanilla

Method

A. Chocolate Sponge

  1. Preheat the oven to 160° C.
  2. Sift flour and cocoa powder together.
  3. Whisk egg and vanilla, till light and fluffy.
  4. Add sugar little and a time, and continue whisking until thick and the mixture falls from the spoon in the form of a ribbon.
  5. Fold in the flour mixture in two additions.
  6. Divide and spread the mixture in two 18 cm rings.
  7. Bake in the preheated oven for 8 minutes or until the sponge springs back when touched.
  8. Cut out from the frame and allow to cool down on a cooling rack.

B) Vanilla Jaconde

  1. Preheat the oven to 160° C.
  2. Sift icing sugar, flour and whisk in the almond powder.
  3. Add the whole eggs and vanilla, and foam it until fluffy.
  4. Make a French meringue with the egg white and sugar.
  5. Fold in meringue to the paste in 2 additions until just combined.
  6. Temper the butter with little batter and fold it into the joconde mixture.
  7. Spread Joconde mixture to 5 mm thickness, on lined baking sheets 50 cm x 30 cm.
  8. Bake at 160°C for 8-10 minutes or until done.
  9. Cool on the tray.

C) Chocolate Cream Frosting

  1. Place chocolate in a mixing bowl.
  2. Bring cream I. to a boil and pour over the chocolate.
  3. Mix until well combined to form a ganache.
  4. Whip cream II. with icing sugar.
  5. Fold the whipped cream into the cooled ganache in 2 additions and chill.

D. Cream Chantilly

  1. Place whipping cream, icing sugar, and vanilla in a mixing bowl and whisk together to medium peak.

E) Assembling and Finishing

  1. Spread a thin and even layer of chocolate frosting on top of the vanilla jaconde sheets and cut into even 30 mm - 35 mm wide strips.
  2. Start to roll the first strip to form a pin wheel (as in Swiss roll) and continue joining the consecutive strips and keep rolling until all the strips are used up and the diameter of the cylinder is the same as the chocolate cake base.
  3. Place one layer of chocolate sponge on a cake board and spread a layer of frosting on top of the chocolate sponge.
  4. Place the white jaconde pinwheel cylinder on the chocolate base.
  5. Spread a layer of cream on top and cover it with the second chocolate sponge.
  6. Cover the sides and top of the cake with frosting and allow it to set in the refrigerator.
  7. Fill cream Chantilly and the left over chocolate cream frosting to 2 separate piping bags set with the 10 mm plain piping nozzles.
  8. Starting from one side of the top, pipe alternate straight lines of Chantilly and chocolate frosting neatly next to each other to cover the entire surface.
  9. Using a wooden skewer or toothpick, feather the surface by drawing through the frosting perpendicular to the long lines, in opposite directions, 1 cm apart.
  10. Pipe the top edge of the cake to finish.
  11. Refrigerator until served.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Bake Chocolate Sponge at 160°C (320°F) for 8 minutes; Bake Vanilla Jaconde at 160°C (320°F) for 8–10 minutes.
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Egg and sugar mixture whisked to ribbon stage; chocolate sponge springs back when touched; Chantilly whisked to medium peak; feathered top with perpendicular lines 1 cm apart.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Chill chocolate cream frosting; set assembled cake in refrigerator and keep refrigerated until served.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Rubber spatula, Saucepan, Hand whisk, Baking paper, Chef’s knife, Sieve, Electric hand mixer, Baking trays, Palette knife, Piping bag, Piping nozzle, 10 mm, plain, Cake board, Oven mitts/gloves, Cake ring 18cm, Aluminum foil, Turn table.
    • Key Learning: Foaming method, Baking, Sifting, Folding, Making ganache, Whipping, Assembling, Piping.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
↑ Back to top

162. Trio Chocolate Charlotte

Category: Entremet / Charlotte | Prep: 10 mins rest before baking + 15 mins blast freeze | Cook/Bake: 12 mins | Yield: 1 x 16 cm dia. x 10 cm height cake/ 800 g

Ingredients

Component Overview: Trio -Chocolate Charlotte is made up of 2 components as under: A) Bisquit A La Cuillere/Lady Fingers, B) Trio Chocolate Bavarois Filling, C) Assembling and Finishing.

A. Biscuit a la Cuillere/Lady Finger Biscuits

  • 36 g / 2 pcs. Egg yolk
  • 20 g Sugar I.
  • 60 g / 2 pcs. Egg white
  • 40 g Sugar II.
  • 60 g All-purpose flour

B. Trio Chocolate Bavarois Filling

  • 3.3 g / 1 pc. Gelatine leaves bronze
  • 130 g Milk
  • 20 g / 1 pc. Egg yolk
  • 30 g Caster sugar
  • 100 g Whipping cream
  • 50 g Dark chocolate
  • 60 g Milk chocolate
  • 75 g White chocolate

C. Garnish

  • 3-5 pcs. Lady fingers small
  • As required Dark chocolate shavings
  • As required Assorted berries
  • 70 g Cream Chantilly

Method

A. Biscuit a la Cuillere/Lady Finger Biscuits

  1. Preheat oven to 160° C.
  2. Sift flour and set aside.
  3. Whisk egg yolk and sugar I. until light and fluffy.
  4. Make a French meringue with the egg white and sugar II.
  5. Fold in the meringue to the yolk mixture in 2 additions.
  6. Fold in the flour until just combined. DO NOT OVER MIX.
  7. Fill the mixture to a piping bag fitted with a 10 mm nozzle.
  8. Pipe out one disc of 15 cm diameter to make the base of the cake.
  9. Pipe the remaining mixture into at least 10-12 large lady finger biscuits, using a mold.
  10. Dust with icing sugar and set on the side for 10 minutes.
  11. Bake for 12 minutes.
  12. Allow them to cool down in the mold.

B. Trio Chocolate Bavarois Filling

  1. Soak gelatine leaves in ice cold water and allow it to bloom.
  2. Place milk in a sauce pan and bring to a boil.
  3. Whisk egg yolk and sugar in a mixing bowl until fluffy.
  4. Add milk to egg yolk mixture whisking continuously.
  5. Pour it back in the saucepan and cook up to 84° C, stirring continuously.
  6. Take it off the heat and continue stirring for 1-2 minutes.
  7. Add drained gelatine leaves and mix until dissolved.
  8. Allow it to cool down.
  9. Whip the cream to medium peak and refrigerate.
  10. Melt 3 kinds of chocolate separately.
  11. For the dark chocolate bavarois: Mix melted dark chocolate with 55 g of the cream anglaise and fold in 30 g of the whipped cream.
  12. For the milk chocolate bavarois: Mix melted milk chocolate with 50 g of the cream anglaise and fold in 30 g of the whipped cream.
  13. For the white chocolate bavarois as the third layer: Mix melted white chocolate with 35 g of the cream anglaise and fold in 30 g of the whipped cream.

C. Assembling and Finishing

  1. Cut lady finger disc with a 12 cm plain round cutter to get a neat edge.
  2. Line a 16 cm cake ring with 50 or 60 mm plastic (acetate) ribbon.
  3. Place the ring on a sheet of baking paper or silicone mat.
  4. Place the cut disc in the middle of the ring.
  5. De mold the lady finger biscuits and cut them half crosswise (neatly).
  6. Place each half standing up next to each other, around the inside of the ring, with the rounded side on top, pressing then in between the ring and the disc.
  7. Pipe the dark chocolate bavarois evenly on top of the disc and place in the blast freezer to set.
  8. Then pipe the milk chocolate bavarois on top and place in the blast freezer to set.
  9. Then pipe the white chocolate bavarois on top and place in the blast freezer to set.
  10. Transfer the cake to the refrigerator after 15 minutes.
  11. Decorate with mini lady finger biscuits, piped cream Chantilly, dark chocolate shavings, and assorted berries.
  12. Refrigerate until served.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Bake Lady Finger Biscuits at 160°C (320°F) for 12 minutes; Cook Bavarois cream anglaise to 84°C (183.2°F).
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Yolk mixture light and fluffy; flour folded until just combined (do not over mix); cream whipped to medium peak; three distinct set layers of dark, milk, and white chocolate bavarois encircled by halved ladyfingers.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Set each bavarois layer in blast freezer, transfer to refrigerator after 15 minutes, and keep refrigerated until served.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Mixing bowls, Rubber spatula, Saucepan, Hand whisk, Baking paper, Chef’s knife, Sieve, Electric hand mixer, Baking trays, Palette knife, Piping bag, Piping nozzle, 10 mm, plain, Cake board, Oven mitts/gloves, Cake ring 18cm, Aluminum foil, Turn table.
    • Key Learning: Foaming method, Baking, Sifting, Folding, Making ganache, Whipping, Assembling, Piping.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. Measurement of dark chocolate bavarois: Cream Anglaise 55 g, Dark chocolate 50 g, Whipped cream 30 g.
      3. Measurement of milk chocolate bavarois: Cream Anglaise 50 g, Milk chocolate 60 g, Whipped cream 30 g.
      4. Measurement of white chocolate bavarois: Cream Anglaise 35 g, White chocolate 75 g, Whipped cream 30 g.

163. Roasted Apple and Coffee Success

Category: Plated Dessert / French Patisserie | Prep: At least 1 hr ganache resting | Cook/Bake: 10 mins (Success Biscuit) + 7 mins (Roasted Apple Segments) | Yield: 1 x 16 cm dia. X 10 cm height / 800 g

Ingredients

Success Biscuit

  • 35 g Egg white
  • 35 g Sugar
  • 10.5 g All-purpose flour
  • 17.5 g Almond powder
  • 17.5 g Hazelnut powder

White Chocolate Coffee Ganache

  • 60 g White chocolate
  • 50 g Cream
  • 3 g / ½ tsp Instant coffee powder

Roasted Apple Segments

  • 1 pc. Green apple
  • 30 ml Butter, melted
  • 20 g Caster sugar
  • 1.5 g / ½ tsp Cinnamon powder

Garnish

  • As required Chocolate
  • As required Roasted hazelnut

Method

Success Biscuit

  1. Make a French meringue with egg white and sugar.
  2. Sift flour and mix together with the nut powders.
  3. Fold dry ingredients into the meringue; place the mixture into a piping bag with a 10 mm nozzle.
  4. Pipe swirls to make 4 discs of 70 mm diameter.
  5. Bake in a preheated oven at 160° C for 10 minutes.
  6. Cut them with a 70 mm plain round cutter to get a sharp edge.

White Chocolate Coffee Ganache

  1. Place the chocolate in a bowl.
  2. Heat cream with coffee to 85° C, and pour it on the chocolate.
  3. Mix to make a ganache and allow it to cool in the refrigerator.
  4. After at least 1 hour of resting or the temperature is between 1 – 5° C, whip it to get a soft peak consistency.
  5. Fill into a piping bag with a 10 mm nozzle.

Roasted Apple Segments

  1. Peel and core the apple and cut to 16 segments.
  2. Place the segments on a silicone mat and brush with melted butter.
  3. Sprinkle generously with caster sugar and cinnamon powder.
  4. Cook segments in a preheated oven at 140° C for 7 minutes.
  5. Allow to cool on the mat.

Assembling

  1. Place one success disc on the presentation plate.
  2. Pipe ganache in the middle of the disc to 2.5 – 3 cm height, leaving 1 cm along the edge.
  3. Arrange 8 pieces of apple segment on the outer edge, spirally around the ganache.
  4. Fill the cavity with more ganache as required to fill in the gaps and pipe a thin ring on the top of the segments.
  5. Stick the second disc on it.

Garnish

  1. Garnish with chocolate and chopped hazelnuts.
  2. Refrigerate until served.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Bake Success Biscuit at 160°C (320°F) for 10 minutes; Heat cream for Ganache to 85°C (185°F) and chill to 1–5°C (33.8–41°F) before whipping; Roast Apple Segments at 140°C (284°F) for 7 minutes.
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Success discs cut with 70 mm cutter for a sharp edge; chilled ganache whipped to soft peak consistency.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Rest ganache in refrigerator for at least 1 hour (1–5°C); refrigerate finished dessert until served.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring jug, Measuring spoon set, Sieve, Mixing bowls, Sauce pan, Hand mixer, Rubber spatula, Piping bags, Piping nozzle, 7 mm plain, Peeler, Corer, Paring knife, White chopping board, Chef knife, Silicone mat, Baking tray, Baking paper, Pastry brush, Oven mitts/gloves, 7 cm rings.
    • Key Learning: Making a French meringue, Sifting, Folding, Piping, Peeling, Coring, Slicing, Baking, Melting, Whipping.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. The flavor or type of the filling could be varied and apples could be replaced with other fruits.
      3. Success is a French dessert made with two layers of daquoise (nut meringue) with a cream or buttercream based filling.
      4. A success varies from a merveilleux, in that a merveilleux is made with two discs of plain meringue filled with whipped cream and dusted with chocolate shavings.

164. Pistachio Gateaux

Category: Gateaux / Entremet | Prep: N/A | Cook/Bake: 10–12 mins | Yield: 1x 16 cm round gateaux

Ingredients

Component Overview: Pistachio Gateaux are made up of 3 components as under: A) Japonaise Sponge, B) Stock Syrup, C) Cream Cheese Frosting, D) Assembling and Finishing.

A. Japonaise Sponge

  • 20 g All-purpose flour
  • 45 g Icing sugar
  • 80 g Pistachio powder
  • 100 g Egg white
  • 25 g Caster sugar

B. Stock Syrup

  • 100 g Water
  • 20 g Sugar

C. Cream Cheese Frosting

  • 100 g Cream cheese
  • 50 g Icing sugar
  • 150 g Cream

D. Garnish

  • 50 g Pistachio nibs

Method

A. Japonaise Sponge

  1. Preheat oven to 160° C.
  2. Line 2 x 16 cm cake rings with baking paper and silver foil.
  3. Sieve icing sugar and flour together and mix in the pistachio powder.
  4. Make a French meringue with the egg white and caster sugar.
  5. Fold dry ingredients into the French meringue.
  6. Distribute cake mixture equally in to the cake rings and spread evenly.
  7. Bake at 160° C for 10-12 minutes.
  8. Allow to cool and remove the rings.

B. Stock Syrup

  1. Boil sugar and water together and allow it to cool down.

C. Cream Cheese Frosting

  1. Beat the cheese until just smooth. Do not over beat or it will curdle.
  2. Whip cream with the icing sugar and fold in to the cheese in 2 additions.
  3. Fill and reserve in a piping bag.

D. Assembling and Finishing

  1. Place one layer of sponge on a cake board and soak generously with stock syrup, using a pastry brush.
  2. Spread a layer of cream cheese frosting evenly and place the second layer of cake.
  3. Soak with syrup and cover with the top and sides evenly with a thin layer of frosting.
  4. Fill the remaining frosting into a piping bag with a star nozzle and decorate as required.
  5. Decorate with pistachio nibs to finish.
  6. Refrigerate until served.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Bake Japonaise Sponge at 160°C (320°F) for 10–12 minutes; Boil Stock Syrup (100°C / 212°F).
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Cream cheese beaten until just smooth (do not over beat or it will curdle).
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Reserve frosting in piping bag; refrigerate finished gateau until served.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Mixing bowls, Baking ring, Baking paper, Aluminum foil, Scissors, Electric hand mixer, Sieve, Rubber spatula, Piping bag, Piping nozzle, star, Sauce pan, Cake board, Turntable, Pastry Brush, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Foaming method, Sifting, Folding, Baking, Whipping, Boiling, Assembling.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.

165. Plated Lemon Posset

Category: Plated Dessert | Prep: 10–15 mins blast freeze + at least 1 hr (ideally overnight) chiller | Cook/Bake: 10–12 mins (Bisquit) + 5–6 mins (Crumble) | Yield: 4 pcs

Ingredients

Component Overview: Plated Lemon Posset has 7 components: A) Japonaise Bisquit, B) Lemon Posset, C) Raspberry Jelly Insert, D) Raspberry Caviar, E) Crumble, F) Pistachio Cream, G) Lavender Foam, H) Finishing.

A. Japonaise Bisquit

  • 35 g Egg white
  • 8 g Caster sugar
  • 15 g Icing sugar
  • 6 g Flour
  • 27 g Pistachio powder

B. Lemon Posset

  • 170 g Whipping cream
  • 50 g Caster sugar
  • 16 g Lemon juice
  • 1 pcs Lemon zest
  • 1 pcs Gelatin leaves

C. Raspberry Jelly Insert

  • 65 g Raspberry puree
  • 30 g Caster sugar
  • 10 g Lemon juice
  • 1 pcs Gelatin leaves

D. Raspberry Caviar

  • 60 g Water
  • 90 g Raspberry puree
  • 10 g Lemon juice
  • 20 g Caster sugar
  • 5 g Agar Agar

E. Crumble

  • 15 g Butter
  • 15 g Flour
  • 15 g Light brown sugar
  • 15 g Almond powder

F) Pistachio Cream

  • 60 g White chocolate
  • 25 g Cream I
  • 25 g Cream II
  • 10 g Pistachio paste

G) Lavender Foam

  • 100 ml Milk
  • ½ tsp Lavender
  • ½ tsp Sugar
  • ½ tsp Lecithin

H) Finishing

  • 100 ml Pistachio nibs
  • ½ tsp White chocolate disc
  • ½ tsp Fresh Raspberry

Method

For the Japonaise Bisquit

  1. Make French meringue from the egg white and caster sugar.
  2. Sieve icing sugar and flour, mix together with the pistachio powder.
  3. Fold dry ingredients to the French meringue.
  4. Spread the bisquit mixture to 140 x 140 mm lined frame.
  5. Bake at 160°C for 10-12 minutes.
  6. When baked, dust icing sugar on the top and turn it upside down. Allow to cool down.
  7. When cold, peel off the paper and cut 120 x 20 mm pieces.

For the Lemon Posset

  1. Soak gelatin leaves in ice cold water.
  2. Boil cream, sugar and lemon zest.
  3. Take off the heat and add drained gelatin and lemon juice.
  4. Blend it together with a hand blender.
  5. Put in the blast freezer for 10-15 minutes or until cold, but not frozen.
  6. Transfer to the chiller for at least 1 hour (ideally overnight).

For the Raspberry Jelly Insert

  1. Soak the gelatin in ice cold water.
  2. Boil raspberry puree, sugar and lemon juice together.
  3. Take off the heat and add drained gelatin.
  4. Distribute the mix in “financier” silicon mold (8 pcs) and blast freeze it.
  5. When frozen, pop out on a sheet of baking paper and keep in the storage freezer.

For the Raspberry Caviar

  1. Boil raspberry puree and lemon juice with the sugar.
  2. Blitz agar-agar with kitchen processor to fine and add to the boiling liquid.
  3. Mix and boil it for a few seconds.
  4. Allow it to cool down a bit and transfer the jelly to a squeeze bottle.
  5. Drop the liquid into ice cold vegetable oil. Strain and keep in the fridge in a clean plastic container.

For the Crumble

  1. Combine the butter, flour, light brown sugar and almond powder using the rub in technique till a sandy texture is achieved.
  2. Chill and bake the crumble at 160°C for 5- 6 minutes.

For the Pistachio Cream

  1. Make a white chocolate ganache with cream I, white chocolate and the pistachio paste and cool it down.
  2. Whip cream II and fold in the prepared and cooled ganache.
  3. Rest in the chiller till further use.

For the Lavender Foam

  1. Boil the milk with lavender and cover for 5 minutes to allow the flavour to infuse.
  2. Pour the infused milk into a measuring jug, sprinkle in the sugar and lecithin and use a stick blender to create foam.
  3. Make just before plating for best texture of foam on the plated dessert.

For the Assembly and Finishing

  1. Line cling film on the working table and place a wire rack with the cakes on it.
  2. Spray it from about 30 cm distance from each side for a few seconds, until the cakes get a velvet effect on the surface.
  3. Plate up and decorate with all the components.
  4. Serve immediately.

Chef’s Notes & Technical Parameters

  • Baking / Cooking Temp: Bake Japonaise Bisquit at 160°C (320°F) for 10–12 minutes; Bake Crumble at 160°C (320°F) for 5–6 minutes.
  • Target Texture / Visual Doneness: Posset chilled until cold, not frozen, then set in chiller; crumble rubbed in until sandy texture is achieved; cakes sprayed from 30 cm distance for a velvet effect on the surface; lavender foam made just before plating.
  • Storage / Shelf Life: Lemon posset chilled at least 1 hour (ideally overnight); frozen raspberry jelly inserts kept in storage freezer; strained raspberry caviar kept in fridge in a clean plastic container; serve plated dessert immediately.
  • Additional Notes & Equipment:
    • Equipment: Measuring scale, Measuring spoon set, Zester/Microplane, Lemon squeezer, Mixing bowl, Measuring Jug, Cling film, Squeeze Bottle, Baking paper, Silicon mold, for lemon posset bar, Silicon mold, 5cm x 1cm rectangle, Baking tray, Sauce pan, Hand Whisk, Rubber spatula, Electric Hand mixer, Immersion Blender, Chef knife, White chopping board, Digital thermometer, Offset Spatula, Oven mitts/gloves.
    • Key Learning: Making a Japonaise, Spherification, Making a floral foam, Assembling and presenting the dessert.
    • Notes:
      1. To multiply the recipe, multiply all ingredients proportionately.
      2. The posset was originally a British drink that was served warm. It was essentially curdled milk that was split using wine or ale.
      3. Towards the end of the 16th century, this drink evolved into a citrus based dessert that is still popular to this day.
